## ✅ 2026-07-23: engagement duration → winner-size test — real effect, but too small and doesn't replicate

Direct follow-up to the entry-timing test below, per the user's pushback that win rate isn't the standard of success here — contained losses with occasional large winners is ("eventually the damn will break with a huge win"). Reframed the hypothesis: instead of using engagement duration to GATE entry (already tested, negative), test whether a touch still active (not stopped/targeted) at bar K predicts a BIGGER eventual winner. Bucketing is real-time-computable (knowing stop/target hasn't been hit by bar K needs no lookahead) — this avoids the entry-timing test's methodology entirely, no re-simulation needed, real `actual_pnl` used throughout.

**Audited before trusting**: verified the unconditional baseline directly via raw SQL (avg win $92.87, avg loss -$127.66, max win $599 exactly) against the script's train+test blend ($92.40/$94.66 avg win, -$124.18/-$143.34 avg loss, $599 max win — exact match on max win, close on the rest, consistent with a per-setup_type chronological split vs a flat sample). No correction round needed this time — the first Gemini pass held up.

**Real, nuanced result — not a clean yes or no**: the user's intuition is partially right. Touches still active at bar K DO have a bigger average win size than the unconditional population, and it grows with K (ALL/train: unconditional avg win $92.40 → Active@10 $99.78, roughly +$3 to +$17 across K=3/6/10 and all 3 views). But the ACTIVE bucket's overall EV is deeply negative (ALL/train: unconditional EV=-$0.86 → Active@3=-$5.21 → Active@6=-$9.93 → Active@10=-$12.46) because win rate collapses hard (56.9% → 49.4% at K=10) — the bigger-winner effect is real but nowhere near big enough to offset how much worse the whole bucket performs. The "avg win size advantage" also failed `computeReplication()` at every K value tried (replicates=false for K=3/6/10 — the specific setup_types that looked like winners on train didn't hold on held-out test).

**Practical takeaway**: do not widen targets/trail wider just because a trade has run N bars without resolving — that population is dominated by grinding losers, not quietly-building winners. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `engagement_duration_conditions_winner_size`, promoted to `scripts/backtest_engagement_duration_sizing.mjs` (one-off, not scheduled).

**Open follow-up, raised by the user immediately after this result**: does candlestick SHAPE at/around bar K — not just the raw fact of still being active — distinguish the minority of genuine still-building winners from the toxic majority dragging the bucket negative? Important context before pursuing this: candlestick-shape-based entry confirmation has already been extensively tested elsewhere in this codebase and failed every variant tried (`docs/CANDLE_ORDERFLOW_RESEARCH_SPEC.md`, `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `volume_confirmed_candle_pattern_low_vol_trap` among others). But none of those tests conditioned specifically on "still active at bar K" — this would be testing candle shape as a discriminator WITHIN an already-identified toxic-but-mixed population, not a re-run of a debunked pooled test. Given the track record (candle shape has failed multiple times, entry-timing triggers failed, duration-alone failed replication), the honest expectation going in should be skeptical, not optimistic — but it hasn't been tested in this specific conditioned form. Not yet built.

`node --check`/`eslint` clean on the promoted script.

## ✅ 2026-07-23: engagement/exhaustion entry-TIMING backtest — resolved negative, real bug caught and fixed mid-thread

Resolves the in-progress thread below. Tested whether waiting for the buyer/seller "tussle" to resolve before entering (user's own discretionary framework) beats immediate entry on first touch.

**Gemini's first pass had a real invalidating bug**, caught by reading its actual script rather than trusting the summary: Arm B (meant to be a "blind mechanical delay" control) set its own entry bar to `trigger1Bar`/`trigger2Bar` — literally the same bar the smart trigger (Arm C1/C2) fired on. B and C1 were byte-identical in every row (confirmed directly: same N/WR/EV to the decimal, across all 3 pooled views). The headline conclusion from that pass ("the edge is entirely from entering early/cheap, confirmation adds nothing beyond blind delay") was not supported by any real comparison — there was no actual blind control in that run. Also found: no per-setup_type breakdown (spec required one), Arm A re-simulated PnL via `replayBars` instead of using the already-correct stored `actual_pnl` (silently discarding the TIME_EXPIRED mark-to-market fix), a cross-check query referenced a `signal_name='POOLED_MAE_MFE'` row that doesn't exist in `OPTIMAL_STOP` (confirmed directly — that table is keyed per setup_type, no pooled row), and `computeReplication()` was imported but never called.

**Sent one precise correction** (2nd of the standing 2-attempt budget) referencing exact lines in Gemini's own script. All 5 items came back genuinely fixed — verified by reading the corrected code, not just the summary: Arm B now delays by a fixed per-setup_type bar count (train-median trigger-fire time) independent of whether a trigger fires on that specific touch; a full per-setup_type table was added; Arm A now uses real `actual_pnl`; the OPTIMAL_STOP query was corrected (though it produced NaN from an unfiltered null — a trivial leftover bug, fixed directly by Claude rather than spending a 3rd Gemini round on something that small); constants now import from `backtest_confluence.js` instead of being hand-copied.

**Independently verified before trusting anything**: a direct SQL query against `active_setups` with the exact population filter (N=10911 raw, avg actual_pnl=$0.98, WR=58.3%) matched the corrected script's Arm A almost exactly (10881 after bar-availability skips, $0.95, 58.3%) — strong confirmation the corrected baseline is real, not fabricated. Also checked for day-clustering (422 distinct trading days across the population — broad, not a few sessions dominating).

**Real, corrected finding**: neither tested trigger (C1: delta favorable 2 consecutive bars; C2: C1 + volume above the rolling median) beat both Arm A (immediate entry, ~$0.95-1.53/trade depending on view) and the genuine Arm B blind delay in any of the 3 pooled views (ALL/CONFLUENCE/NON-CONFLUENCE) — both triggers consistently made things worse, from -$1.80 to -$7.51/trade. The 36 (C1) and 35 (C2) setup_types that looked like train-data winners failed `computeReplication()` on held-out test data (`replicates=false` for both — held-out favorable fraction under 50%, selected-pool test EV not meaningfully better than the never-selected pool) — pure train-set overfitting across ~90 setup_types × 2 triggers, not a real generalizable edge.

**Important nuance, not a blanket verdict**: this debunks these two SPECIFIC simple delta-based mechanical proxies, not the underlying idea that engagement quality matters. A different trigger definition (stronger delta magnitude threshold, requiring price to reclaim some fraction of its own adverse excursion before entering, per-setup-type wait windows instead of one pooled window) has not been tested and remains open if there's appetite to try a different formulation.

Promoted to `scripts/backtest_engagement_confirmation_entry.mjs` (reran independently by Claude, reproduced the corrected numbers within noise — N=6954 vs 6950, EV=$0.65 vs $0.62 for NON-CONFLUENCE Arm A, both well within expected variance). Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `engagement_confirmation_entry_timing`, `status='CONFIRMED'` (the negative finding itself is confirmed/reproduced, not provisional). Not scheduled on a recurring cron — this is a one-off test of two specific candidate triggers, not an ongoing calibration; a future session testing a different trigger definition should build a fresh test rather than expecting this script to self-update.

Also produced, as a genuine byproduct answering "what's the typical price/bar movement before the move is made": across all levels, the pivot (worst point before recovery) typically arrives within p25=1/p50=6/p75=27/p90=100 bars of first touch, at p25=12.5/p50=36.75/p75=77/p90=106.75 points of adverse excursion — cross-checked against `OPTIMAL_STOP`'s own average p75_mae (74.90pt) and found consistent, not contradictory.

`node --check` clean on both the Gemini-authored `scratch/engagement_test.mjs` and the promoted `scripts/backtest_engagement_confirmation_entry.mjs`.

~~**IN PROGRESS 2026-07-23: engagement/exhaustion entry-TIMING backtest — dispatched to Gemini, not yet audited or promoted.**~~ **Resolved 2026-07-23 — see the entry above ("engagement/exhaustion entry-TIMING backtest — resolved negative, real bug caught and fixed mid-thread").** First Gemini pass had a real invalidating bug (Arm B's "blind delay" control reused the trigger's own fire bar, making it identical to Arm C1 by construction); corrected, independently re-verified against a direct SQL query, and reproduced by Claude in `scripts/backtest_engagement_confirmation_entry.mjs`. Final result: negative for both tested trigger definitions.

## ✅ 2026-07-23: confluence + volume/delta exhaustion interaction — built, tested, and wired as a monitored (not yet execution-gating) signal

Direct continuation of the 2026-07-22 walk-through of a real failed-short day (price topped at genuine CAM_R1+PD_POC confluence, 5 short attempts near the top, 2 of 5 well-timed by a discretionary volume/delta "exhaustion" read, 3 not). User explicitly authorized building this into a real backtest ("this is how the system should assess for trades... can we backtest it all like this?"), then, after a first-pass leak was found and fixed, asked how to monitor and build off the finding going forward: **"The market isnt just going to give you money. So the tussles between buyers and sellers is where its telling to get in or not. Or look for absorption from a buyer or seller."**

**What was built** (matches CLAUDE.md's "no dead ends" rule — persisted, recheck path, wired-or-flagged consumer, discoverable):
1. `scripts/backtest_confluence_exhaustion_interaction.mjs` — production version of the corrected (`scratch/confluence_exhaustion_combined_v2.mjs`) methodology: chronological 80/20 split PER setup_type FIRST, cutoffs (volRatio/rangeRatio p50) computed from TRAIN only and frozen before evaluating TEST (the leak in the first pass computed cutoffs from the full population before splitting — see the prior session's entry). Persists two things every run: `performance_audit signal_type='EXHAUSTION_SIGNAL_CALIB'` (live-usable cutoffs, full history, no split — this is a classification threshold for future touches, not a backtest result) and `RESEARCH_CLAIM confluence_exhaustion_interaction` (the genuine holdout check, rechecked every run).
2. Scheduled in `scripts/run_confluence_backtest.sh` right after `backtest_confluence.js` (not a separate cron entry) — this script's `hasConfluence` computation depends on that run's fresh `VALIDATED_PAIR` rows.
3. `active_setups.exhaustion_signal_at_detection` (BOOLEAN, new column) — computed live in `acd.js` (added `volume` to the `allRthBarsRow` query for parity with the backtest's volRatio definition), mirrors the backtest's definition exactly, reads cutoffs from `liveStats._exhaustionCutoffs` (new `exhaustionCalibQ` query alongside the existing `_pairBonus` block). Persisted on every live INSERT.
4. Surfaced to the user as a caution note appended to the setup's `description` string (same convention as the existing `confluenceNote`) — **explicitly NOT wired into `sizeMultiplier` or suppression**, since the finding is real but not yet decisive.

**First real run's result**: TRAIN confluence+exhaustion n=173 EV=$0.41, TEST n=26 EV=-$7.22. Additive-effects check: predicted +$9.86/trade if confluence and exhaustion were independent non-interacting effects, actual $0.41 — a real ~$9.45 gap survives the leak fix, but neither TRAIN nor TEST clear `computeRigor`'s stability bar yet (matches the pre-fix leaked version's false "clean/stable" TRAIN reading — the corrected version doesn't have that property, which is itself evidence the fix was real). `RESEARCH_CLAIM` status: `PROVISIONAL`.

**Why this can't be decisive yet, and why that's not a dead end**: all 31 confluence-eligible levels' historical population is overwhelmingly `BACKFILL`-origin (confirmed: widening from BACKFILL-only to all origins barely changed N). Real (`ACTIVE`/`SHADOW`-origin) touches only exist from 2026-07-09 onward. This can only become genuinely decisive as real touches accumulate — which requires `exhaustion_signal_at_detection` to actually be persisted live starting now, which is exactly what this build does. The weekly rerun will re-test the interaction and refresh the live cutoffs automatically as that data grows; nothing further needs to happen for this to keep improving on its own.

Docs updated in the same session: `ARCHITECTURE.md` (`active_setups` entry + new script entry), `CLAUDE.md` "Where to look". `node --check`/`eslint` clean on `server/routes/acd.js` and the new script (verified live via the post-edit-lint hook).

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: fixed a real bug in `/api/stats/capture-ratio`, then retracted the interpretation built on it

User asked to work through the capture-ratio gap (treated earlier the same day as strong evidence of a real execution/behavioral problem, -40%). Investigating it surfaced a real, separate bug: `model_pnl` in `server/routes/stats.js`'s capture-ratio query had **no `origin_status` filter** — it summed real `ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` trades together with synthetic `BACKFILL` rows. Root cause: `scripts/backfill_unified_levels.mjs` (run 2026-07-18) queries `trade_date < CURRENT_DATE`, so a backfill re-run inserts synthetic rows all the way up through the day before it's run, not just old historical dates — confirmed directly, `BACKFILL`-origin rows exist with trade_dates as recent as 2026-07-16. Over a 60-day window, `BACKFILL` contributed $11,092.98 of `model_pnl` (698 of ~994 rows) vs real `ACTIVE`+`SHADOW`'s combined $3,146.76 — the "-40%" figure was mostly comparing synthetic data against the real account, not a real model-vs-execution measurement.

**Fixed** the query to require `origin_status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')`. Restarted the systemd-managed `trading-journal-server.service` (the process actually bound to port 3002, not the nodemon dev process) to pick up the change, confirmed via direct `curl`. Corrected result: only **2 trading days** have both real model data and real account data overlapping (2026-07-09 onward is all `origin_status` tracking exists) — nowhere near enough to compute a trustworthy capture ratio at all yet.

**Investigated those 2 real days directly and found something that changes the whole framing — then the user clarified their trades aren't indicative of this system's analysis and asked that they not be looked at further.** Retracting the earlier -40%/-59.4% capture-ratio findings as evidence of a real execution/behavioral gap — `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `capture_ratio_interpretation_corrected` records this explicitly so it doesn't get cited as settled evidence in a future session. The origin_status bug fix itself stands (real, independently-justified correction); the *interpretation* built on top of the pre-fix number does not. **Standing rule going forward: do not analyze or reference the contents of the `trades` table as evidence about whether this system's signals are being followed** — per explicit user instruction 2026-07-22.

`node --check`/`eslint` clean on `server/routes/stats.js`.

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: added an "all wired setups, no filter" scenario to the prop challenge — a ceiling, not a recommendation

Direct follow-up: user asked to run "all active wired setups" as a walkthrough. Checked whether this could be done with strictly-real (non-`BACKFILL`) data first — `active_setups.origin_status` only distinguishes real `ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` fires starting **2026-07-09** (13 days, 223 rows total, most setup_types single-digit N) — nowhere near a year. Asked the user to choose; they picked falling back to a full 1-year window using all available data (including synthetic `BACKFILL`) over accepting the honest-but-tiny 13-day answer.

Added a third scenario, `ALL_WIRED_NO_FILTER`, to `scripts/backtest_1yr_globex_inclusive_prop_challenge_20260720.mjs`: every setup_type that fired at least once in the window, with no quality gate at all (not the strict `SETUP_STATUS` check, not even `LEGACY_ROLLING`'s looser rolling N≥20/EV≥-$5 walk-forward check) — includes every currently-`SUPPRESS`-status setup_type mixed in with everything else.

**Result: $38,869.54 RTH-only at $400 DLL (N=4,436)** — much bigger than `LEGACY_ROLLING`'s $14,836 and `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER`'s -$1,697.60. **Documented clearly as a ceiling, not a recommendation** — this reflects mostly-`BACKFILL`-origin data (the system's retroactive calibration of the ideal stop/target per historical touch), with zero correction for setup_types already independently proven to lose (`SUPPRESS` status). Removing every filter doesn't validate anything; it just measures the full population's aggregate edge with the known losers still mixed in, diluted by everything else. Explicitly flagged: do not read this as "turn off suppression" — the whole `SETUP_STATUS` pipeline exists because an aggregate like this one hides real, individually-negative setups.

Updated `docs/GLOBEX_INCLUSIVE_1YR_PROP_RESULTS_20260722.md` in place (same file, new scenario added) with a dedicated section explaining exactly what `ALL_WIRED_NO_FILTER` is and isn't. `node --check`/`eslint` clean.

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: corrected the 1-year Globex-inclusive prop challenge — old doc's Globex leg was inflated by in-sample leakage

User asked for a 1-year prop walkthrough. One already existed (`scripts/backtest_1yr_globex_inclusive_prop_challenge_20260720.mjs`, built 2026-07-20) — but its Globex leg priced trades using `OVERNIGHT_OPTIMAL_STOP`, which a same-day fresh train/test holdout had independently found makes things *worse* on real out-of-sample data (flat $6,357 vs calibrated -$4,717, N=2,618 — `OPEN_DECISION` `overnight_calibration_needs_genuine_fresh_holdout_test`, resolved "do not wire in," `docs/OVERNIGHT_RESEARCH_SPEC.md` Part 6). The original script predates that resolution and used the calibration anyway.

**Fixed narrowly**: removed the `OVERNIGHT_OPTIMAL_STOP` lookup entirely, always use the flat fallback (90/40pt, 60/30pt Monday) — everything else in the script was already audited 2026-07-20 as "mechanics sound" (real chronological DLL interleave, fresh `SETUP_STATUS` query), so no other changes needed. Re-ran with today's date (window auto-updates to `MAX(trade_date)` — 2025-07-22 to 2026-07-22, 257 days) and current roster (reflects everything wired this session: new overnight levels, breakeven-trail variants, etc., since the roster query is dynamic).

**Result: dramatically smaller than the old doc, and that's the correct, honest number.** `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER` Globex-Included at $400 DLL: $3,117.82 (N=1396, WR 69.1%) — vs the old doc's $32,188 (N=1224). The Globex leg's real contribution is ~$3-4k, not $32-33k; the old figure was re-aggregating the same held-out evidence used to validate the (bad) calibration.

**Other real findings from this run**:
- `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER` RTH-only is flat-to-negative (-$1,611 to -$1,697, only 128-129 trades across the WHOLE YEAR) — reinforces today's earlier capture-ratio/sizeMultiplier findings that the honestly-currently-eligible RTH roster is thin and not clearly profitable alone.
- **Combining sessions under one shared DLL can reduce total P&L, not just add risk**: `LEGACY_ROLLING` (the more permissive scenario) does WORSE with Globex included than excluded at every DLL level ($16,510 vs $23,962 at $200 DLL) — extra Globex trades trigger more/faster lockouts (152 vs 131 lockout days) that cut off otherwise-profitable RTH trading later the same day. A real, non-obvious shared-DLL risk finding.
- Flagged clearly (not silently glossed over): the Globex leg's eligibility gate (rolling N≥20/EV≥-$5 walk-forward) is looser than the RTH `CURRENT_VALIDATED_ROSTER` gate (strict current-`SETUP_STATUS` check) — the two legs aren't apples-to-apples in every row, and only ~12 of the ~50 simulated overnight level-touches are actually wired into `detectGlobexSetup()` live today.

**Cleanup**: new corrected doc `docs/GLOBEX_INCLUSIVE_1YR_PROP_RESULTS_20260722.md`; old `docs/GLOBEX_INCLUSIVE_1YR_PROP_RESULTS_20260720.md` marked superseded in place (not deleted) with a caution banner explaining exactly why and pointing to the correction, per the standing "clean up what it replaces" rule. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `GLOBEX_INCLUSIVE_1YR_PROP` got a fresh 2026-07-22 row (N=1396, EV=$2.23/trade) — same slug, new `run_date`, matching the existing multi-row-per-signal convention (latest row is authoritative). `node --check`/`eslint` clean.

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: sizeMultiplier trust check + Bookmap-inspired volume-climax/absorption tests — both negative

Two threads, same session. **(1) User asked whether to trust "the sizing stuff."** Opus Audit 2 (2026-07-07, `scratch/opus_audit_2_results.md`) had already found the sizeMultiplier stack discriminates almost entirely through ONE factor (win/loss streak), with the other ~13 (now ~35 `mult=` lines) either redundant with streak, absorbed by the 1.5x ceiling (46% of setups hit it from streak alone), or possibly backwards (VWAP-extension). Checked the REAL `size_multiplier` column directly (only 4 rows existed at audit time, reconstruction-only) — now 63 resolved rows, but only **3 distinct values** (0.50 ×57, 0.70 ×1, 1.00 ×5). Confirmed root cause: 53 of the 57 rows at 0.50 have a prior loss earlier that same day (the cross-setup `hasLossToday` floor) — the elaborate stack is masked by this single binary floor for ~84% of every resolved trade with a multiplier. Capture ratio (model P&L vs actual account P&L) is now **-40%** (39 days, model +$14,636, actual -$5,860) — worse than the +$68K/-$7.8K gap that triggered the original audit, while sizeMultiplier complexity kept growing instead of freezing per that audit's own recommendation. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `sizemultiplier_real_data_shows_near_zero_variety`. **Decision: freeze further sizeMultiplier factor additions** (including not trusting today's earlier confluence-pair bonus as proven to matter yet), hold the RTH/Globex sizing-extraction idea, and prioritize Audit 2's own item #1 (join real trade fills to `active_setups` model verdicts) as the next real work — not yet built, `trades.setup_type`/`acd_signal` are entirely unpopulated (checked directly) so it requires genuine fuzzy date/time/price/direction matching, not a simple join.

**(2) User shared a Bookmap feature-comparison screenshot, asking about Large Lot Tracker / Strength Level / Imbalance / Absorption.** True L2-depth-based versions (Strength Level, true Imbalance) aren't buildable — no order-book depth data, not backfillable. Liquidation Indicator is crypto-exchange-specific (no CME equivalent feed). But volume-climax (LLT proxy) and absorption both have bar-level proxies buildable from `price_bars_primary`'s existing volume/bid_volume/ask_volume — and both are *also* verbatim Opus Audit 2 §6A untapped-data recommendations, never built until now. Built `scripts/pilot_volume_climax_absorption.mjs`, reusing the exact real-population/enrichment/3-way-confound-control pattern from `pilot_cvd_divergence.mjs` (real resolved BACKFILL `active_setups`, N=7200 across 60+ setup_types) rather than a from-scratch touch detector. **Both tested negative-or-unusable, honestly reported:**
- **Volume Climax**: real but tiny pooled effect (+$2.09/trade) that fails `computeRigor()`'s stability check despite huge N (1802/5398) and day coverage (374/410 days) — and the per-setup-type breakdown is genuinely inconsistent (roughly half the setup_types do better under climax, half do worse). Looks like an average of opposing per-setup effects cancelling out, not a real universal signal. `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `bookmap_volume_climax_llt_proxy_weak_unstable`.
- **Absorption**: first attempt (quartile cutoffs on both volume AND range) found only 1 of 7200 touches qualified — diagnosed as volume and range being structurally anti-correlated (high-volume bars mechanically tend to have wider ranges, confirmed directly: 537 actual joint occurrences vs ~1800 expected if independent), not merely a rare-and-real pattern. Loosened to median splits per the standing "don't give up on a thread without vetting it thoroughly" rule rather than reporting N=1 as the final word. Real result: **the hypothesis is backwards** — tight-range + delta-opposing-the-fade-direction predicts a stable, clean, but *negative* -$5.66/trade (and -$6.54/trade at N=3105/4095 when isolated from the volume condition). `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `bookmap_absorption_proxy_negative_not_positive`.

**Neither wired into setups.** Given both are negative/unusable and the sizing-system trust question is still open, this isn't the moment to add a new filter based on an ambiguous result either. `node --check`/`eslint` clean on the new pilot script.

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: Globex/overnight confluence-pair backtest — 5 pairs validated, standing new rule for future setup work

Direct follow-up, same day: user asked "these are 24 hour trades right?" — no, `backtest_confluence.js` uses `getRTHBars()`, strictly 9:30am-4pm ET, and had never been tested against the overnight/Globex window at all. User then gave a standing directive: **any new setup/signal/calibration finding must be evaluated for BOTH RTH and Globex windows before being considered complete** — added as a new CLAUDE.md hard rule (not just handled once here).

Built `scripts/backtest_confluence_globex.js`, mirroring the RTH script's pipeline over the overnight window (6PM-8:30AM ET) instead of RTH hours, reusing the RTH script's exported calibration/constants/helpers directly (exported `PNL_PER_POINT`/`COMMISSION`/`FADE_STOP`/`PROXIMITY`/`LOOK_FORWARD`/`PAIR_MIN_DISTINCT_DAYS`/`loadPairProximityThresholds()`/`pct()`/`fmt()` from `backtest_confluence.js` for this). Excluded same-day-forming levels (OR/IB), circularly-defined ones (ONH/ONL), and `RTH_VWAP` (live-accumulates through its own date) from the Globex candidate set, and looked up level values as of the prior trading day (not the session's own date) — both conservative lookahead-safety calls made under time pressure rather than verified case-by-case.

**Found and fixed a real bug mid-build**: `backtest_confluence.js` called its own `run()` unconditionally at module scope with no entry-point guard. The moment the new Globex script `import`ed its shared exports, that import triggered a full second RTH `run()` as a side effect — racing concurrently against the real Globex run for the same DB connection, with whichever finished first calling `process.exit(0)` and killing the other mid-flight. Caught because the first "Globex" result exactly matched the RTH numbers (touch counts, qualified-pair counts, all identical) — the standard too-clean-result tell. Fixed with the same `import.meta.url` entry-point guard already used elsewhere in this codebase (`flag_decision.mjs`); re-verified RTH still reproduces its own 71/1461 result standalone after the fix.

**Real, clean result**: 421 overnight sessions, 890 qualified (N≥10) pairs, only **5 clear the full validation bar** — much thinner than RTH's 71/1461, consistent with overnight structure being less consistent than RTH. The 5: `PD_IB_LOW+PD_LOW` ($10.56, N=1354), `FLOOR_PIVOT+PD_SESSION_MID` ($7.01, N=1422), `PD_HIGH+PD_IB_HIGH` ($6.83, N=2225), `PD_CLOSE+PD_POC` ($4.31, N=1254), `CAM_S4+FLOOR_S1` ($4.06, N=998). 2 of these (`FLOOR_PIVOT+PD_SESSION_MID`, `PD_CLOSE+PD_POC`) also independently validate on the RTH side — genuinely cross-session-robust, not a session-specific artifact. Persisted to `CONFLUENCE_AUDIT_OVERNIGHT` (distinct signal_type from RTH's `CONFLUENCE_AUDIT`).

**Wired what's appropriate, flagged what isn't**: `confluence_score_at_detection`/`confluence_levels_at_detection` now persist on every `detectGlobexSetup()` insert (same fields as RTH — `candidates` after its existing `.filter()` is already the full "levels near price this poll" set, no separate nearLevels computation needed). NOT wired: `detectGlobexSetup()` has no `sizeMultiplier` stack at all (unlike the RTH engine), so there's no mechanism to hook a pair-bonus into yet — rather than bolt one on ad hoc (a real design decision: baseline, interaction with the SHADOW/ACTIVE promotion gate, cap), flagged as `OPEN_DECISION` `globex_confluence_pair_bonus_needs_sizing_mechanism`. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `confluence_pair_globex_backtest`. `test_invariants.mjs`/`eslint`/`node --check` all clean.

## ✅ 2026-07-22 continued: per-pair calibrated proximity + 2-year backtest — 71 pairs now genuinely validated and wired live

Direct follow-up to the entry immediately below (same day) — user asked to build the per-pair proximity calibration flagged as `OPEN_DECISION` there, then backtest 2 years before wiring in the good ones and leaving the rest unwired. **Superseded that entry's "zero pairs qualify" conclusion** — that was correct for what it tested (180-day window, flat 15pt/30pt proximity for every pair uniformly) but not the final answer.

**Widened `backtest_confluence.js` to the full available `level_prices` history** (~420 trading days / ~1.7yr, earliest row 2023-11-13 — reported precisely rather than rounded to "2 years"). **Built `loadPairProximityThresholds()`**: computes each pair's own historical p25 `|priceA-priceB|` directly from `level_prices` (>=30 days shared history required) and uses `min(30pt, that pair's own p25)` as the real threshold — confirmed directly that some pairs (`PD_HIGH`/`PD_IB_HIGH`, `IB_HIGH`/`OR_HIGH`, `IB_LOW`/`OR_LOW`, etc.) sit within 30pt of each other on almost every single day by construction (p25 distance 0-2pt), so the old flat cap couldn't tell genuine rare convergence from routine near-duplication for those specific pairs.

**Found and fixed a real, separate bug mid-build**: `getRTHBars()`/`getTradingDays()` had no `symbol='NQ'` filter on `price_bars_primary` — the documented ES-contamination window (2023-11-15 to 2023-12-15) silently mixed ES bars (~$4,500 in that period) into the NQ bar sequence for dates in that range, producing multi-thousand-point spurious MAE/MFE. Caught directly (not by any automated check) via two pairs showing `MFE P50 > 11,000pt`, an impossible value for NQ in a 30-bar window — same standing bug class `data_sanity_audit.mjs` already flags, just never fixed in this specific script. Fixed by adding `symbol='NQ'` to both queries.

**Dispatched a full-qualified-set audit to Gemini** (1463 qualified N>=10 pairs — far more than the old top-10-by-EV persistence slice) as a cross-check. Its methodology was sound (copied the script, reused real functions, didn't touch the DB) but its numbers weren't trusted as final: its scratch copy may have raced against the mid-session symbol-filter fix, and it dispatched before the `isCalibrated` distinction existed, so it couldn't have separated real calibration from the developing-level fallback. Its output was useful only as a rough directional sanity check (confirmed the right order of magnitude, ~300+ pairs clearing `distinctDays>=20`) and correctly flagged that `VWAP`/`DEV_POC` pairs dominated the top of the list — which is exactly the confound this session then addressed properly. Final authoritative numbers came from re-running the corrected script directly myself.

**The confound**: session `VWAP` and `DEV_POC` are *developing* levels — computed per-bar inside the script, not stored in `level_prices`, so they can never get real per-pair calibration and always fall back to the flat cap. Worse, a developing level tracks price all session by definition, so "VWAP near static level X" mostly just means "price lingered near level X for a while," not two independent structural levels genuinely converging — the same "does the smarter arm have a structural advantage baked in" question this codebase's confound checklist already asks for comparison backtests, applied here to a rare-event / independence assumption instead. Added an `isCalibrated` flag (persisted per pair, not inferred from level-name string matching at the consumer — brittle, since `WEEKLY_VWAP`/`MONTHLY_VWAP` matched a naive `%VWAP%` filter despite being real once-daily static levels that calibrate normally) so `VALIDATED_PAIR` status structurally excludes any developing-level pair regardless of its raw numbers.

**Rewrote the persistence loop's selection criterion from "top 10 by raw EV" to a real validity gate** — the old ranking was actively wrong: a pair with 1-2 clustered days can show huge EV from a handful of lucky touches, while a pair with real 20+-day coverage and modest EV is the trustworthy one. `recommendation='VALIDATED_PAIR'` now requires ALL of `distinctDays>=20` + `isCalibrated` + `computeRigor().clean` + `ev_per_trade>0` in one place, persisted so the live consumer (`acd.js`) never re-derives the criteria itself.

**Final result: 71 of 1461 qualified (N>=10) pairs are genuinely validated** — real N (301-1291 touches), EV $4.07-$19.99/trade (avg ~$10.43), e.g. `CAM_S1+DAILY_OPEN` ($19.99, N=301), `CAM_R1+PD_VAH` ($13.95, N=871), `PD_CLOSE+PD_POC` ($13.48, N=824). The other 266 pairs clearing `distinctDays>=20` but failing `isCalibrated`/`clean`/EV get `NEAR_MISS_MONITOR` — no live bonus, but continue accumulating real data via the existing weekly `run_confluence_backtest.sh` recompute, same self-healing behavior as everything else in this pipeline. **Wired live**: `acd.js`'s `liveStats._pairBonus` now filters on `recommendation==='VALIDATED_PAIR'` directly, replacing both the original hardcoded 5-pair map and the temporary zero-bonus state from the first pass. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `confluence_pair_proximity_2yr_backtest`; `OPEN_DECISION` `confluence_proximity_should_be_per_pair_calibrated` resolved. `test_invariants.mjs`/`eslint`/`node --check` all clean.

## ✅ 2026-07-22: confluence data persistence — schema + live wiring + real pair-bonus lookup (first pass — see corrected result immediately above)

User question ("what happened to the confluence data? there may be levels that perform poorly alone but better near other levels") surfaced a real gap: `acd.js` computed `confluenceCount`/`confluenceLevels`/`confluencePairPartner` per-candidate live but **never persisted any of it** — `active_setups.confluence_score_at_detection` (a real, pre-existing INTEGER column) was written by nothing. User's follow-up point sharpened the ask: audit by the *specific combination* of levels present, not just a count, then "do it and wire it in."

**Built all 3 parts**: (1) added `confluence_levels_at_detection TEXT[]` to `active_setups`; (2) wired both live INSERT paths in `acd.js` — the main candidate insert AND, more importantly, the suppressed-near-level SHADOW-audit insert (the population that actually matters for the user's hypothesis, since it's the currently-suppressed levels whose confluence performance is unknown); (3) replaced the hardcoded, never-validated 5-pair `_PAIR_BONUS_MAP` with a live lookup (`liveStats._pairBonus`) against `backtest_confluence.js`'s real `PAIR:X+Y` data (a genuine bar-simulation, not `active_setups`-sourced, so not BACKFILL-tainted).

**Before trusting any pair data as a live sizing input, dispatched a stability check to Gemini** — its response file came back garbled/inconsistent (looked like an interleaved write) claiming a suspiciously uniform "100% of every pair fails, zero exceptions." Treated as suspect per the standing audit rule and re-derived directly instead. **Real finding, more precise than Gemini's**: every qualified pair converges on only 1-8 *distinct days* out of the 180-day window. Since both levels in a pair are static/fixed-for-the-day, "close together" is a fact about the day, not the touch — a pair's N can be almost entirely one single day of intraday re-touching. The standard touch-count clustering check is structurally the wrong diagnostic here (it will read as "clustered" for any real or fake pair effect); `distinctDays` is the correct unit of independent evidence, and none of the 15 currently-qualified pairs clear any meaningful floor. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `confluence_pairs_fail_distinct_day_floor`.

**Fixed at the source, not just diagnosed**: extended `backtest_confluence.js`'s pair-persistence loop to compute `computeRigor()` + `distinctDays` (previously only the SINGLE/DOUBLE/TRIPLE/QUAD tier loop had rigor checking) and persist both as structured JSON in `notes`. The live lookup gates on `meetsMinDistinctDays` (`PAIR_MIN_DISTINCT_DAYS=20`, reusing this codebase's existing N≥20 significance floor applied to the correct unit for this data shape). As of 2026-07-22 this correctly yields **zero bonuses** for every level — same practical effect as deleting the hardcoded map outright, but self-healing as real convergence days accumulate via the existing weekly `run_confluence_backtest.sh` recompute, instead of sitting frozen on unvalidated guesswork forever.

**Also flagged, not built**: the flat 15pt proximity window used to define "near" in the first place (live in `acd.js` twice, plus the backtest script) is itself a static threshold — some level pairs are structurally almost always within 15pt of each other, others rarely are, so one flat cutoff can't distinguish routine proximity from genuine rare convergence. User raised this directly; recorded as `OPEN_DECISION` `confluence_proximity_should_be_per_pair_calibrated`, scoped as its own follow-up research pass (needs per-pair historical distance-distribution calibration from `level_prices`) rather than bolted onto this fix.

**Verification**: `node --check` + `npx eslint` clean on both touched files, `test_invariants.mjs` full pass (2 pre-existing `PD_POC`/`PD_VAL` `OPTIMAL_STOP` drift failures found and fixed via a routine `update_optimal_stops.mjs` re-run, confirmed via `git stash` to predate this session's changes).

Fixed directly (removed the erroneous line, restored the reference script's true first-touch-any-direction semantics, fixed the results-object initialization to allocate both directions per level) and re-ran locally.

**Corrected result: 4 of 19 confirmed safe with clean agreement** — `MPP_FADE_SHORT`, `MONTHLY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`, `10D_IB_MID_FADE_SHORT`, `WR1_FADE_SHORT` (small N/EV deltas, no sign flips, both RTH and wide-window numbers reproduce closely). ## 🆕 2026-07-21 continued: does the breakeven-trail mechanism's benefit vary by day-type?

Follow-up to the "change what/how we ask questions" pivot below — instead of testing another new entry signal, combined two things that have each independently proven out: the breakeven-trail mechanism (real, 6 survivors) and day-type/session conditioning (the one signal category Opus Audit #4 found reliably generalizes). First ran the discrimination check (does trail improvement correlate with setup quality? No clean pattern, N=6 — `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `breakeven_trail_no_quality_correlation`; also flagged `OPEN_DECISION` `floor_s1_fade_long_trail_fires_on_suppressed_base_entry` since that setup's base entry is a confirmed stable loser). Now testing whether the mechanism's benefit concentrates on certain day-types (TREND vs BALANCE vs TURBULENT) — dispatched to Gemini, reusing `backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs`'s exact bar-walk mechanics and each setup's ALREADY-calibrated trail width (deliberately not re-sweeping per day-type — individual setup N, 16-77 trades, is too thin to support a fresh 3-way split without reopening the overfitting risk the original calibration's guardrails exist to prevent). Pooled across all 6 setups first (individual per-setup buckets expected to mostly be thin), with a per-setup breakdown too.

**Result: genuinely inconclusive, neither confirmed nor cleanly rejected.** Pooled, `TREND` shows the biggest delta (+$5.90, N=167) and `TURBULENT` the only one with confirmed-clean rigor (+$3.20, N=183) — `BALANCE` (+$1.43, N=410) and `TREND`'s rigor status came back as "Failed, no reason provided" from Gemini instead of the actual clustered/stable detail requested, a real gap in the response worth noting. Per-setup, the story doesn't hold together: TREND-day delta is positive in 5/6 setups but `DAILY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`'s is negative (-$0.59), and `TURBULENT` — not `TREND` — has the biggest delta in 3 of 6 setups individually. The pooled "TREND wins" read looks driven by a couple of higher-N setups rather than a universal pattern. Recorded honestly as inconclusive (`RESEARCH_CLAIM` `breakeven_trail_daytype_conditioning_inconclusive`) rather than forced into a win or a loss — not every test resolves cleanly, and this one doesn't. Not used to condition anything live. Worth revisiting once the 6 live-wired variants accumulate real forward touches, rather than pursued further on this historical sample alone.

## ✅ 2026-07-21: Opus Audit #4 results — the meta-pattern is real but narrower than stated, and one of today's own findings didn't survive a fair test

Ran `docs/OPUS_AUDIT_PROMPT_4.md` (full results: `scratch/opus_audit_4_results.md`). Headline: **"order-flow generalizes, price-action doesn't" is real but overstated — the true distinction is magnitude-vs-direction, not order-flow-vs-price-action.** Verified Opus's own work before acting on it (spot-checked its verification script `scratch/fair_test_volconf.mjs` reuses the real, unmodified classifiers with no reimplementation, and its per-setup numbers exactly matched what was already recorded for 3 setups checked by hand).

**Q1 corrected meta-pattern**: inside the narrow "what happens at a level touch" family, the split IS clean — every pure price-shape entry signal has failed (candle shape ×2, 2026-07-15 price-action touch-quality pilot, regime A/C z-scores from `REGIME_DETECTION_SPEC.md`), and the only feature that generalized is `touchQuality.js`'s volume **magnitude** z-score. But projected onto the FULL `RESEARCH_CLAIM` ledger (87 claims, pulled live), order-flow **direction/delta** signals have failed at least 4 times (`overnight_cvd_weak_range_signal_no_directional_power`, `intrabar_cvd_divergence_no_edge_confounded`, `volume_spike_entry_signal_gemini_bug_corrected`, `session_pulse_down_low_signal` failing stability), while several price-derived *conditioning* signals (not fresh shape/z-score constructions, but conditioning an already-validated setup) produced real, clean, stable wins (`session_bias_conflict_sizing_factor`, `regime_b_stretched_low_validated_stretched_high_not`, several regime-conditioning splits). GARCH and `CVD_DAILY`, cited earlier today as "generalized order-flow wins," are corrected: GARCH is a modest minority effect not wired live, `CVD_DAILY` explicitly found no directional power. **Corrected header framing**: pure-geometry entry-timing signals fail (5+ independent fails); order-flow *magnitude* generalized once and is live; order-flow *direction* has failed 4×; the most reliable live findings are conditioning signals, categorically outside both buckets.

**Q2 — do not fund another delta-direction signal.** Evaluated the 3 prompted ideas: cumulative session-delta trend is bracketed by two failures of the same construct (low priority); bid/ask imbalance persistence is the best of the delta-direction ideas but still capped by that family's 0-for-4 record (one cheap control-gated pilot only, if anything); level-agnostic absorption (a level-fade-paradigm-questioning idea) is a genuine multi-session research direction, correctly out of scope for a single build. **Top pick instead**: volume z-score *trajectory* across the reaction window (rising vs fading participation, not just the peak-z snapshot `touchQuality.js` currently reduces to) — extends the one feature that actually generalized, reuses `getVolumeBaseline()` verbatim. **Second pick**: level-anchored volume-node/absorption reusing `developingValueService.js`'s `computeProfile()` (the correct volume-profile reference implementation). Neither built yet — offered, not requested.

**Q3 — `volume_confirmed_candle_pattern_low_vol_trap` FAILS A FAIR TEST, retracted with full account (not silently edited).** The claim (recorded earlier the same day) selected the 6 largest-effect-size setup_types from a 48-setup exploratory pass. Opus ran the missing fair test: full-corpus pooled HIGH_VOL_PATTERN EV=-$5.00 (N=1919) — the WORST bucket corpus-wide, not the best — vs LOW_VOL +$1.06 (N=3845) vs NO_PATTERN +$4.88 (N=1436); only 13 of 48 setups favor HIGH_VOL at all, and the selected 6 were entirely drawn from that minority direction. Held-out replication (excluding the selected 6): only 7 of 42 remaining setups favor HIGH_VOL, N-weighted pooled HIGH_VOL=-$10.26 vs LOW_VOL=+$3.69 — **the opposite sign from the original claim**. A double selection-bias artifact (minority direction + largest-effect tail of a sweep), same failure family as the 2026-07-19 scale-out-runner baseline-mismatch retraction and the 2026-07-20 `ON_LEVEL_EDGES_CALIBRATED` in-sample retraction. `RESEARCH_CLAIM volume_confirmed_candle_pattern_low_vol_trap` updated in place with the full retraction account (not deleted — matches this file's own "resolved items get struck through/updated, not silently removed" convention). **Do not wire an `ACDView.jsx` badge for this finding.**

**Q4 — yes, build a lightweight guard, but narrowly.** The confound-then-retraction pattern has recurred at least 5 times across ~5 sessions (2 reaching `RESEARCH_CLAIM`/`OPEN_DECISION` before retraction, now 3 counting today's Q3 finding), always looked like a real win first. Recommends: (1) add `computeReplication()` to `server/services/rigorDiagnostics.js` alongside `computeRigor()` — a held-out/permutation check specifically for "top-K selected from a sweep, then rigor-checked" findings (the one cheaply automatable sub-family); (2) document a 4-item confound checklist as a CLAUDE.md Conventions entry, referencing the `pilot_cvd_divergence.mjs` 3-way-control template as the pattern for the OTHER sub-family (structural/algebraic advantage in one arm), which is domain-specific each time and NOT auto-detectable. Explicitly recommends against a generic "detect any confound" function — would create false confidence. Not built yet — offered, not requested.

Full report: `scratch/opus_audit_4_results.md`. Consolidated action items (none built yet, all offered pending explicit go-ahead): soften this file's own "order-flow generalizes" header (done, see above); pilot volume z-score trajectory; pilot level-anchored volume-node/absorption; flag level-agnostic absorption as its own multi-session `OPEN_DECISION`; add `computeReplication()` + the confound checklist convention.
