## 🆕 2026-07-17: Setup Log duplicates + hidden-ACTIVE-setups — user screenshot, both fixed same session

User flagged two symptoms via screenshot: (1) `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` appearing twice, identically, in both the sidebar Session Timeline and the Setup Log table; (2) `VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT` (a real, resolved, target-hit trade visible in the sidebar) never appearing in the Setup Log table at all, plus more sidebar entries "missing in history" generally.

**Duplicate bug — root cause found, not just patched.** Queried `active_setups` for the full day: `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` had duplicated on *every single fire* that day (10:39, 14:05, 14:30, 14:54, 14:55, 14:58 — all identical `fired_at` pairs), not an isolated glitch. Traced to `acd.js`'s `sessionEndET` hard cap, hardcoded to **1:00 PM ET since the file's original 2026-06-01 commit** — never updated across two later extensions (full RTH detection, then 2026-07-16's full-Globex-hours poller). Any setup detected after 1 PM got an `expires_at` already in the past at the moment of insert, so the very next 15s poll instantly expired it (`TIME_EXPIRED`), and since the dedup check only looks at still-open `ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` rows, the poll after that re-inserted a fresh duplicate for the exact same touch. Full technical writeup and the fix (4 PM cap with day-rollover, plus a new unconditional `(trade_date, setup_type, fired_at)` unique index so this class of bug can't recur regardless of cause) is in CLAUDE.md's new hard rule. Repaired 13 pre-existing duplicate pairs across all history (6 from that one day alone) — backups: `active_setups_duplicate_touch_backup_20260717`, `trade_timeline_events_duplicate_backup_20260717` (the sidebar reads a *separate* table, `trade_timeline_events`, joined via FK to `active_setups.id` — that's why the duplicate showed in both places and had to be cleaned up in both).

**Hidden-ACTIVE-setups bug — separate root cause, same session.** `VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT` was being filtered out by a hardcoded `SHADOW_SETUP_TYPES` list in `acd.js` (a display-filter list, not a trading-suppression list, but the same anti-pattern) — checked it against the live SETUP_STATUS pipeline and found it stale: `OPEN_DRIVE_SHORT`, `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_LONG`, and `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` were hardcoded as permanently hidden while the live pipeline had already promoted all three to `ACTIVE` (real N≥44 samples, positive EV). Fixed by replacing the hardcoded list with a live `performance_audit` SETUP_STATUS query, matching the exact same `recommendation IN ('SUPPRESS','THIN_N')` logic the live detection path already uses for `_suppressedSetups`. `VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT` itself is genuinely `THIN_N` (N=2) so it's still correctly hidden by default — the fix isn't "unhide everything," it's "make what's hidden match the live pipeline, not a stale manual list."

**Verified end-to-end**: restarted the server, confirmed `/api/setups/history` now returns 11 rows for the day with zero duplicates (was 6, undercounting due to the dedup gap), confirmed `shadow=show` correctly surfaces `VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT`/`OPEN_DRIVE_LONG` with accurate `is_shadow_type` flags, confirmed zero new duplicates after the restart. `test_invariants.mjs`/`data_sanity_audit.mjs` both clean except one expected transient: removing a duplicate `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG` row shifted its trade population enough that its persisted `OPTIMAL_STOP` briefly disagreed with a fresh sweep — resolved by re-running `update_optimal_stops.mjs` (and `backtest_setup_status.mjs` for full consistency), both clean after.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: Why didn't setups fire overnight after yesterday's Globex-hours fix? — process instability, not a code bug

Full decision text: `node scripts/flag_decision.mjs --list` → `overnight_globex_fix_never_ran_uninterrupted` (HIGH). Summary here for narrative context.

Commit `9fb677b` (2026-07-16 18:04 ET) extended the autonomous setup-detection poller (`server/index.js`'s `setInterval`) from RTH-only to real CME Globex hours, so level-fade setups should now fire 24hr/day. Checked the very next morning: **zero `active_setups` rows were created between 2026-07-16 18:00 ET and 2026-07-17 09:36 ET** — the entire overnight session produced no detections, despite `price_bars_primary` showing a clean, gap-free 60-bars/hour feed all night (data ingestion is healthy and unaffected).

Investigation ruled out the obvious suspects in order:
1. **Not a bug in the Globex-hours code itself** — read correctly, and the fire-gate logic looks sound.
2. **Not the machine/WSL going fully offline** — `trading-journal-watcher.service` (a separate always-on unit) stayed up the whole night with no gap, so the VM itself didn't suspend.
3. **Not a crash in the new code** — `journalctl -u trading-journal-server.service` shows the server unit was stopped+started ~15 times between 18:19-21:00 ET (expected: matches last night's active dev-session commit cadence exactly — normal manual restarts during development), but then **kept restarting unattended overnight with zero commits happening**: 00:38, 04:58, 05:28, 06:30, 07:08, 07:43, 07:48 ET. Every one of these is a clean stop/start with no "Main process exited" abnormal code, no stack trace, no uncaught exception anywhere in the logs — ruling out a crash-on-bad-overnight-data theory.
4. **Not `gemini_error_watcher.mjs`** — it only restarts the server on unreachable/degraded endpoints and logs every such event to `scratch/gemini_alerts.txt`; that file has zero entries for the whole overnight window.

**Clock-drift lead — investigated further, now definitively ruled out.** The continuous `Clock change detected. Flushing caches.` (systemd-resolved, every ~30-35s) turned out to be constant background noise, not something specific to the overnight window — confirmed it fires at the identical cadence during stable daytime hours with zero restarts happening. Traced every plausible clock-correction source and found none active: the user had a Sierra Chart NTP client enabled, but its own settings showed "Has Privilege to set System time: No" (never capable of adjusting the clock) and a 60s poll interval that didn't match the observed ~35s cadence anyway; disabled it live and confirmed the cadence was completely unchanged. Windows' own `W32Time` service and the Hyper-V Time Synchronization Service (`vmictimesync`) are both **Stopped**. With every checkable mechanism ruled out, the messages are almost certainly just WSL2's own internal Hyper-V-clocksource-based guest-clock housekeeping (visible in `dmesg` as `hyperv_clocksource_tsc_page`) — a permanent, benign characteristic of the VM architecture, not a lead worth re-chasing.

**Why it matters**: this app runs on a local WSL2 dev machine, not a stable always-on host. Even fully correct "fire 24 hours a day" code can't deliver on that promise if the process hosting it gets bounced every 30-90 minutes overnight for reasons outside the app's own logic. Next time someone reports "X didn't fire overnight," check `systemctl --user status trading-journal-server.service` and its restart cadence in journalctl *before* assuming the detection logic is at fault.

**Status**: root cause still unknown — every investigated lead (crash, OOM, watchdog, cron, `gemini_error_watcher.mjs`, and now clock drift) is exhausted. `server/index.js` now logs a durable `process_log` row (`process_name='SERVER_SHUTDOWN'`, `status`=signal/reason) on `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT`/`uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection`, verified working across real restarts. Nothing more can be learned from archaeology on the past incident — the next natural unattended restart will finally show, via `process_log.status`, whether it's an external `SIGTERM` (shifts the mystery to who's issuing it) or a genuine crash (now named specifically instead of a silent gap).

**Update, same session — dug deeper, still no root cause, but instrumented for next time**: ruled out more candidates (no crash/exception anywhere in the logs, no OOM kill, no systemd watchdog — `WatchdogSec` isn't even set in the unit file, no cron/external script anywhere calls `systemctl` on this unit, not a session/VM-wide bounce). Confirmed `gemini_error_watcher.mjs`'s restart mechanism is real and has fired historically (`scratch/gemini_watcher.log` shows genuine events on 7/2 through 7/14) — but it always logs a `SERVER_DOWN` alert *before* restarting, and there's no such alert for this specific night, so it's very unlikely to be the trigger here (though whether its logging could silently fail while still restarting isn't 100% ruled out). The restart signature (clean `Stopping→Stopped→Started`, no failure code) matches an external `systemctl restart` command, not a crash — but who issued it is still unknown.

Since pure log archaeology hit a wall, added real instrumentation instead of continuing to guess: `server/index.js` now has `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT`/`uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` handlers that log to console *and* insert a durable `process_log` row (`process_name='SERVER_SHUTDOWN'`, `scheduled_time`=the signal/reason, `metadata`={pid, uptimeSec}) before exiting. Verified live across two real restarts — the `SIGTERM` row lands correctly. Next time this recurs, `process_log` will finally say whether it's an external stop (proving something *is* calling `systemctl restart`, shifting the mystery to who) or an actual crash — something journald alone couldn't distinguish before.

## ✅ 2026-07-17: volume-area bucket-by-low bug — fixed, backfilled, trades re-tagged — **Resolved 2026-07-17 (same session)**

Full resolution text: `node scripts/flag_decision.mjs --list-all` → `value_area_volume_bucketing_bug_all_locations`. Summary here for narrative context — this closes out the bug first found during the "review the other levels" thread below.

`scripts/compute_levels.js`, `server/routes/acd.js` (6 spots), and `server/routes/weekly.js` (3 endpoints, all confirmed dead code) all hand-duplicated a volume-profile calculation that bucketed 100% of each 1-minute bar's volume at that bar's LOW price, then split the value area as a fixed 35%-up/35%-down from POC instead of the standard alternating-to-70% method. Fixed by centralizing on `server/services/developingValueService.js`'s already-correct `computeProfile()` via a new exported `computeVolumeProfileForRange(queryFn, opts)` wrapper — see CLAUDE.md's new hard rule.

Backed up and recomputed the full `level_prices` history for the 9 affected level_names (`PW_VAH/PW_VAL/PW_POC`, `PM_VAH/PM_VAL/PM_POC`, `3M_VAH/3M_VAL/3M_POC` — 416/416 dates, 0 errors). The real before/after diff (not Gemini's estimate — see below) showed the value area compressing toward POC: VAH averaged -725pt (3M) / -422pt (PM) / -145pt (PW) lower, while VAL/POC averaged +91 to +877pt higher. The old bug's bar-low-only bucketing spread volume across many distinct low prices, artificially widening the apparent value area; the fix concentrates it at the true traded prices.

Re-tagged all 9,051 currently-tagged trades' `level_proximity` against the corrected data (`scripts/retag_level_proximity_va_fix.mjs`) — **4,919 (54%) changed nearest_level or tag**, confirming the corruption's reach was much wider than the 653 trades whose nearest level was directly one of the 9 affected names (a wrong VA value could displace a correct level from a trade's top3 even when the final tag wasn't itself VA-related).

Also resolved in the process: both `/weekly/va-history` and `/daily/va-history` (plus `/weekly/bars`) turned out to have zero frontend callers — the "is this a second, disagreeing source of truth for daily VA" concern from the original flag was moot since nothing currently consumes either path live.

**Gemini audit note**: dispatched Gemini to quantify drift magnitude as a supplementary read-only analysis while the code fix was in progress. Its report claimed all-positive drift across POC/VAH/VAL — directly contradicted by the real backup-vs-recompute diff above (VAH moved negative). Spot-checked one of its "buggy" reconstructions directly against the actual pre-fix SQL and it didn't match (a genuine replication error on Gemini's part, not a case of the underlying bug behaving inconsistently) — caught via the standing "audit all Gemini output" rule before any of its numbers were used. Its output was discarded in favor of the authoritative backup/recompute diff.

**Verified clean**: `node scripts/test_invariants.mjs` and `node scripts/data_sanity_audit.mjs` — both show only pre-existing, unrelated flags (WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP base/variant conflict; the standing ES-symbol-in-`price_bars_primary` exception). `npm run lint` + eslint on all 4 touched files: 0 errors.

**Update, same session**: items 8-9 (the deferred backtests) were then also run, closing the loop the original flag actually asked for ("run when this is picked up — do not stop partway," which the initial pass had not fully honored):
- **2D_POC backtest** (`scripts/backtest_pd2_2dpoc_complete.mjs`, extending `backtest_pd2_final.mjs`'s validated methodology — realistic touch-bar-open entry, no volume trigger): `2D_POC_FADE_LONG` N=87, EV=-$28.87, WR=26.4%, stable (not day-clustered). This is the final, definitive close-out of the level that started the entire "review the other levels" investigation — the original stale +$23.66 EV claim was an artifact at every stage (stale pipeline → buggy synthetic backtest → entry-price-bugged fixed backtest → wrong live price feeding the level itself); once modeled correctly end-to-end, there is no edge. PD2_VAH/PD2_VAL re-ran identical to their already-closed prior result (N=53 EV=-$41.16; N=67 EV=-$35.70), confirming methodology consistency. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `2d_poc_fade_no_edge`.
- **Stage 1 tolerance re-run** (`scratch/gemini_full_tolerance_study.mjs`) against the corrected `level_prices`: confirmed *concrete* damage, not just theoretical — **`PW_VAL`/`PM_VAL` had spuriously collapsed onto `PW_LOW`/`PM_LOW`'s exact touch statistics pre-fix** (identical N, identical percentiles for both pairs), because the buggy VAL computation pushed all the way to the session/period low on many dates. Post-fix each is correctly distinct (`PW_VAL` N=58→90, `PM_VAL` N=28→43), and `3M_VAL` cleared the N≥20 floor for the first time (17→29). Non-VA-family levels confirmed unchanged between runs, isolating the effect to exactly the 9 fixed level_names. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `va_family_stage1_tolerance_rerun_post_bucketing_fix`. No live-fireable trading decision follows from this specific number (the volume-spike-trigger idea built on top of Stage 1 was already tested and rejected).

## 🆕 2026-07-17: "review the other levels" thread — from a 3-level backtest to a real methodology bug to paused entry-tolerance research

Narrative summary tying together a chain of same-session findings, each persisted individually as its own `OPEN_DECISION` (`node scripts/flag_decision.mjs --list` for full text) — this entry is the story, not the state.

1. **Started from**: reviewing PD2_VAH/PD2_VAL/2D_POC's fresh backtest (see the entry below) prompted "can we review the other levels like this?"
2. **Found the scope was much bigger than 3 levels**: 27 level types total (not 3) have essentially zero real live-fire history in `active_setups` (≤5 total rows ever, most literally 0) vs. ~35 others that went through the audited 2026-07-14 backfill fix wave. 23 of the 27 read a simple stored value from `level_prices` (trivial); 1 (`10D_IB_MID`) needed custom formula replication; 3 already done.
3. **Ran the same bug-fixed methodology across the 24 remaining levels** (`scripts/backtest_thin_levels.mjs`) — only 12 of 48 variants cleared N≥20. One (`DAILY_OPEN_FADE_SHORT`) was immediately confirmed as a pure artifact: `DAILY_OPEN`'s stored value sits within 15pt of that same day's own 9:30 open 100% of the time (avg distance 0.0) — its "touches" are just the market open every day, not a real level test.
4. **The remaining 11 had suspiciously high EVs ($14-80/trade)** — dispatched to Gemini for root-cause audit with a specific hypothesis to test (not just "look for problems"). **Found and independently re-verified (pulled raw bars myself, matched Gemini's numbers exactly): both this backtest AND the earlier PD2/2D_POC one shared a real methodology bug** — assuming a fill at the exact stored level price regardless of whether the touch bar's own open/close ever traded there. Concretely: `ONL_FADE_LONG` touch on 2025-07-01 gives MAE=7pt under the level-pegged assumption (survives a 3pt stop) vs. MAE=39.5pt under a realistic touch-bar-open entry (immediate stop-out). **This invalidates the EV/stop numbers from BOTH of tonight's fresh backtests** — persisted as `OPEN_DECISION` `entry_price_pegged_to_level_bug_both_backtests`, paused deliberately before re-running anything, per explicit user instruction ("I don't want to start tearing everything down without knowing the impact").
5. **User proposed the actual fix**: empirically study real overshoot/undershoot per level to derive a genuine data-driven entry tolerance (replacing both the naive exact-level assumption AND the blanket static 15pt window), then require a volume-spike (reusing the already-live `touchQuality.js` z-score machinery — confirmed real and cron-wired, not just claimed in a comment) as an entry trigger within that tolerance, persisting the calibration per level if it validates.
6. **Stage 1 (research only) ran clean**: 17 real level types, 3093 touches. Confirmed the bug's scope is huge (only 25-40% of "touches" ever actually reach the exact level) and produced sane, realistic distance percentiles (median 23-33pt, comparable to real calibrated stops) — full results and the honest caveat (these numbers are suspiciously *similar* across totally different level families, suggesting they may partly reflect generic 1-min-bar-size/velocity rather than purely level-specific behavior) persisted as `OPEN_DECISION` `level_approach_tolerance_and_volume_spike_entry_research`.
7. **Paused here, deliberately** — next step (normalize by daily ATR to separate real level-specific effects from generic noise, vs. proceed straight to the volume-spike cross-reference) needs a decision, not a guess. **Nothing live has been touched at any point in this entire thread** — every finding lives in `scratch/` scripts and `OPEN_DECISION`/`RESEARCH_CLAIM` rows.
8. **Process note, separate from the trading finding**: mid-thread, realized `ARCHITECTURE.md` already had a complete, accurate writeup of `touchQuality.js`/`calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` (added 2026-07-15) — the "rediscovery" wasn't a documentation gap, it was skipping the cheap check of the canonical doc before exploring. Added a new `CLAUDE.md` hard rule (`grep ARCHITECTURE.md` first) and updated `ARCHITECTURE.md` itself with tonight's real new additions (`flag_decision.mjs`/`OPEN_DECISION`) so the next session doesn't have to rediscover *those* either.

## ✅ 2026-07-17: no structural way to tell "live pipeline" from "one-off/abandoned" — **Resolved 2026-07-17 (same session)**: built `scripts/flag_decision.mjs` + session-start hook `OPEN_DECISIONS` section, see the standalone entry near the top of this file. The specific "backtest freshness+consumption report" idea below is itself now tracked as a pending `OPEN_DECISION` (slug `backtest_pipeline_freshness_consumption_report`) rather than left as unstructured prose — the tracking mechanism exists now, the report script itself is still unbuilt.

User question, prompted by a night of exactly this archaeology (PD2/2D_POC, TOD_ALPHA, `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND`, and yesterday's separate SSOT/dead-end audit hitting the same wall three more times): "shouldn't active backtests be in a different spot than one-off backtests?"

**Why this keeps happening**: every backtest — live-cron-scheduled-and-consumed or a one-off scratch run from months ago — writes to the same `performance_audit` table under a `signal_type` string, with no column marking whether it's still alive. The only way to know is manual reconstruction: find the writing script, check if it's in `scripts/` or `scripts/archive/`, check cron wiring, grep for live consumers. `playbook.js`'s pipeline-status dashboard is *supposed* to be the authoritative map of this — but it's hand-maintained metadata (a JS object someone wrote once, matching by name-guessing) and was itself found wrong in 3 places just yesterday (`TOD_ALPHA`/`DOW_TOD_ALPHA`, `LEVEL_FADE_DELTA`, `SYSTEM_BACKTEST` — see the SSOT/dead-end audit entry below). The dashboard meant to solve this problem has already drifted from reality once.

**Real axes worth separating** (not just "archived or not," which `scripts/archive/` already coarsely captures): (1) is the *script* still cron-scheduled, (2) is the *data* it produces actually read by live code. Tonight alone hit every combination: scheduled+consumed (`backtest_unified.js` → `2D_POC`/`PD2_VAH`/`PD2_VAL`, healthy pipeline, just structurally starved out of live firing by the single-primary-selection contest); scheduled+unconsumed would be pure waste (not confirmed to exist yet); unscheduled+consumed would be worse than either (stale data quietly feeding a live decision — not confirmed to exist currently, but structurally possible); unscheduled+unconsumed (`MIDPOINT_FADE_AUDIT`/`PD_IB_AUDIT`, deliberately and correctly frozen).

**Not built** — this is real infrastructure work (a proper metadata table or generated report, not another hand-maintained JS object that will itself go stale the same way `playbook.js`'s did), sized for its own session. Flagged here for a future decision rather than started unprompted tonight. Candidate scope for that session: (a) a query-derivable freshness+consumption report (cross-reference `performance_audit` signal_types against cron wiring and a live grep for consumers — largely already the methodology used ad-hoc tonight and yesterday, just needs to be made a standing, re-runnable check instead of one-off archaeology), (b) a decision on whether `scripts/` needs a third state beyond "active"/"archived" (e.g. "scheduled but currently unconsumed — candidate for revival or retirement") since that's the specific gap PD2/2D_POC exposed.

## ✅ 2026-07-17: `OPTIMAL_STOP.ev_per_trade` never matched the swept EV — found, fixed, and a standing invariant added — **Resolved 2026-07-17**

Surfaced while auditing a Gemini-dispatched "runner" mining task (below) — the audit process itself caught this, exactly what it's for. **CONFIRMED via `git log -p`, not a guess**: `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s `ev_per_trade` column had piped straight from `ROUND(AVG(actual_pnl))` (a separate raw-stats query) since the file's very first commit (`a04b2fd`, 2026-07-05) — **before the EV-sweep feature even existed**. At that point `optimal_stop`/`optimal_target` were themselves just plain percentiles (`p75_mae`/`p50_mfe`), so the raw average was a defensible, honest, standalone stat. When the sweep was added 4 days later (`de3e407`, "EV-sweep targets") to compute a genuinely optimal `optimal_stop`/`optimal_target` pair, the `ev_per_trade` write path was never rewired to report `swept.ev` — it silently kept reporting "what this setup's raw historical average P&L was" instead of "what EV the newly-computed optimal stop/target would produce," for every setup_type, every run, for 8 days before breaking and then 8 more days (plus 4 more commits touching this same file, including one earlier this same day) before anyone caught it.

**Directly reproduced live, not just theorized**: `CAM_R1_FADE_LONG` showed `stop=23/target=40/EV=-$14` in `performance_audit` and in the script's own console output. Exhaustively re-running the identical EV-sweep formula over the identical 116 trades at stop=23/target=40 gave **EV=+$13.92** — a $28/trade sign-flipping discrepancy on the same (stop, target, N). `AVG(actual_pnl)` for those 116 trades was -$13.77, confirming exactly what was actually being stored/displayed.

**Live impact — corrected after a fuller downstream audit than the initial pass**: `optimal_stop`/`optimal_target` (what `liveStats._opt[type]` actually feeds into real trade stop/target placement in `acd.js`) were never affected — verified both live gating sites (`acd.js` ~4981, ~5283) only read `.stop`/`.target`, never `.ev`, so **no live trade was ever placed with a wrong stop/target because of this**. The initial writeup overstated `ev_per_trade`'s display exposure — a full trace of every consumer found:
- **`/api/performance-audit/unified` (Unified Signal Table): NOT actually exposed.** The SQL does sort by `pa.ev_per_trade DESC`, but the endpoint's JS filter (`acd.js` ~7541-7548) only displays rows with a recognized `recommendation` value — `OPTIMAL_STOP` rows never set `recommendation` at all, so they're silently dropped before display, every time, regardless of the SQL ordering. The Unified Table never showed this value to a user.
- **`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` / `/api/acd/setup-status`: NOT exposed** — that endpoint is scoped strictly to `signal_type='SETUP_STATUS'`, a completely separate (and correctly-computed, unaffected) raw-average EV used for SUPPRESS/PROMOTE decisions.
- **`playbook.js`'s "Ask Claude" context stats: NOT exposed** — scoped to `SESSION_BIAS`/`CONTEXT_ANALYSIS`/`DAY_TYPE_ALPHA`/`PERMISSION_SLIP` only.
- **`QuickStatsModal.jsx` via `/api/performance-audit/search`: genuinely exposed**, via a fallback path (`server/routes/performanceAudit.js` ~70-72) that returns raw `OPTIMAL_STOP` rows verbatim (including `ev_per_trade`) when a searched setup_type has no matching `SETUP_STATUS` row. Checked live: currently **zero** setup_types are in that orphaned state, so this was dormant in practice, not actively misleading anyone today — but it's a real, reachable path and a latent risk for any future setup_type that gets `OPTIMAL_STOP`-calibrated before its `SETUP_STATUS` row exists.
- The clearest actual harm: **this session's own MFE-runner-mining analysis** (see the entry below) initially compared a correctly-computed uncapped-sweep EV against this buggy `ev_per_trade` value as its baseline — caught and corrected before being trusted, but it's the demonstrated case of this bug actually misleading an analysis, not a hypothetical.

**Fixed**: `update_optimal_stops.mjs` now writes `swept.ev` (falling back to the raw average only in the documented thin-tail-gate case, matching `optStop`/`optTarget`'s own fallback) — one-line change at the INSERT, plus the console log now prints both the corrected EV and the raw average side by side for visibility. Recalibrated all 71 setup_types; many EVs moved substantially and in both directions (e.g. `CAM_R1_FADE_LONG` -$14→+$13.92, `PD_VAL_FADE_LONG` -$8→+$21.67, `OPEN_TEST_DRIVE_LONG` -$95→-$55.51 — still negative, but the *real* negative, not the wrong one).

**Prevention, not just the one-off patch** (the user's explicit ask — "prevent this type of mistake"): `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs` now exports its own core formula (`computeEvAtStopTarget`, `sweepOptimalStopAndTarget`, `DEFAULT_DPP`) instead of keeping them script-private, and `scripts/test_invariants.mjs` gained a new check `[5]` that re-derives every `OPTIMAL_STOP` row's expected stop/target/EV via the REAL, imported sweep function (not a hand-copied formula — a second copy would itself be exactly the same bug class) and fails if the stored row doesn't exactly match either the fresh sweep result or the one documented fallback case. **Verified the check actually works, not just that it runs**: deliberately reintroduced the old buggy line, reran, confirmed 65 failures across affected setup_types; restored the fix, reran, confirmed a clean pass. Also caught and fixed a real gap in the check's own first draft mid-build: an initial looser version accepted "stored value matches the raw average" as always-valid (to avoid false-positiving on the legitimate thin-tail fallback case) — which a bug that *always* writes the raw average would trivially satisfy 100% of the time, completely defeating the check. Rewritten to fully re-derive the real expected sweep result via the actual production function and require an exact match, closing that loophole.

General lesson, now also in `CLAUDE.md`'s Hard rules: **when a script both computes a derived value and separately stores/displays a number labeled as describing it, that pairing needs a standing invariant check — a feature added on top of an existing field can silently break the field's own meaning without anyone touching the field's line, and a normal code review or diff read only catches "does this line look right," not "does this field's original meaning still hold after a later feature was bolted on three commits ago."**

`RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `ev_per_trade_not_swept_ev_bug` (CONFIRMED, updated to reflect the fix).

## 🆕 2026-07-17: MFE "runner" mining — real but small effect, doesn't rescue any losing setup (corrected after finding the bug above)

User asked whether trading small size with a wider (MFE-tail-aware) target could catch "runners" the current p75_mfe-capped target convention misses. Dispatched to Gemini first; its initial result compared the EV-sweep's uncapped-to-p95 EV against `performance_audit.ev_per_trade` directly — which, per the bug above, is invalid (comparing a correctly-swept number against a raw historical average, not the same methodology on both sides). **Corrected the comparison** (`scratch/gemini_mfe_runner_mining.mjs`, fixed after the audit) to run the identical EV-sweep formula twice per setup_type — once capped at `p75_mfe` (byte-identical to today's live convention, sanity-checked to reproduce the live `optimal_target` exactly) and once extended to `p95_mfe` — so the delta isolates only the effect of widening the target.

**Real result, 71 setup_types checked**: the effect is real but modest. Max delta: `BRACKET_BREAKOUT_LONG` +$11.56/trade (-$26.91→-$15.35 — still a net loser, just less bad). No currently-losing setup flips to profitable from target-widening alone **except one**: `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` (-$1.99→+$5.25 EV, N=75, top-5-days 6.7% of N — clean, worth a closer look). For already-profitable setups the boost is +$1-2/trade (`OPEN_DRIVE_LONG`, `PD_VAL_FADE_SHORT`, `WR1_FADE_SHORT`, `FLOOR_PIVOT_FADE_SHORT`, `WEEKLY_VWAP_FADE_LONG`). **Bottom line: the existing p75_mfe target cap is not leaving large EV on the table — it was a reasonable design choice, not a costly one.** `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `mfe_runner_target_widening_mining` (PROVISIONAL). The `ev_per_trade` bug above is now fixed and recalibrated — `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT`'s numbers here already came from the apples-to-apples sweep-vs-sweep comparison (not the buggy field), so they don't need re-deriving, but the setup's live `OPTIMAL_STOP` row now correctly shows `ev_per_trade=$-1.99` (matches `cappedEV` above) instead of a stale/wrong value. Not actioned — `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE_SHORT` is still the only candidate worth a follow-up look, no longer blocked on anything.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: finer DLL sweep confirms $75/day remains the local optimum; growth-sizing doesn't rescue No-DLL

Direct follow-on to the No-DLL Monte Carlo finding above. Extended sweep across $50-$350 DLL (5000 MC paths each, same eligible trade stream): **$75 DLL remains the standout** — median $5,792, mean $5,448, 20.5% blown — narrowly ahead of $50 (median $5,438, 20.4% blown) and $90 (median $5,498, 22.6% blown). Beyond ~$110 both blown% and median degrade monotonically ($150 DLL: 45.1% blown, median only $926; $350 DLL: 70.8% blown, median -$2,033). Separately: layering the >$3k-profit growth-sizing rule on top of **No DLL** does not rescue it (95.3% blown — slightly *worse* than plain No-DLL's 94.3%, since a larger position size once growth-sizing kicks in occasionally accelerates the blow before the profit threshold is reached). `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `prop_2k_dll_finegrid_sweep` (PROVISIONAL). Not actioned — same as the parent thread, changing the live DLL guardrail is the user's call.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: tested "no DLL, flat 1 MNQ" from $2,000 — it's the worst config tested, not the best

Direct follow-on to the walk-forward/DLL-sweep thread below (same session). User asked what max-gain looks like with flat 1 MNQ contract, no daily loss limit at all, starting from a $2,000 account with only the account's own $2,000 total-drawdown as the stop. Built `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_nodll.mjs` (deterministic, same point-in-time eligibility filter as the existing walk-forward) and extended `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_montecarlo.mjs` with a matching No-DLL config (`dailyDll: Infinity`) using the same cached day-plans/bootstrap methodology as the rest of that thread.

**Result: removing the DLL makes it worse, not better.**
- **Deterministic real sequence** (2025-07-16→2026-07-16): No-DLL blows the account on **day 5** (2025-07-22 — a trend day where nearly every long-side fade setup lost together, one -$928 day stacked on prior losing days), final -$2,157. The *same trade stream* with a $400 DLL survives a week longer before blowing at -$2,046 — barely better, but better.
- **Monte Carlo** (5000 block-bootstrap-by-day paths, same eligible trade stream as the earlier sweep): **No-DLL blows in 94.3% of path orderings**, median final -$2,058 — essentially "always loses the account." For comparison: $400 DLL blows 83.4% (median -$2,046), $75 DLL blows only 20.1% (median **+$5,829**, mean +$5,419).

**Why**: at a $2,000 account, the DLL isn't capping upside — one correlated bad day (several fade setups losing together on the same trend day) is large enough on its own to end a thin account. The DLL's job is keeping the account alive long enough to reach the roster's real positive-EV stretches, not limiting how much it can make. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `prop_2k_nodll_vs_dll_montecarlo` (PROVISIONAL — real methodology, matches the existing sweep's rigor, not yet independently re-derived a second way).

**Not actioned**: same as the sweep below — whether to actually change the live DLL guardrail (`server/routes/dll.js`) toward the $75-100 range for a $2,000 account is a real risk decision for the user, not something to change unilaterally. This finding narrows the recommendation further: "no DLL" is now a specifically tested and ruled-out option, not just an untested one.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: standing data-sanity audit built (item 4 of the reflection pass) — institutionalizes today's manual bug-catching

Follow-on to the day's real findings (MAE/MFE ES-contamination, `DEFAULT_DPP=5`) — both were caught by manually re-deriving numbers from scratch, which shouldn't require another multi-hour session for the next occurrence. Built `scripts/data_sanity_audit.mjs`, wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh`:
1. **MAE/MFE outliers** — self-calibrating (flags anything >10x the table's own p95), not a hardcoded point threshold.
2. **`$/pt` uniformity** — implied `$/pt` per setup_type (same method used to catch `DEFAULT_DPP=5`) compared against `LIVE_INSTRUMENT.dollarsPerPoint` (±10% tolerance).
3. **`price_bars_primary` symbol check** — flags any symbol beyond the known set (`NQ`). Would have caught the ES-contamination bug immediately, before any downstream MAE/MFE computation touched it.
4. **`performance_audit` bounds sanity** — win_rate/sample_size can't be genuinely impossible values.

**Found something building check 4**: `performance_audit.win_rate` is not uniformly scaled — `RESEARCH_CLAIM` and `LEVEL_FADE_AUDIT` store it as 0-100 percentage, everything else as 0-1 decimal, in the same column. Real inconsistency (anyone averaging `win_rate` across signal_types without normalizing gets nonsense), but out of scope for this check to fix — flagged as informational output, not treated as an anomaly (the check itself was initially too strict and produced false positives on this before being corrected).

**Current state**: exit code 1, one standing flag — the `ES` symbol data is still physically present in `price_bars_primary` (2023-11-15 to 2023-12-15). The MAE/MFE fix corrected the *queries* that read it (added `symbol='NQ'` filters), not the underlying data — deliberately left in place rather than deleted, since it's not itself wrong data, just a landmine for any *future* query over that date range that forgets the filter. This is expected, not a regression — the check exists specifically to keep reminding future sessions of this risk.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: closed-loop learning gap fixed — `origin_status` column, historical reconstruction, `expireStaleSetups()` fix, `SHADOW_VALIDATION` hook section

Full implementation of Opus Audit #3's recommendation (`scratch/opus_audit_3_results.md`, dispatched after investigating "can we tell if the system is learning" surfaced that suppression decisions had no way to be validated against their own forward outcomes).

**What was actually built** (all independently verified before/after — see the audit-checking pass earlier this session that confirmed every Opus number against direct DB/code queries, zero discrepancies):
1. **Schema**: `active_setups.origin_status VARCHAR(12)` — set once at insert, never referenced by any resolution UPDATE. Values: `ACTIVE`, `SHADOW`, `BACKFILL`, `UNKNOWN`.
2. **All 7 INSERT sites wired** (`server/routes/acd.js:486, 4966, 5203, 5956, 6082, 6160`, `server/services/minuteBarSignalDetector.js:148`) — each populates `origin_status` with the same value it already computes for `status`.
3. **Historical backfill** (dispatched to Gemini for the reconstruction query, audited and independently verified before running for real against the live DB): `BACKFILL` for 6,379 synthetic rows (never fired live — `resolution_method IN ('BACKFILL','EARLY_TOUCH_BACKFILL')`), reconstructed via point-in-time `SETUP_STATUS` lookup (`run_date <= trade_date`, no lookahead) for 110 rows dated `>=2026-07-09` (44 `SHADOW`, 66 `ACTIVE`), `UNKNOWN` for the remaining 1,499 rows dated before 2026-07-09 — no `SETUP_STATUS` snapshot exists that far back to reconstruct against, labeled honestly rather than guessed. All 7,988 rows classified, zero overlap, zero left behind (verified via `SELECT origin_status, COUNT(*) GROUP BY 1` before/after).
4. **`expireStaleSetups()` fixed** (`server/routes/acd.js`) — previously ran `DELETE FROM active_setups WHERE status='SHADOW' AND trade_date < $1` unconditionally on ANY prior-day SHADOW row, including ones with real entry/stop/target that simply hadn't been given a chance to resolve — a second, silent data-destruction point alongside the resolution-UPDATE overwrite. Fixed: SHADOW rows with real levels now go through the same expire-to-`EXPIRED` path as ACTIVE (preserving `origin_status`); only rows with no `expires_at` at all (the CASCADE_BREAKER / suppressed-near-level-audit inserts, which log a suppressed touch as evidence with no entry/stop/target to resolve against) get a terminal mark (`resolution='NO_EXPIRY_SET'`) instead of physical deletion.
5. **`SHADOW_VALIDATION` session-start hook section** — for every currently-`SUPPRESS`/`THIN_N` setup_type, reports what its real forward `origin_status='SHADOW'` trades did since the decision; flags N≥20 disagreements (positive forward EV despite suppression) for re-examination, confirms N≥20 agreements. Currently reports "no forward data yet" honestly — tracking only starts today, this isn't a result to trust yet, it's a mechanism that will start producing real signal within days. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `shadow_suppression_validation` (PROVISIONAL, N=0, exists to be re-checked as data accumulates).
6. `server/schema.sql` regenerated (138 tables/views — the `+1` from the last regen is the `active_setups_maemfe_backup_20260717` backup table, not a bug).

**Verified before considering done**: no UTC/timezone risk (the backfill only ever compared `active_setups.trade_date`/`performance_audit.run_date`, both pure `DATE` type, never touching the `timestamp without time zone` columns that would be vulnerable to the naive-timestamp bug documented elsewhere in this file); all 7 INSERT sites confirmed via grep (none missed, no duplicates); server restart clean; `test_invariants.mjs` clean (same 1 pre-existing unrelated warning); no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.

**Not done, explicitly deferred (not urgent, not silently dropped)**:
- ~~**`pattern_discoveries` has the identical defect**~~ — **Fixed 2026-07-17.** Added `win_rate_at_first_seen`/`sample_size_at_first_seen`/`net_pnl_at_first_seen` (set once at INSERT, never touched by the nightly re-scan UPDATE) and `degraded_on` (set once on the ACTIVE→DEGRADED flip) to `pattern_discoveries`. Backfilled `degraded_on = last_updated` for the 286 pre-existing DEGRADED rows (accurate, not a guess — that UPDATE only ever fires on rows still in the ACTIVE set, so `last_updated` genuinely reflects the degrade date). `_at_first_seen` columns are honestly `NULL` for all 313 pre-existing rows — unrecoverable, only the current mutated snapshot ever existed for them, same "don't guess, label honestly" precedent as `active_setups.origin_status='UNKNOWN'`. Verified via a direct insert-and-check test (not just trusting a real mining run, which happened to only hit the "strengthen an existing pattern" branch and correctly left `_at_first_seen` untouched — that's correct behavior, not a bug, confirmed by testing the INSERT branch in isolation).
- ~~**`dynamic_edges_mining`** has the same shape~~ — **Fixed 2026-07-17, and it was worse than first assessed**: `scripts/edge_miner.mjs` didn't just overwrite rows in place, it ran `DELETE FROM dynamic_edges_mining` before every weekly run, unconditionally destroying **all** prior history, not just the latest scan's stats. Fixed: added `run_date` to the table, changed the unique constraint from `(setup_type, dimension, segment)` to `(setup_type, dimension, segment, run_date)`, removed the `DELETE`, script now appends (`ON CONFLICT` handles a same-day re-run). **Also had to fix the live read path** (`server/routes/antigravityEdges.js` ~line 485) — it queried the whole table with no date filter, which only worked because the table used to hold exactly one row per combo; now scoped to `DISTINCT ON (setup_type, dimension, segment) ... ORDER BY run_date DESC` so a stale prior week's row doesn't double up with the current one. Verified end-to-end: ran `edge_miner.mjs` for real, confirmed the original 81 rows (`run_date=2026-07-13`) survived alongside 209 new rows (`run_date=2026-07-17`), and confirmed the live query's dedup logic returns zero duplicate combos.
- **One-off historical `repair_*.mjs` scripts** (`repair_top8_window_mismatch.mjs` etc.) insert `resolution_method='BACKFILL'` rows directly — none are in any active cron, so not retroactively fixed, but if any of them (or a similarly-shaped future repair script) ever runs again, it should also set `origin_status='BACKFILL'` at insert time or it'll silently reintroduce a NULL-origin_status row needing yet another one-time backfill.
- **Live-fire verification**: no real live `SUPPRESS`/`THIN_N` type happened to touch a level during this session's testing window to confirm a fresh SHADOW insert populates `origin_status` correctly end-to-end (the code path was read and verified, restart was clean, but an actual live fire wasn't observed). Worth a spot-check next session during active market hours.
- **A genuine edge case found while re-verifying every reference to the new column (user explicitly asked for this check, good catch)**: `scripts/backtest_setup_status.mjs` (lines ~274-291) has its own separate `UPDATE active_setups SET status='SHADOW'|'ACTIVE'` for still-**open** (`resolution IS NULL`) rows when a setup_type gets newly suppressed/promoted mid-flight, while a trade is still unresolved — a code path outside `acd.js`/`minuteBarSignalDetector.js` that Opus Audit #3 never covered (its INSERT-site/UPDATE-site audit was scoped to the live poller, not the weekly calibration script's own retroactive status flip). Confirmed via live data: 2 of 7,988 rows have `suppression_reason='PERFORMANCE_BELOW_THRESHOLD'` (this UPDATE's own marker) — one shows `status='RESOLVED'` but `origin_status='ACTIVE'`, i.e. it fired ACTIVE, got mid-flight-suppressed before resolving, and `origin_status` correctly preserved the *original* firing decision rather than tracking the later flip. **This is the intended, deliberate behavior of "immutable at insert, forever"** — `origin_status` answers "was this trade visible to the user when it fired," not "was this setup_type ever suppressed at any point during this trade's open lifetime." Extremely rare in practice (2 rows historically) so not worth re-engineering, but noting it explicitly rather than letting the "never touched by any resolution UPDATE" claim in `ARCHITECTURE.md` overstate itself — there IS a code path that changes `status` without touching `origin_status`; that's correct by design, not an oversight, and now it's on the record as a deliberate choice instead of an unexamined one.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: 4th occurrence of the wrong-$/pt-constant bug — `update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s `DEFAULT_DPP=5` fallback, defended by a false "verified" comment

User asked me to re-run `update_optimal_stops.mjs` to pick up the corrected MAE/MFE percentiles (a small, low-risk follow-up) — reading the file before running it surfaced something much bigger. `DEFAULT_DPP = 5` (the dollars-per-point fallback used when a setup_type has <20 STOP_HIT or <20 TARGET_HIT trades, in the EV-sweep that picks each setup's stop/target) was defended by a comment claiming: *"real $/pt is cleanly bimodal (~$5 for the level-fade family, ~$2 for IB_BULLISH/BEARISH/OPEN_DRIVE/C_STANDALONE/etc.)... Independently verified via Gemini mining pass, cross-checked by Claude against direct SQL before trusting."* That claim is false. Directly re-verified against every setup_type with N≥20 STOP_HIT trades (54 types, `ABS(actual_pnl)/ABS(entry_zone_low-stop_level)`): **every single one resolves to $2.01-$2.06/pt** — MNQ's real $2/pt plus the $1 flat commission spread across each trade's point distance. No bimodal split, no level-fade exception, anywhere.

This is the **4th independent occurrence** of the wrong-$/pt-constant bug class already documented in `server/config/instruments.js`'s own header (backfill_level_fades.js → 6 copied scripts; a frontend modal; TRT_LONG's trade-brief text) — and the most dangerous variant so far, because it came with a comment claiming due diligence had already been done. **25 currently-live setup_types had an `OPTIMAL_STOP` row computed using the wrong 2.5x-inflated fallback** on at least one side before this fix.

Fixed: `DEFAULT_DPP` now imports `LIVE_INSTRUMENT.dollarsPerPoint` from `server/config/instruments.js` instead of a 4th redeclared literal; corrected all 3 misleading comments in the file; re-ran full recalibration (71 rows upserted into `performance_audit` `OPTIMAL_STOP`). Verified via `test_invariants.mjs` (clean, same 1 pre-existing unrelated warning) and a clean server restart. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `optimal_stop_dpp5_fallback_bug_fixed` (CONFIRMED). `server/config/instruments.js`'s header updated to note this as the 4th occurrence.

**Process lesson, worth restating**: a comment claiming "independently verified... cross-checked before trusting" is not itself verification — it's a claim, and this session found it was wrong the moment someone actually re-ran the check. Don't let a confident-sounding audit trail in a comment substitute for redoing the check when a downstream consumer (in this case, literally running the script) depends on it being right.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: real MAE/MFE data-corruption bug found and fixed (ES symbol contamination), plus a Gemini volume-spike bug caught before it entered the record

User asked to check MAE/MFE on the walk-forward's worst-drag setups ("something needs to be fixed"). Found two real bugs in one pass:

**1. MAE/MFE ES-symbol contamination — confirmed and fixed.** `scripts/backfill_mae_mfe.mjs` and `server/services/maeMfeReplay.js`'s `computeMaeMfe()` (imported live by `server/routes/acd.js`) both fetch `price_bars_primary` bars for MAE/MFE replay with **no `symbol` filter**. `price_bars_primary` turns out to contain an `ES` (S&P 500 futures) batch spanning exactly 2023-11-15 to 2023-12-15 (29,280 rows) alongside the primary `NQ` series. Any `active_setups` row that fired in that window had its replay silently comparing NQ price levels (~16,000) against ES bars (~4,500-4,700), producing physically impossible "11,000+ point" excursions. Root-caused via direct instrumentation (not guessed) — traced a specific corrupted row (id=134) to a phantom `low=4610.5` bar, then found that value lives on a real `ES`-symbol row, not NQ. Affected 430 rows (5.4% of all MAE/MFE-populated rows) across 76 setup_types. Fixed: added `symbol='NQ'` to both queries, backed up the corrupted rows (`active_setups_maemfe_backup_20260717`), nulled them, re-ran the already-idempotent NULL-only backfill script. Verified 0 impossible values remain; averages and medians now agree (e.g. `C_STANDALONE_DOWN` avg_mfe went from a bogus 801.6 to a sane 79.6, matching its own median of 50.8). Percentile-based stats (`p75_mae`/`p50_mfe`, what `OPTIMAL_STOP` actually reads) were largely already robust to this since it only touched a minority of most setups' own N — but any code reading a raw average was meaningfully wrong. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `mae_mfe_es_symbol_contamination_bug_fixed` (CONFIRMED).
- **Not yet checked**: `scripts/pilot_touch_quality.mjs`, `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs`, `scripts/pilot_touch_quality_orderflow.mjs` each have their own independent `price_bars_primary` bar-fetch queries (don't go through the now-fixed `computeMaeMfe`) that appear to have the same missing-symbol-filter pattern on inspection (grepped, no `symbol=` near any of their `FROM price_bars_primary` clauses). Not confirmed exploited — no corrupted output traced to them yet — but same latent risk. Worth a follow-up pass.
- **Separately, still open**: the 5 biggest walk-forward drag setups (`C_STANDALONE_DOWN`, `CAM_S1_FADE_LONG`, `OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_OR_MID_FADE_LONG`) had **zero `OPTIMAL_STOP` rows** at all (not just stale ones) as of this check — meaning whatever stop/target they actually resolved against wasn't the data-derived p75-MAE/p50-MFE value the rest of the system uses. Not yet determined whether this is because they're currently `SUPPRESS`ed (and the calibration script skips suppressed types) or a genuine coverage gap — needs a direct look at `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s selection criteria before concluding either way.

**2. Gemini's volume-spike backtest (from the earlier dispatch this session) had a real bug — caught before being trusted.** Initial result was WR=0.84% (0.00% longs / 1.58% shorts) — implausibly low, flagged per the standing "0%/100%-ish WR needs a methodology check" rule. Root cause: `node-postgres` returns NUMERIC columns as JS strings; the script's stop/target check used `entryPrice + targetPts` (or `+ stopPts`) for one side of each direction's check — in JS, `string + number` is string concatenation, not addition, so half of every stop/target comparison was silently comparing garbage lexicographic strings instead of real prices. Fixed with `parseFloat()` at the DB boundary in `scratch/backtest_volume_spike.mjs`, re-ran: corrected result is WR=36.50% (N=4,663), EV/trade=-$0.32, roughly breakeven — not a tradeable continuation signal as specified, but nowhere near the originally-reported "emphatically un-tradeable" 0.84%. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `volume_spike_entry_signal_gemini_bug_corrected`.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: root-caused the walk-forward backtest's -$2,725 loss, then found the REAL account-viability answer: it's DLL sizing relative to account, not setup quality

Direct follow-on to the walk-forward backtest above. User pushed back hard on the -$2,725 headline ("why are these all losing, my older prop tests would have made money") — root-caused it properly rather than accepting the number at face value.

**Root cause of the -$2,725 (attribution, not guesswork):** split the loss by source. -$12,166.76 came from setup_types that are correctly `SUPPRESS`ed *as of today* but were still eligible earlier in the walk-forward window before enough losing evidence accumulated to trigger suppression (inherent "detection lag" cost of any N≥20-floor adaptive system — it has to lose real money to prove a setup is bad before it can react). -$337.50 came from the `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` DAY_TYPE_MANAGED carve-out, which is never blanket-suppressed by design and is still live today at negative blended EV (same fragile state the `DTM_WATCH` session-start hook already flags every session). **Everything currently eligible today, non-carve-out, made +$9,779.19 over the same window** — the live roster's edge is real; the loss is 100%+ explained by these two specific, identifiable drags, not by stop/target calibration (which was never the issue — MAE/MFE-derived stops/targets control how a fired trade exits, not whether it should have fired at all).

**Then found the actual, bigger problem: account-size-to-DLL ratio, not setup quality.** User specified a $2,000 account with $400/day DLL (20% of capital/day) plus a rule to switch from flat 1 MNQ to 1%-of-equity risk sizing once cumulative profit crosses $3,000. Built `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward.mjs` (two regimes) then `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_sweep.mjs` (parameter grid) on the same point-in-time-eligible trade stream:
- **$400 DLL blows the $2,000 account in 9-10 trading days** — 5 of the first 10 days hit the $400 DLL (day P&L swings of -$400 to -$1,387 unrestricted), which alone is ~$2,000. The account never reaches the $3k-profit growth-sizing threshold, so Regime A and Regime B were identical (moot) at this DLL.
- Sweep A (DLL fixed $400, balance varied): survival threshold is between $5,000 (still blows, 9 months in) and $7,500 (survives, converges to the same -$2,725 as the unconstrained run).
- Sweep B (balance fixed $2,000, DLL varied): every DLL below $400 survives and is profitable — $250→+$3,355, $150→+$3,491, $100→+$5,535, **$75→+$6,308 (best flat-sizing result)**, $50→+$5,917 (too tight, starts cutting off good days).
- Adding the user's growth-sizing rule on top of $75 DLL: **+$7,328** (vs $6,308 flat), switches to 1%-equity sizing 2025-08-27, never blown, max DD $3,978.

**Then Monte Carlo-validated it (user explicitly asked, since a single deterministic path doesn't capture sequence-of-returns risk)** — `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_montecarlo.mjs`, 5000 block-bootstrap-by-day paths per config, following the same methodology as `scripts/monte_carlo_optimizer.js`/`docs/MONTE_CARLO_METHODOLOGY.md`:
- **$400 DLL / $2,000: blows in 82.9% of random path orderings, median outcome -$2,045** — confirms the real historical blow-up was NOT bad luck, it's the typical outcome at that DLL/balance ratio.
- **$75 DLL / $2,000 + growth-sizing: blows in 20.9% of paths, median +$6,428, mean +$6,189** — growth-sizing adds real value across the full distribution (median +$555, p95 +$2,372 vs flat), not just the one historical path.
- $100 DLL performed WORSE under MC (27.5% blow) than $75 DLL despite looking fine on the single historical path — real confirmation that a single backtest path is not sufficient for DLL selection; the MC step mattered.
- Non-blown paths at $75 DLL: median max drawdown $2,520-2,574 against median profit ~$6,000 (~2.3:1 ratio) — better risk-adjusted than the single-path $3,978-DD-for-$7,328-profit read suggested, but the ~21% chance of losing the whole $2,000 account is the number to weigh, not the median.

Both findings recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM`s (`prop_2k_account_dll_montecarlo_sweep`, plus the earlier `prop_1yr_walkforward_400dll_backtest`), both `PROVISIONAL`. **Not yet actioned**: (1) whether to actually reduce the live DLL guardrail (`server/routes/dll.js`) to something in the $75-100 range for small accounts — this is a real risk-management decision, not a Claude call to make unilaterally; (2) the still-open question of whether `C_STANDALONE_DOWN`/`CAM_S1_FADE_LONG`/`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`/etc. (the bottom contributors from the original walk-forward) should be reassessed for tighter/faster SUPPRESS given how much the "detection lag" cost the account; (3) user asked whether the tens-of-thousands-dollar figure they recalled from "the last prop run" exists anywhere findable — searched `performance_audit` and `scratch/`, found no matching record; only documented prior number is the older $2K→$6,695 note (different roster/methodology, flagged superseded elsewhere in this file) — needs the user to point to the specific run if it's meant to be reconciled.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: all-time pattern tracker built + first 1-year walk-forward prop backtest run — roster is net-negative under a $400 DLL despite both guardrails working

**All-time pattern discovery tracker (user's explicit ask — "this pattern tool is literally the core of what I'm trying to do as far as learning and self-management... we might need longer term to anticipate less frequent large moves"):** `mineLevelFades()` (`server/services/patternScannerService.js`) now takes `{windowDays, windowType, keyPrefix}` params. Default behavior (nightly, `server/index.js`) is byte-identical to before. New `scripts/mine_level_fades_alltime.mjs` runs the same logic over full price history (`windowDays: null`, `windowType: 'ALL_TIME'`, `keyPrefix: 'ALLTIME:'`), wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh`. `pattern_discoveries` got a new `window_type` column so the two scans have fully independent ACTIVE/DEGRADED lifecycles (a rolling-90-day run can never degrade an all-time discovery or vice versa — see the scoped `WHERE status='ACTIVE' AND window_type=$1` DEGRADE query). First run (2026-07-17): 417 RTH trading days, 194s, 15 new discoveries the rolling scan structurally could never have surfaced — e.g. `IB_HIGH×Fri×15:00` (74% WR, N=23), `PD_VAL×Tue×11:00` (67% WR, N=27). `learningDigestService.js` needed no changes — its "new pattern" query is already keyed on `first_seen = CURRENT_DATE`, which correctly treats these as genuinely new regardless of which scan wrote them.

**1-year walk-forward prop backtest, $400/day DLL (user's explicit ask):** dispatched to Gemini with a detailed no-lookahead spec (point-in-time setup eligibility recomputed per day using ONLY `trade_date < D` data, same SUPPRESS bar as `backtest_setup_status.mjs`: N≥20 & EV<-$5/tr, DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL types gated per-day-type). **Gemini's response file (`scratch/antigravity_response.md`) came back truncated** — the actual numbers (headline table, most of the monthly breakdown) were overwritten by Gemini's own wrapper narration, leaving only the tail of the real report. The underlying script (`scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward.mjs`) existed and had genuinely run, so rather than burn a correction round-trip on a file-write glitch, audited the script directly and found **two real methodology bugs**: (1) it capped `size_multiplier` at `min(mult, 1.0)`, silently discarding any size-up trade (up to 1.5x live) — verified this is currently a no-op (DB check: `MAX(size_multiplier)=1.000` across all 78 rows, zero >1.0, because the size-up overwrite bug wasn't fixed until earlier this same session — no resolved trade has reflected a real size-up value yet) but left uncorrected it would silently bias future re-runs pessimistic; fixed to apply the real multiplier uncapped. (2) it filtered to `resolution IN ('TARGET_HIT','STOP_HIT')`, excluding 331 real `TIME_EXPIRED` rows that have legitimate settled `actual_pnl` — fixed to include all three. Fixed and re-ran directly (`node scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward.mjs`, writes to `scratch/backtest_prop_1yr_walkforward_RESULTS.md` now, not the shared Gemini response file).

**Result (window 2025-07-16 to 2026-07-16, 244 trading days):**
| Layer | Total P&L | N | WR |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fully unrestricted (no DLL, no eligibility filter) | -$21,776.38 | 4,498 | 59.8% |
| + $400 DLL only | -$5,780.44 | 3,018 | 61.7% |
| + $400 DLL + point-in-time eligibility filter (the real walk-forward number) | **-$2,725.07** | 1,990 | 60.2% |

Max drawdown $8,716.12; 68 of 244 days (28%) hit the DLL lockout. Both guardrails independently verified to reduce losses (DLL alone: -73%; eligibility filter on top of DLL: another -53%), and they compound to an 87% total reduction vs. fully unrestricted — but **the current live setup roster, traded exactly as the system would have actually gated it day-by-day, is still net-negative over the trailing year even with both protections active.** Top contributor `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` (+$1,679, N=118), bottom `C_STANDALONE_DOWN` (-$2,601, N=67) — both checked clean for day-clustering (top-5-day % <20%), not single-day artifacts. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `prop_1yr_walkforward_400dll_backtest` (status `PROVISIONAL` — real methodology, single run, not yet independently re-derived a second way). **Not yet acted on** — this is a finding to react to, not something fixed this session; worth a dedicated look at whether the bottom contributors (`C_STANDALONE_DOWN`, `CAM_S1_FADE_LONG`, `OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_OR_MID_FADE_LONG`) should be reassessed for SUPPRESS given this is a live-roster-matching, not retroactively-fitted, view of their actual contribution.

## 🆕 2026-07-17: Sierra Chart live orderflow, volume-spike entries/exits, and sim auto-trading latency — three parked ideas, none investigated yet

User raised three related-but-distinct ideas in one message. None have been researched or scoped — recorded here so they aren't lost, per this file's own purpose.

1. **Live orderflow access while in a trade, to inform stay-longer / add-to-position decisions.** Question: is it possible to give Claude a live read of Sierra Chart order-flow (bid/ask imbalance, tape, DOM) once a position is open, so trade management (hold longer / scale in) could react to real-time flow rather than only the pre-entry setup logic this system already has? Unresearched — needs (a) a check of what Sierra Chart's ACSIL/DTC or export mechanisms actually expose in real time (per the standing "do not guess on Sierra Chart specifics — fetch docs or ask" hard rule, not to be answered from assumption), and (b) a design decision on whether "help stay in a trade longer" means an advisory alert (info surfaced to the user, who still acts manually) or something closer to automated management — the two have very different build and risk profiles and haven't been distinguished yet.
2. **Large standard-deviation volume-spike bars as entry or exit signals.** Idea: detect bars where volume is N-σ above its rolling mean (matches this codebase's own no-static-thresholds convention — the σ cutoff itself would need to be data-derived, not a hand-picked spike size) and test whether they have predictive value as entries or exits, independent of the existing level-fade/day-type signal families. **Needs a real backtest before anything else** — this is squarely a mining/analysis task (dispatch to Gemini per convention), not something to build live-facing UI for first. No work done yet.
3. **Sim auto-trading test in Sierra Chart, with an explicit latency concern.** User wants to actually test automated order placement in Sierra Chart *sim* mode, but is not yet convinced the round-trip — server-side signal firing → the alert actually appearing on the user's screen — is fast enough for live-market timing, and worries this could produce misleadingly-clean "sim fills" that wouldn't hold up against real market speed. This needs an actual latency measurement (timestamp at signal computation vs. timestamp at UI/alert delivery, under real market conditions, not assumed) before any auto-trading test is meaningful — matches this codebase's own "verify actual performance, don't just assume it's fast" convention already applied to page-load/query work. Not started.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (continued): full app audit — single-source-of-truth, dead-ends, self-recalibration — plus a real branch-divergence finding

User asked for three standing audits after the day's IB_BULLISH/$-per-pt incidents: (1) does shared data come from one source of truth throughout the app, (2) are there dead-ends (built-but-unreachable code), (3) is the system actually self-recalibrating and visibly so. Also surfaced explicit standing rules mid-session (persisted to memory `feedback_single_source_of_truth_no_deadends.md`): check git history before changing a "finding" (understand why it's there, make a non-breaking change, don't just revert); when something needs fixing, fix it (search first to confirm nothing breaks) rather than only reporting it.

**Real, standalone finding surfaced while auditing: `main` is 55 commits behind `perf/react-lazy-splitting`.** Two Explore agents were dispatched with `isolation: worktree`; both defaulted to branching from `main` (not the current branch), and `main` turned out to be at commit `9297a6b` — 55 commits behind HEAD, predating essentially this entire multi-day session's work (IB_BULLISH fix, timezone repair, dedup pass, RESEARCH_CLAIM ledger, everything from today). This caused real staleness in both agents' reports (see below) but is itself worth flagging: weeks of work are sitting on a feature branch that's never been merged back. **Not merged — needs an explicit user decision**, not something to do unilaterally given the size of the diff and that this session never verified `main`'s own deploy/branch-protection assumptions.

### Audit 1: Single source of truth

Dispatched to an Explore agent; verified every claim directly against current HEAD before acting (the agent's worktree was stale — see above — so several of its findings were already-fixed-this-session bugs re-reported; discarded those, verified the rest fresh).

**Confirmed real and fixed:**
- `server/services/setupBacktestService.js` hardcoded `$5/point, $5 commission` (comment even said "NQ full contract = $5/point" — wrong twice over, real NQ is $20/pt and this journal trades MNQ at $2/pt). Fixed via the shared `LIVE_INSTRUMENT` constant (same fix pattern as the `$/pt` bug found earlier the same session). Feeds `setup_outcome_backtest` via `POST /api/setups/backtest/run`, which currently has no frontend caller (curl/admin-reachable only) — real bug, low current blast radius.

**Checked and confirmed FALSE POSITIVE (matters for trusting future audits: don't act on an agent finding without tracing it):** the agent flagged `TRT_LONG`'s stop distance disagreeing 3-4x between `server/routes/setups.js`'s `FALLBACK_MEDIANS` (33pt) and `acd.js`'s `PROFILES` coaching text ("~117pt"). Traced `FALLBACK_MEDIANS` to its actual consumer (`setup_move_stats.avg_move_30d/90d/alltime`) — it's a **target/MFE-move-distance fallback**, not a stop-loss distance at all. Two genuinely different metrics that only *look* like the same fact because both are point-distances for the same setup type.

**Confirmed real, not yet fixed (SEVERITY 2 — duplicated, values currently agree, same latent-risk shape as today's bugs but not currently wrong):**
- `inferDirection`/`isLong` heuristic reimplemented independently in 7+ places (`acd.js` has two different versions in the *same file*; `setups.js`, `setupBacktestService.js`, `patternMemoryUpdate.js`, `morningBrief.js` (missing `_UP`, narrower than the others), `maeMfeReplay.js`, `App.jsx` all have their own copies).
- Setup-type label/description dictionaries redefined 4x independently in `App.jsx` alone (`SETUP_DISPLAY_LABELS`, `SETUP_EVENT_DESCRIPTIONS`, `PLAYBOOK_SETUPS`, an inline `edgeCtx` object) — no shared `setupDisplay.js` constants file exists yet for the frontend (the backend has `server/config/setupTypes.js`).
- `API_URL = '/api'` redeclared as a local const in 23 separate frontend files (one, `App.jsx:8467`, diverges in *behavior* — can resolve to `window.API_URL` if ever set, unlike the other 22).
- NL30 `±9` threshold and value-area `70%` hardcoded independently in ~10 files each (values agree everywhere currently) — also a "No static thresholds" hard-rule angle worth folding into the same fix.
- 570/630/960 RTH/IB session-minute boundaries redeclared as named constants in 4 different service files (`dayTypeReassessmentService.js`, `developingValueService.js`, `caseEngine.js`, `volatilityRegimeService.js`), plus 150+ raw magic-number occurrences across the codebase. All currently consistent, zero shared source.
- Monte Carlo `commission: 0.50` duplicated within the same file (`monteCarloService.js`, two call sites) — worth checking whether MC sims are silently 2x too optimistic on commission vs. the live $1 round-trip value (not verified this session).
- `server/db.js` vs. `sierra-chart-integration/server/db/pool.js` have different DB credential fallback defaults (`postgres`/no-fallback-password vs. `trader`/hardcoded `trader123`) — low risk since `sierra-chart-integration/` appears dormant (no systemd service, nothing modified since May 22).
- One real (not just duplicated-but-agreeing) inconsistency found and not yet fixed: `acd.js`'s `isRTH` check at line ~2312 only verifies "before/at 4:00pm," missing the 9:30am start-boundary check that the standard `570-960` convention (used correctly elsewhere in the same file, e.g. line ~7743) has.

**Not started**: building the actual shared-source files this all points to (`inferDirection` reused as `server/config/setupTypes.js` already exports for setup_type direction but frontend/backend both still have independent reimplementations; a frontend `setupDisplay.js`; a `src/constants/api.js`; a `server/config/session.js` for the minute boundaries). This is real, valuable follow-up work, sized for its own session rather than bolted onto an already-long one.

### Audit 2: Dead-ends

Dispatched to a second Explore agent (same worktree-staleness caveat as above; this agent proactively flagged it itself before reporting, and its findings are about files untouched by this session, so verified as still-accurate on current HEAD via spot-checks rather than re-verifying all ~30 individually).

**Confirmed real and fixed:** 7 exported functions with zero callers anywhere in the repo, grep-verified, no wildcard imports hiding a usage — removed: `getMeasuredWinRate`, `getValueArea`, `getPriorMonthValueArea` (`setupBacktestService.js`/`queries.js`), `isHoliday` (`marketCalendar.js`), `nl30ToBucket`/`nl30ToTrend` (`queries.js` — also a "no static thresholds" violation via hardcoded 9/15, moot now they're gone), `tagAllHistorical` (`levelProximityService.js`, no CLI driver either unlike the genuinely-deliberate `replay_all_setups.js`/`populate_setup_daytype_winrates.js` manual-backfill pair, which was correctly left alone).

Don't do either unilaterally.
