- ~20 backend routes with no frontend caller (full list in the agent's report, not re-copied here) — several are self-documented as superseded by a comment right next to them (`/risk/kelly`+`/risk/sizing` explicitly noted as replaced by `/tearsheet-overview`; `/stats/by-hour` etc. have a comment saying "Use EXTRACT directly" instead). One duplicate route definition found (`/level-regime-performance` defined in both `acd.js` and `keyLevels.js`) worth resolving regardless of live usage. Most of the rest look like genuine abandoned/superseded work, not admin-only-by-design (the agent explicitly separated out the ones that look intentionally curl/admin-triggered and did not flag those).
- 2 scripts (`populate_setup_daytype_winrates.js`, `replay_all_setups.js`) referenced only in a code comment, no cron/CLI trigger — but self-documented as "REPORT ONLY, run once by hand," so likely a deliberate manual pair, not abandoned. Lower confidence than the rest of this list.

### Audit 3: Self-recalibration

Cross-referenced every `performance_audit` `signal_type`'s last-run freshness against the actual cron wiring (OS crontab, `run_weekly_backtests.sh`, `run_daily_calibration.sh`, `run_context_analysis.sh`, `run_session_bias.sh`, and `server/index.js`'s internal `node-cron` scheduler — 28+ jobs there alone, mostly Sunday night, confirmed to be an intentional documented fallback relationship with the OS crontab's own Sunday-10:30pm run, not an accidental duplication).

**Fixed**: `server/routes/playbook.js`'s pipeline-status dashboard (`/api/playbook/pipeline-status`) had 3 signal_types with provably wrong metadata — traced via `git log --all -S"<name>"` before touching anything, per this session's own new git-history-first rule:
- `TOD_ALPHA`/`DOW_TOD_ALPHA` — real origin was a one-off `scratch/calculate_dow_tod.mjs` run (Jul 8, never committed to git), not the real weekly `mine_tod_patterns.mjs` script the dashboard claimed (that script only ever writes the separate, real `TOD_PATTERN` signal). The metadata was written the next day by the commit that built this whole dashboard, guessing the source by name-similarity without checking.
- `LEVEL_FADE_DELTA` — real writer (`backtest_session_delta.mjs`) was archived in the *same* commit that correctly marked two sibling audits (`MIDPOINT_FADE_AUDIT`/`PD_IB_AUDIT`) as frozen, but missed this one — it was left pointing at `backtest_level_patterns.mjs` (wrong; that script only writes `LEVEL_PATTERN`).
- `SYSTEM_BACKTEST` — script reference was stale (`backtest_full_system.js`, archived) instead of the real current writer (`backtest_unified.js`); the schedule/freshness claim was accidentally already correct.
- None of these 3 signal_types are read anywhere outside this dashboard (grep-verified) — display-accuracy fixes, not live-decision ones.

**Found, not yet resolved**: `audit_setup_latency.mjs` (writes `LATENCY_AUDIT`) is only scheduled Sunday night via `server/index.js`'s internal cron — given this session found and fixed 2 real live detection bugs today by looking at latency data, a weekly-only cadence seems too infrequent for something meant to catch live issues promptly. Worth a look in a future session (not a "fix," a cadence/priority judgment call). Also: `scripts/backfill_volatility_regime_history.mjs` (writes `VOL_REGIME_HIST`, built earlier today) isn't wired into any recurring cron — if this history table is meant to keep growing day-by-day (rather than being a one-time historical backfill), it needs a daily job; not yet decided which it's meant to be.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (new session, post-`/clear`): worked the 5-item priority list — a live IB_BULLISH regression caught and fixed, a real sizeMultiplier overwrite bug found and fixed, session-bias-conflict extended to level-fades with a backtested factor, execution-quality audit concluded (blocked on data, not code), query-performance concern not currently reproducible

User gave an explicit 5-item priority order at the start of a fresh context. Summary in that order:

**1. Execution-quality audit — resolved, root cause was data attribution, not the 4 levers themselves.** Lever 1 (detection latency) unchanged, already partially done. For levers 2 (fill/slippage) and 4 (stop/target discipline): built the real trade-to-setup matching query (direction + price-proximity, not just the pre-existing 5-min time-only match) and found the honest population is ~4 real matches across the *entire* trade history (40k+ trades, 6.4k+ resolved fade setups) — nowhere near N≥20. Root cause: `trades.setup_type`/`tags`/`acd_signal` are 100% NULL, and `trade_feedback` (the purpose-built `setup_id`-linking table) has 13 rows total, 0 with `setup_id` populated. No slippage/discipline number can be honestly reported until the user starts consistently logging trades against setups (via `trade_feedback`/QuickTradeLog or by tagging `trades.setup_type` at entry) — this is a data-collection gap, not a query one. Fixed the matching query itself along the way: `/api/setups/history` and `/api/timeline/today`'s "Your Trade" LATERAL joins had no direction filter (34% of matches were the wrong direction) and no price-proximity filter (some matches were 1000+ points from the setup's own level, clearly a different trade) — both fixed with a direction + 30pt proximity guard. Lever 3 (sizing-multiplier adherence) surfaced a real bug instead of just being blocked: see next.
- **Found and fixed: `active_setups.size_multiplier` (and the live API field `MarketPulseBar.jsx` reads for the user's actual sizing guidance) was silently discarding the entire ~20-factor level-fade `sizeMultiplier` calculation.** `server/routes/acd.js` ~line 5855 unconditionally overwrote `active.sizeMultiplier` with a crude `hasLossToday ? 0.5 : 1.0` binary (built for a different setup family — the IB_BULLISH/TRT/OPEN_DRIVE "Trade Brief" profiles) *after* the real factor stack (streaks, day-type, NL30, confluence, revisit-latency, etc., range 0.10-1.5x) had already computed a real value. Confirmed via data: 100% of the 78 `size_multiplier` rows persisted since the column's 2026-07-06 rollout were exactly 0.500 (one 1.000) — zero variety, impossible if the real factor stack were reaching the DB. Fixed: the post-loss "Death Sequence protection" is now an additional ceiling (`min(realValue, 0.5)` when `hasLossToday`) instead of a wholesale replacement — preserves size-up signals (up to 1.5x) on clean days, still enforces the post-loss cap on loss days. This is an advisory sizing display, not an auto-executed order, so no historical rows needed repair — only the live value going forward changes. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`s: `execution_quality_audit_blocked_on_attribution`, `size_multiplier_overwrite_bug_fixed`.

**2. Long-tail setup-type audit (checklist items 5-9) — found one genuine live regression, one bounded-risk stale row, rest checked clean.**
- **IB_BULLISH had silently regressed from `SUPPRESS` back to `ACTIVE`** between the 2026-07-14 fix (documented earlier in this file) and 2026-07-15's weekly recalibration, despite blended EV staying deeply negative (-$29.04/tr, N=107) and *every* day-type bucket still negative (BALANCE -$48.59, TURBULENT -$19.30, TREND -$2.94). Root cause in `scripts/backtest_setup_status.mjs`: the `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` "recovered from SUPPRESS" branch used too weak a bar — *any* bucket with N≥20 clearing the -$5 line (TREND barely ticked from -$16.24 to -$2.94 on noise) flipped the whole type straight to `ACTIVE` (bypassing per-day-type gating entirely) instead of `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED` (still gated per-day-type by the hardcoded null-out in `acd.js` ~line 3945). Practical live exposure was bounded by that hardcoded gate the whole time (IB_BULLISH can only ever fire on TREND days regardless of the SETUP_STATUS label) but the Unified Signal Table displayed it as fully-vetted `ACTIVE` at a real loss. Fixed: this branch now always sets `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED`, never `ACTIVE`, matching IB_BEARISH's own (never-suppressed) convention. Re-ran the calibration script to confirm: IB_BULLISH now correctly shows `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED` for 2026-07-16. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `ib_bullish_daytype_conditional_regression_fixed`.
- **`MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND`'s SETUP_STATUS row (`ACTIVE`, N=714, EV=+$10.72) is an orphaned one-time offline-backtest number, not a live-confirmed one** — `active_setups` has zero rows matching `setup_type LIKE 'MOMENTUM%'` anywhere in its history, confirmed directly. `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s weekly run reads exclusively from `active_setups` and would never touch/refresh this signal_name since no matching rows exist to `GROUP BY`; the N=714 is `backtest_momentum60_daytype.mjs`'s original pre-live calibration backtest, sitting stale forever. Practical risk is bounded — `minuteBarSignalDetector.js` (the actual live poller) has its own correct `getLiveStatus()` check requiring N≥20 *real* resolved trades before ever firing a live alert or graduating past SHADOW — but the Unified Signal Table's `ACTIVE` label is misleading to anyone assuming it means live-confirmed. Not fixed this session (bounded risk); may simply mean the trigger condition (extreme 60m momentum on a TREND day) is genuinely rare rather than broken. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `momentum60_trend_orphaned_calibration_row` (PROVISIONAL).
- Checked clean: other `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL`-style exemption-carve-out patterns (none found beyond IB_BULLISH/IB_BEARISH, already fixed), standalone-poller promotion-gap pattern (only `minuteBarSignalDetector.js` exists outside the level-fade candidates array, and it already has the correct dynamic `getLiveStatus()` re-check), Unified Signal Table status-mapping (`/api/performance-audit/unified` ~line 7477 already recognizes every `recommendation` value currently in use: `ACTIVE`/`PROMOTE`/`DAY_TYPE_MANAGED`/`SUPPRESS`/`THIN_N`). Not done: a full manual per-type real-stop/target-simulation review (checklist item 5) across the ~100 setup_types beyond the level-fade family — the automatable slice is done, that piece is still a bigger undertaking if wanted.

Neither is near the previously-logged 41s/17s. Dispatched to Gemini for root-cause diagnosis; both of its theories (an N+1 loop calling `getSetupStats` per setup, a missing index causing a slow momentum-lookback query) were checked directly via `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` and didn't hold up (`getSetupStats` has one on-demand call site, not a loop; the momentum query already runs in 0.19ms). Likely explained by other fixes landing later in the 2026-07-15/16 sessions (DB pool resize, `Promise.all` batching) after the original 41s/17s reading. Full detail folded into the (now-struck-through) original note further down this file.

**4. Monday/Friday root-cause — no new angle, as expected.** Dispatched a bounded, 15-minute-capped check to Gemini for one specific untested dimension (does the user simply trade *more* on Mon/Fri — a behavioral/volume explanation rather than a market-conditions one). Result: volume hypothesis is false — Monday has the *lowest* trade count of the week (7.6/day) yet the biggest losses; Friday's count (11.5/day) is unremarkable next to Thursday's (14.4/day). Nothing else surfaced in the time-boxed window. Consistent with the user's own framing going in — not re-litigated further.

**5. Session-bias cross-check — design decided by user, backtested, and wired live.** Investigating the design question surfaced that a flag-only version already existed for IB_BULLISH/IB_BEARISH only (`sessionConflictFor`, built the same day as that incident, never extended or escalated). Presented the user two real decisions: (a) extend to the level-fade family (the setup family that actually fires most often) — yes; (b) what should happen on conflict: visible flag only / size-down factor / suppress — size-down factor, backtested first, not guessed. Built `scripts/backtest_session_bias_conflict.mjs`: for every resolved level-fade trade, reconstructed what `matchPermissionSlips()` (imported directly, not reimplemented) would have returned for that date, classified each trade as CONFLICT (fired against a ≥65% WR opposing PERMISSION_SLIP read, same threshold the IB-only flag already used) or not. **Result: CONFLICT N=4,037 WR=58.6% EV=-$14.93/tr vs NO_CONFLICT N=1,887 WR=78.1% EV=+$28.62/tr (z=-8.25)** — both buckets individually rigor-clean (not day-clustered, stable across chronological thirds). Checked explicitly for a day-type confound before trusting it (this codebase was burned by exactly this shape of false signal in the "rotation as sizing factor" investigation elsewhere in this file) — holds up independently within BALANCE (-$6.06 vs +$46.46), TREND (-$24.01 vs +$5.23), and TURBULENT (-$27.80 vs +$11.80), so it's a real, independent effect, not a re-labeled day-type signal. Wired live as a new -0.25 `sizeMultiplier` factor for the level-fade family (`server/routes/acd.js`, reuses the existing `sessionConflictFor` closure rather than duplicating it), added to `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx`'s factor-stack description. Caveat carried over from the identical `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA`/NL30 backtest convention already used elsewhere in this codebase: uses the *current* PERMISSION_SLIP calibration applied retroactively across all history, not a true walk-forward re-derivation. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `session_bias_conflict_sizing_factor`.

**Verified end-to-end**: `node --check` on every edited backend file, server restart clean, both touched endpoints (`/api/acd/setup-detection`, `/api/performance-audit/unified`) return 200 post-restart, `/api/setups/history` and `/api/timeline/today` re-tested directly (down to ~4 honest matches, as expected), `npm run lint:frontend` and `npm run build` both clean (one unrelated pre-existing `UpdateDot.jsx` warning), `node scripts/test_invariants.mjs` passes (1 pre-existing unrelated warning: `WPP_FADE_SHORT` base+variant both active simultaneously, not introduced this session), no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` from any of this session's changes.

**Unrelated, noticed in passing, not investigated**: `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` has a cluster of `ReferenceError` `CLIENT_ERROR` entries from earlier 2026-07-16 (14:57 ET and before) across many components (`SessionPulseCard`, `LiveSessionPanel`, `EdgeSectionsPanel`, `VolatilityRegimeCard`, others) — classic "used-before-defined across a code-split/HMR boundary" shape, consistent with the current branch (`perf/react-lazy-splitting`). All entries are stale relative to this session (nothing new since the session started) and the specific components touched by this session's own frontend change (`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx`) are unaffected (build/lint both clean). Flagging for whoever picks the lazy-splitting branch work back up — not chased further here, out of scope of the 5-item list.

**Follow-on, same session: built a standing check so the IB_BULLISH regression class can't go unnoticed for two days again.** User pushback after the 5-item list: "very concerned of us perpetually building but the system isnt learning... the left hand doesnt know what the right hand is doing." Fair — the IB_BULLISH regression above is a direct instance of that: a 2026-07-14 fix got silently undone by an unrelated 2026-07-15 script run, and nothing surfaced it until this session happened to look. Root cause on reflection: a pure "does the stored recommendation match what re-deriving it right now would produce" consistency check would NOT have caught this — IB_BULLISH's TREND bucket really did (barely) clear the -$5 bar (EV=-$2.94, N=33), so the code's own logic was internally consistent, just using too blunt a threshold for a borderline bucket. Fixed by making the *visibility* the fix, not a smarter threshold: `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` now has a `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED WATCH` section (new `DTM_WATCH` psql block + JS parsing) that prints every `DAY_TYPE_CONDITIONAL` type's full per-day-type bucket breakdown at the start of *every* session, and flags (⚠️) any bucket that's the sole reason a type isn't `SUPPRESS`ed while sitting within $10 of the bar or under N=50. Verified live: correctly flags IB_BULLISH's TREND bucket (the real regression) and, as an honest bonus, IB_BEARISH's TURBULENT bucket (N=31 — not broken, genuinely strong at $65/tr, but thin enough to be worth a glance) — both intentional, not noise. New CLAUDE.md hard-rule note added alongside the existing `sizeMultiplier` conventions. This doesn't prevent the underlying class of bug (a carve-out's own threshold being too blunt) — it makes sure a human sees the fragile state every session instead of it living silently in a database row, which is the actual fix for "the left hand doesn't know what the right hand is doing": stop relying on someone remembering to look, put the fact in front of them automatically.

**Follow-on, same session: applied the same "build the self-check at the same time as the feature" principle to close the execution-quality audit's actual blocker.** User's point generalized further: every new fix/feature should carry its own standing re-check, not become a future landmine. Investigated why `trade_feedback.setup_id` was 0/13 populated (the root cause of the execution-quality audit being blocked, above) and found the mechanism to fix it already existed — `TradeFeedbackBar` (`src/components/dashboard/QuickTradeLog.jsx`) has correct logic to post a real `setup_id` when a live setup fires, but it was never imported or mounted anywhere in the app. A built-but-disconnected feature is the same class of landmine as an unmonitored regression — nobody would ever have found this without going looking. Fixed: wired into `App.jsx`'s `LiveSessionPanel` Active Setup Card (the always-visible sidebar card, keyed on `setupId` so its local TAKEN/PASSED state correctly resets per new setup). Verified backend end-to-end via a live curl call (real `active_setups` id, `setup_id` populated correctly, test row cleaned up) and frontend via Playwright (zero console/page errors) — could not visually confirm the button itself since no setup was actively firing during the check. **Closed the loop the same way as the DAY_TYPE_MANAGED watch**: added a `FEEDBACK_COVERAGE` check to `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` that surfaces `trade_feedback` setup_id coverage every session and explicitly nudges "re-run the execution-quality audit" once N≥20 is reached — so accumulating real data actually gets used instead of sitting there the same way the disconnected component did. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `trade_feedback_setup_id_ui_gap_fixed`.

**Follow-on, same session: detection latency (item 4 of the original 5) — built the missing direct heartbeat, then found and fixed a genuinely serious active bug while verifying it.**
- **Built `server/services/detectionHeartbeat.js`**: the real July 13 ~1-hour detection gap (docs/OPEN_THREADS.md, still unexplained) had no root cause because nothing durable measured the poller's own success/failure — `/api/settings/process-health`'s `SETUP_DETECTION` entry only ever inferred health from bar freshness (`MAX(ts) FROM price_bars_primary`), which can't distinguish "detection stopped" from "no new bar yet." Wired the real poll result (success/HTTP status/timeout) from `server/index.js`'s 15s poller into an in-memory heartbeat that logs a durable `process_log` row on outage-start (3 consecutive failures) and recovery (with real duration) — cheap (no DB write on every healthy poll) but survives the exact "no server logs survived" failure mode that blocked the July 13 investigation. `/api/settings/process-health` now reads this directly instead of the bar-freshness proxy. Verified live: server restarted, heartbeat recorded a real success within one poll cycle, endpoint reflects it correctly.
- **Found and fixed a real, currently-active, every-poll-cycle bug while verifying the above.** `journalctl` showed `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "idx_as_unique_setup"` firing on essentially every 15s poll, from 3 call sites (`resolveSetupsByPrice`, `expireStaleSetups`, `structurallyInvalidateSetups`). Root cause: `idx_as_unique_setup` was a blanket unique index on `(trade_date, setup_type, status)`, but the earlier-same-session re-touch fix (above, "two real live-firing bugs fixed") now legitimately allows a second `ACTIVE` row to open for a `(trade_date, setup_type)` after an earlier one on the same day already closed — when that second row tries to resolve (`status` → `RESOLVED`/`EXPIRED`), it collides with the *first* row already occupying that exact slot. Confirmed directly: `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `IB_LOW_FADE_LONG`, and `STOP_SWEEP_LONG` all had a second row stuck in `ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` today, unable to ever resolve — a **silent data-loss bug**, not just log spam; the stuck row would never contribute `actual_pnl`/resolution data to any EV calculation. Fixed by making `idx_as_unique_setup` a **partial** unique index (`WHERE status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')`) — preserves the original anti-duplicate-concurrent-insert guarantee for open setups while allowing unlimited `RESOLVED`/`EXPIRED` rows per type per day, which is what real re-touches actually produce. All 6 `ON CONFLICT (trade_date, setup_type, COALESCE(status,''))` clauses against `active_setups` (5 in `acd.js`, 1 in `minuteBarSignalDetector.js`) updated with the matching `WHERE status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')` predicate — required for Postgres to match a partial index as the conflict target. Verified live: zero duplicate-key errors across 2+ poll cycles post-fix, and all 3 previously-stuck rows from today correctly resolved once the fix went live. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `active_setups_retouch_unique_index_deadlock_fixed`.
- **Not done**: root cause of the original July 13 gap itself remains unknown (no data survived to investigate retroactively) — the heartbeat only prevents this from being unrecoverable *going forward*. `PD_IB_MID_FADE_SHORT`'s recurring lag (3 distinct historical dates) and the full checklist-item-5 pass (real stop/target simulation across the long tail of setup_types) dispatched to Gemini, results pending review.

**Follow-on, same session: user visually caught a real, live, wrong-$/pt-constant bug from a screenshot — traced to 3 independent occurrences, fixed all of them, built a shared instruments table.** A setup card's risk/reward labels ($475/$220 for a 95pt/44pt setup) didn't match its own resolved P&L (+$87) shown right next to them. Root cause: `App.jsx`'s `CaseSetupDetailModal` hardcoded `* 5` for risk/reward dollar math — the exact wrong constant CLAUDE.md already documents once for a backend script (`PT=5/COMM=5`), now found independently in a live frontend component. Swept for more and found 2 additional instances of the same bug class: `SetupHistoryView.jsx`'s P&L column tooltip claimed "$20/pt" (NQ, not MNQ), and `acd.js`'s `TRT_LONG` trade-brief text separately hardcoded "$5/pt". Fixed all three. Built `server/config/instruments.js` + `src/constants/contract.js` as a single shared `INSTRUMENTS` table per explicit user request ("a product table that these views reference so that they are not stale") — migrated `RiskView.jsx` and `acd.js`'s `resolveSetupsByPrice()` to reference it instead of redeclaring the constant. Verified directly that real NQ contracts (not just MNQ) genuinely appear in `trades` history — per-contract P&L runs ~9.2x MNQ's for the same contract period, matching the real $20-vs-$2 ratio almost exactly, confirming this is genuine NQ trading and not mislabeled data (a claim in an early draft of the fix, "this journal has never traded NQ live," was wrong and corrected before being committed). NQ's `commissionPerRoundTrip` is recorded as user-recalled ($2), explicitly flagged as unverified against a real statement, not a guess presented as fact. **Extended the standing hardcoded-threshold-pattern infrastructure** (the pre-commit + Stop hooks that already block/flag patterns A-D) with a new Pattern E for wrong $/pt constants — deliberately scoped broadly across `server/`+`src/`, not narrowed to `acd.js`/`caseEngine.js` like patterns A-D, since this bug class has now shown up in a frontend modal and a table tooltip too. Verified the pattern catches the exact bug shape and doesn't false-positive on the fix; one real false-positive found and excluded by path (the instruments table's own canonical `NQ ($20/pt)` definition) and one reviewed-and-excluded non-bug (`PlaybookView.jsx`'s intentional NQ-scale observational stat). **Also added a session-start check (`UNTRACKED_SYMBOLS`) per explicit user request** ("if you detect new products… this table needs to be updated") that flags any traded instrument root not yet in `INSTRUMENTS`, so a genuinely new instrument gets noticed automatically instead of silently producing wrong math the way MNQ/NQ's constant did for an unknown period. `RESEARCH_CLAIM`: `wrong_dollar_per_point_frontend_bug_fixed`.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (yet another follow-on): Setup Log silo fixed — live-current WR + drift flag + trade reconciliation + MAE/MFE

User noticed the Setup Log (`src/views/SetupHistoryView.jsx`, `GET /api/setups/history` in `server/routes/acd.js`) never showed their own executed trades and asked why the system's data doesn't all feed into each other. Investigated (dispatched an Explore agent, verified its findings directly) and confirmed three real gaps, all in the same table:

- **No connection to `trades` at all** — the query only ever touched `active_setups`. A `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` matching trades to setups by time-proximity already existed elsewhere (`/timeline/today`, same file ~line 6425) but was never reused here. Added the same pattern to `/setups/history` — new "Your Trade" column, matched within 5 min of fire.
- **"WR at Fire" was a frozen snapshot, not a live stat** — written once at INSERT time from `getHistory()`/`getReplayBaseline`, never updated. Added a second column, "WR Now," joined live from the same `performance_audit` SETUP_STATUS rows that drive live sizing (`server/scripts/backtest_setup_status.mjs`, weekly/daily recalibration) — **and a ⚠ drift flag when the two disagree by ≥8pp**. Real example caught immediately on first test: `IB_LOW_FADE_SHORT` showed 70.5% WR at fire (N=271) vs. 61.9% WR / **negative EV (-$9.29/tr)** currently (N=21) — a setup that looked great when it fired and is now barely above the N≥20 floor and losing money, invisible before this fix.
- **MAE/MFE existed on `active_setups` already but were never selected/displayed** — added as a new column, shown once a setup resolves.
- **Live browser check done, corrected after an initial mistake.** First said Playwright wasn't available in this environment — wrong, and a real miss: didn't check npx's cache (`~/.npm/_npx/*/node_modules/playwright`) or the already-downloaded browser cache (`~/.cache/ms-playwright`), which is exactly what CLAUDE.md's own "2026-07-15 /tmp node_modules-symlink trick" note pointed at. Symlinked `node_modules/playwright`/`playwright-core` into the project (same trick, now redocumented here for the next session that hits this) and ran a real Chromium check against the live dev server: all 4 new columns (MAE/MFE, WR at Fire, WR Now, Your Trade) render with correct labels and working tooltips, real data loads, no errors from the change itself. Found one unrelated pre-existing bug in passing: `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` (a sibling BacktestView tab that stays mounted per this app's tab-persistence convention) polls `/api/acd/engine-summary`, which 404s — no such route exists server-side. Not fixed, not in scope of this task, flagged here for a future session.
- **Not done**: this same "frozen snapshot presented without a live counterpart" pattern may exist in other views built the same way — not audited elsewhere this session.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (latest session): 24-hour setup firing scoped out as its own dedicated task (deliberately deferred); volatility regime history now persisted canonically; two Opus consultations on system-learning architecture

**User request**: "fire these trades 24 hours and filter by RTH and Non RTH, and both in the sidebar." Investigated, found the real scope is much larger than a poller tweak, and — per explicit user direction — **stopped here and deferred it to its own future session** rather than rewriting the core live-detection engine mid-session.

- **What's actually blocking 24-hour firing**: `server/index.js`'s autonomous poller was extended (done, harmless) to call `/api/acd/setup-detection` across real CME Globex hours (Sun 6PM ET–Fri 5PM ET, minus the daily 5-6PM maintenance break) instead of RTH-only. But this alone does nothing — the query that fills `allRthBarsRow` (`server/routes/acd.js:2951-2958`) is hard-scoped to `ts::date = todayET AND et_min BETWEEN 570 AND 959` (today's own 9:30am-4pm bars only). That array feeds essentially the entire live detection engine — VWAP magnet, level-scalp, session delta, first-15, touch detection — 20+ call sites from line 3957 through 5800+, all gated on `allRthBarsRow.rows.length >= N`. Before ~9:33 AM ET each day it has 0-2 rows by construction, so every one of those blocks silently no-ops. **The poller change makes the endpoint get called overnight; the endpoint itself still detects nothing overnight.**
- **Real scope for a future session**: decide which setup types are genuinely overnight-eligible (ONL_FADE, PW_*/PD_* reference levels — don't need *today's* RTH bars to exist, just a current price + a known static level) vs. structurally RTH-only (anything keyed to IB/OR/session-VWAP, which are defined *by* today's RTH session and can't exist before it opens) — then give the overnight-eligible ones their own bar source (current Globex price via `latestBarRow`-style query, not `allRthBarsRow`) instead of retrofitting the entire 4,000+ line block. This is a real redesign of live-money detection logic in a function CLAUDE.md already documents several sharp footguns in (block-scoping on `liveStats`, positional `Promise.all` destructuring) — treat it with the same care as any other new-setup-type work, not a quick patch.
- **RTH/Non-RTH sidebar filter is blocked on the above, not built yet** — with detection still RTH-gated, every fired setup is RTH by construction, so a Non-RTH filter bucket would always be empty. Build the filter (tag setups live from `fired_at` ET time, no schema change needed; 3-way sidebar toggle persisted via `sessionStorage` matching the existing `acd-dash-tab` pattern) as part of the same future session once overnight detection actually produces something to filter.

**Volatility regime history — now real and persisted.** Two Gemini attempts at a Markov-vs-existing-regime EV comparison were internally inconsistent (HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL scoring worse than HIGH-VOL-CHOP, contradicting prior validation) because `volatilityRegimeService.js` had no persisted history, forcing Gemini to hand-reimplement `classifyRegime()` twice with no fidelity check against the live version. Fixed at the root: exported the live functions (`fiveMinBars`, `stdevLogReturns`, `getPercentile`, `classifyRegime`, `getMorningVolBaseline`) and built `scripts/backfill_volatility_regime_history.mjs`, which imports them directly (no reimplementation) and persists one row/day to `performance_audit` (`signal_type='VOL_REGIME_HIST'`). Ran clean: **357 days classified** (LOW-VOL 68, NORMAL-VOL 195, HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL 50, HIGH-VOL-CHOP 44), 60 skipped for thin baseline. **Not yet done**: re-run the Markov-vs-regime EV comparison against this real history (existing `scratch/antigravity_response.md` result is still built on the old, untrustworthy reimplementation — don't treat its numbers as final).

**Both Gemini tasks completed, audited, and acted on** — user directed "Both use Gemini" for the Markov re-run and the RESEARCH_CLAIM inventory below.

- **Markov-vs-regime re-run, now fully audited**: Gemini's response file hit the same recurring truncation bug (its own wrap-up narration overwrote the top of the real Task 1 output), so the "by real regime" section had to be recovered from an earlier mid-write read, and the Markov>0.8/<0.2 buckets were missing entirely — recomputed independently by Claude (`computeRigor` imported directly, no reimplementation) and confirmed to match Gemini's other numbers almost exactly (e.g. Markov>0.5: Claude N=1451/EV=-$3.15 vs Gemini's N=1454/EV=-$3.32). **Real answer, N=4,231 fade trades**: the existing volatility-regime classifier separates good/bad conditions far better than the Markov model — real-regime spread is HIGH-VOL-CHOP +$6.75/tr (N=761, rigor-clean+stable) vs HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL -$17.99/tr (N=766, not clean) ≈ $25 spread, vs. Markov's Markov<0.2 +$2.58/tr (N=2451, clean+stable) vs Markov>0.8 -$7.48/tr (N=1032, not clean, and trending toward *improving* over its own three chronological thirds) ≈ $10 spread. **Conclusion: Markov adds no value over the existing classifier — don't build it in.** Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `markov_vs_regime_comparison`.
- **RESEARCH_CLAIM inventory — caught a real fabrication, a live example of why every Gemini claim gets independently verified before being trusted, not just spot-checked for plausibility.** Gemini's inventory listed "TRIPLE zone confluence fade... EVs of +$14.32, +$13.73, and +$11.30 across its chronological thirds" as a `CONFIRMED`-worthy claim. Checked directly against `scratch/verification_findings.json` (`confluence['10'].TRIPLE['120']`): the real thirds are **+$12.18, +$12.61, +$18.06** (N=2975, all-time EV=+$14.29/tr) — plausible-sounding but invented numbers, not present anywhere in the source file. The underlying claim direction (clean, stable, improving) was correct; the specific digits were not. Recorded with the **corrected** numbers as `triple_zone_confluence_alltime` (`CONFIRMED`). A second candidate claim ("5D_OR_MID fade +$9.85 LONG / +$10.25 SHORT") couldn't be traced to any source file after a real search attempt — **excluded from the ledger rather than trusted on Gemini's word alone.**

Note: overrode 2 of Gemini's own suggested statuses (`STALE`→`PROVISIONAL` for two claims that had *just* been rigor-checked and failed, which isn't what "stale" means) — Gemini's status labels are a draft, not authoritative.

**Follow-up, same session: acted on Opus's #2 and #3 priorities (export-not-reimplement rule, folding `backtest_confluence.js` into the rigor lifecycle) — found `minePatterns()` isn't actually live, and caught a real cross-run discrepancy in the TRIPLE-zone number the fix was meant to prevent.**

The systemic fix stays in place regardless: CLAUDE.md hard rule ("Resolve every `isolation: worktree` Agent dispatch...") plus the `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` check that will flag any future orphaned worktree at session start.

Corrected the ledger entry to point at `performance_audit`'s `CONFLUENCE_AUDIT` rows (the actual canonical, live, cron'd source) as the source of truth going forward, not any scratch file. Real-time proof the ledger + rigor-wiring combination catches the exact failure mode it was built for.

LOW-VOL (+$17.57/touch) and NORMAL-VOL (-$22.16/touch) both kept their usual sign, just at worse magnitude. Day-of-week and day-type genuinely show no pattern (Gemini was right about that part). **Not actionable yet** — day-level N for the HIGH-VOL-CHOP bucket is only 8, and 2 of those days account for most of the damage — but it's a real, specific, falsifiable hypothesis worth re-checking as more data accumulates, not "normal variance." Recorded as the corrected `triple_zone_recent_flip` claim.

Also checked whether the TRIPLE-zone/HIGH-VOL-CHOP anomaly (above) was a broad regime-mislabeling problem: it isn't — the full `_FADE_` population was actually *positive* in HIGH-VOL-CHOP over the same window (+$11.83/tr, N=142/7 days), so whatever went wrong is TRIPLE-zone-confluence-specific, not systemic.

Verified both paths directly (inserted a throwaway test row with a past-due date, confirmed it printed as `🔴 RESEARCH_CLAIM LEDGER — 1 claim(s) past their recheck date`, then deleted the test row; confirmed the clean/no-claims-overdue path separately). This closes the last piece of Opus's sustainable-learning proposal that wasn't yet automatic.

**Two Opus consultations this session** (`docs/OPUS_AUDIT_PROMPT*.md` convention — ad hoc strategic consults, not a formal audit pass):
1. **"Should I build an automated Sierra Chart trader with free reign, trading intraday every day?"** — Opus's answer, grounded in this session's own freshly-verified Mon/Fri finding: no. The honest constraint isn't intelligence, it's that no software here has a live P&L feed to detect real-time tilt/drawdown state (the reason the whole conversation started — user already has that problem *manually*). Building a bot that must trade daily regardless of edge quality inverts the one process failure (forcing trades on bad days) already found and confirmed in the data.
2. **"How do we make the system keep learning without me having to keep re-catching its own errors?"** — Opus proposed: (a) a `RESEARCH_CLAIM` ledger in `performance_audit` so exploratory/research-tier findings (not just setup-calibration ones) get durable provenance and a weekly re-check instead of evaporating at session end; (b) an "export-not-reimplement" convention — formalizing what just fixed the Markov/regime inconsistency above: any backtest depending on live-derived classification logic must import the real exported function, never reimplement it inline; (c) fold `minePatterns()` (`patternScannerService.js`) and `backtest_confluence.js` into the same rigor/lifecycle discipline the other 4 mining pipelines already have (`computeRigor()`, ACTIVE/DEGRADED). **(a) built this session**: `scripts/record_claim.mjs` (`recordClaim()`/`listClaims()`, writes `RESEARCH_CLAIM` rows to `performance_audit` with a 30-day `next_recheck_due`), seeded with 7 audited claims (see above). **Not yet done**: (b) isn't written up as a formal rule anywhere beyond this file and the Markov backfill's own comment header — worth promoting to CLAUDE.md's Hard Rules if it recurs a second time; (c) not started, lower priority per Opus.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (later same session): trades-table timezone bug found and fully repaired — data integrity restored, Mon/Fri finding survives a 4th independent check

Follow-on from the win/loss audit below, triggered by the user pushing back ("my trades look correct on the trade page though") on Claude's initial alarm about a 4-hour shift found in raw `entry_time` values. That pushback was the right instinct and led to the real, complete picture — full account now in `CLAUDE.md`'s timestamp-handling convention (search "Never parse a naive"), not duplicated here in full. Summary:

- **Root cause**: `server/parsers/sierraParser.js`'s `parseDateTime()` used ambient-timezone-dependent `new Date(str).toISOString()` on naive (no-tz) Sierra Chart strings. Ambient system timezone silently changed at some point, so every import since 2026-06-09 stored `entry_time`/`exit_time` shifted +4h(EDT)/+5h(EST) off the real Sierra time, while the frontend's own UTC→ET display conversion accidentally *cancelled out* that shift — meaning recent trades displayed correctly (confirming the user's observation) while older, correctly-stored trades displayed 4-5 hours **early** (the opposite of either party's first assumption). Fixed with an explicit `Intl.DateTimeFormat`-based ET-anchoring function, verified immune to ambient `TZ`.
- **Data repair, all independently verified (Claude + Gemini cross-checks) before executing, full backup taken first** (`trades_backup_20260716`): 35,813 pre-2026-06-09 rows had their timestamps corrected (date-aware +4h/+5h, not a flat shift); three separate duplicate-import issues cleaned up (4,100 rows from a 2026-06-09 re-import, 1,168 from an unrelated 2026-04-22 re-import of the original 2026-03-11 historical load, 3,769 from a structural Sierra Chart TAL quirk — the same completed position logged on multiple lines with different BP/EP suffix placement, present across the *entire* 2+ year history, not just the two known re-import events). `trades`: 40,453 → 31,416 rows. Zero `trade_screenshots` (only FK referencing `trades.id`) impact confirmed before any deletion.
- **The Monday/Friday finding was re-verified a 4th time on the fully clean data and is essentially unchanged**: Mon -$409/day (2/10 win days), Fri -$486/day (1/10), vs. Wed +$484/day (8/12), Thu +$348/day (7/11) — this was never a data-quality artifact.
- **Not done / residual**: the root cause of *why* the ambient timezone flipped around 2026-06-09 was never identified (git history has an unexplained commit gap 2026-06-03→2026-06-12 spanning it) — not blocking, since the fix doesn't depend on knowing why, but worth keeping in mind if a similar environment-dependent bug surfaces again. The 2026-04-22 re-import's own root cause also wasn't investigated (lower priority, already cleaned up).

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (continued session): two real live-firing bugs fixed, SessionPulseCard fully audited/fixed, PW_MID added, trade win/loss audit run (Gemini-mined, Claude-verified) — Mon/Fri deep-dive dispatched, not yet back

Picked up mid-session from a live user report ("this just fired but says 9:34") plus a series of Session Pulse card questions that turned into a full audit of that card's rigor.

**Two real live-firing bugs found and fixed, both deployed same session:**
- **`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE`'s live fire gate was wrong** (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 4884) — waited for IB close (`etMinNow >= 630`, 10:30 ET) when the level it fades (the 5-min Opening Range mid) actually forms at ~9:35 ET and has nothing to do with IB (60-min) closing. Root cause confirmed via git blame: introduced in `bf65b47` (2026-07-02) as a copy-paste of the sibling `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE` line in the same commit, not a deliberate backtest finding. Fixed by removing the redundant gate on this one entry only (`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE`'s own gate is untouched — `ibMid` is structurally unavailable before 630 regardless). Verified live: fired correctly at 10:29:22 ET the same session, correctly held at `SHADOW` per its real negative live EV (N=121/97, EV=-$5.90/-$5.12, still `SUPPRESS`-eligible) — gate fix doesn't make it tradeable, it was never meant to.
- **Stale `fired_at`/entry/stop/target on re-touch** (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 5955, `/acd/setup-detection`) — the "does this setup already exist today" lookup had no `status` filter, so it found ANY row for `(trade_date, setup_type)` regardless of whether it was already `EXPIRED`/`RESOLVED`, and echoed back that old row's stale `fired_at`/levels instead of starting a fresh row for a genuine new touch. Live symptom: `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` invalidated at 09:36 was still shown as "active" at 10:25 with "fired 09:34 ET" when price re-touched the level. Fixed: filter to `status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')` (the two still-open states this INSERT ever assigns) — a closed row no longer blocks a fresh one. The unique index is `(trade_date, setup_type, COALESCE(status,''))`, so this doesn't conflict with the old closed row.

**SessionPulseCard.jsx — full rigor audit, real bugs found and fixed, prescriptive language stripped, real backtest numbers wired in:**
- **`LOWER_LOWS`/`LOWER_HIGHS` label mismatch — bearish structure had never once rendered its intended color or narrative.** Backend (`morningBrief.js` line 787) computes and emits `microTrend: 'LOWER_LOWS'` (tracks whether swing lows are falling — it never looks at highs at all, so `LOWER_HIGHS` was always the wrong name for what's measured). Frontend's `CHIP_DETAIL.structure`/color logic/exhaustion-score only recognized `'LOWER_HIGHS'`, a string the backend has never emitted — so every bearish reading silently fell through to the neutral-gray default and was never counted toward the exhaustion score. Renamed consistently to `LOWER_LOWS` throughout.
- **The STRUCTURE narrative ("Buyers absorbing every pullback... Shorts covering = fuel") was unconditional boilerplate keyed only on `microTrend`, ignoring DELTA/VOLUME/efficiency-ratio shown right next to it, and asserting a mechanism ("shorts covering") this system has no data to ever measure.** Found via a live example (10:15 ET, HIGHER_LOWS structure but FLAT delta, 0.83x volume, 0.06 efficiency ratio — the numbers argued against the story the text was telling). Same problem existed in the DELTA and VOLUME chip narratives ("Supports long fades and upside setups" etc.) — none of the 4 chips' directive/prescriptive language had ever been backtested. Stripped all prescriptive language from all 3 chip narratives (rotations' narrative was left as-is, not flagged this pass), replaced with purely descriptive text ("no backtested edge behind this reading yet").
- **Dispatched a Gemini backtest** (does the 4-signal combination — delta+structure agreement, conditioned on rotations/volume conviction — actually predict a real forward outcome with a real stop/target, not just a raw point value) rather than build a "unified verdict" card on intuition alone (this codebase has been burned by exactly that shape of mistake before — see the `MOMENTUM_60m` "critical correction" entry further down this file). **Result: no bucket clears N≥20 + rigor + real EV.** `UP_LOW` (N=58) is EV-negative (-$5.79/tr); `DOWN_LOW` (N=46) is EV-positive (+$7.70/tr, 65.2% WR) but fails the 3-way chronological stability check; `UP_HIGH`/`DOWN_HIGH` never reach N≥20 (too rare intraday). Confirms directive language should stay off this card — a "unified UP/DOWN/conviction" verdict was deliberately **not** built.
- **Counter-Move Tracker's 0.46/0.68/0.88 retracement multipliers and the "374pt/89%" bounce claim were also both hardcoded/unsourced** (same commit, `2a69377`, as everything above — no backtest ever existed for either). Built `scripts/backtest_countermove_retracement.mjs` (promoted from Gemini's scratch draft, will need a re-run periodically — not yet wired to `performance_audit` for auto-refresh, a known follow-up) — real numbers, N=241 days with an >80pt open extension over the last year, rigor-clean (not clustered, stable): **P25=0.31 / P50=0.65 / P75=1.47** (P75 >100% means in the top quartile of cases price doesn't just retrace, it reverses through the open and keeps going), **median bounce 156pt, 51.5% saw a 150pt+ bounce** (not 374pt/89%). Real bonus finding: DOWN moves snap back much harder than UP moves (215pt vs 126pt median bounce), and TREND days barely retrace at all (median 22%) vs TURBULENT days (83%) — wired the P25/MED/P75 targets, the bar's visual scale, and the real bounce stat into the live card. **Known caveat, not resolved**: the live card's own `morningDrop` (`|closeVsOpen|` at the current live moment) isn't necessarily identical to the backtest's `peakMove` (the single most extreme point reached after the initial 80pt trigger) if price is still extending when the card reads it — order of magnitude and direction are solid, exact percentile alignment against the live field hasn't been bar-by-bar reconciled. Worth a look if the live numbers seem off after a few days of observation.

**`PW_MID` added to `compute_levels.js`** (prior week's `(PW_HIGH+PW_LOW)/2`, matching the existing `PD_SESSION_MID`/`PD_OR_MID` pattern) — user asked whether "CW MID"/"PW MID" (seen on a Sierra Chart display) exist in this system; neither did (no "CW" prefix exists here at all, and PW previously had no MID variant). Verified live: computed correctly (29564 for the current prior week) and persisted to `level_prices` for today. **Not** wired into any live fade candidate in `acd.js` — this is a queryable/referenceable level only, it does not create a new trading signal. Side effect: running `compute_levels.js` to add this also backfilled today's `level_prices` row, which had been stale since 2026-07-13 (a gap already tracked elsewhere in this file under the confluence-zone thread) — only today's gap, the underlying nightly-cron root cause for the recurring gap is still not diagnosed.

**Win/loss trade pattern audit — user asked for help finding why they've been losing.** Dispatched to Gemini (last 60 trading days / 90 calendar days, all accounts per explicit user choice), then independently audited per the standing rule, not just trusted:
- **Caught a real error**: Gemini's `direction` dimension claimed 100% of trades were LONG. Direct query: actual split is 2023 LONG / 1040 SHORT. That entire dimension in `scratch/trade_audit_findings.json` is wrong and was discarded — a live example of why the audit-before-acting rule exists.
- **The real, verified-independently headline finding is day-of-week, not day_type or setup_type.** Claude re-derived this directly with the correct CumPL-diff method (not just Gemini's trade-count framing): Mon avg **-$463**/day (3/10 win days), Fri avg **-$496**/day (1/10 win days) vs Wed **+$510**/day (8/12) and Thu **+$358**/day (7/11). Checked Friday specifically for day-clustering (Gemini itself flagged this as needing verification) — **not clustered**, 9 of 10 Fridays lost money, broadly real. Also checked whether this is secretly the same signal as the `day_type` finding below — it isn't: TREND days are not concentrated on Mon/Fri (Wednesday has the *most* TREND days of any weekday, and Wednesday is the best day), so these are two independent patterns.
- **`day_type` (TREND days worst, avg -$166/day) is directionally real but thin** — only 11 TREND days in this 60-day window, below this codebase's own N≥20 floor. Worth revisiting with a longer lookback.
- **Only 12.3% of trades match to any system-detected `setup_type`** (fuzzy-joined to `active_setups` by date/direction/time/price proximity, since `trades.setup_type`/`tags`/`acd_signal` are 100% NULL for the whole window) — the large majority of the user's trading is discretionary, off any system-tracked setup. This means "is it a specific setup type" mostly isn't answerable from current data.
- **Real, not-yet-fully-explained sizing pattern**: losing days show more BOTH 1-lot trades (40.3% vs 35.3%) AND 3+-lot trades (26.3% vs 18.2%) than winning days, while winning days concentrate more around 2-lot (46.5% vs 33.4%) — deviating from normal size in either direction correlates with losing days. Not yet dug into further.
- **`scratch/mon_fri_deepdive_findings.json` (the Mon/Fri deep-dive follow-on) landed at 11:40 ET but is CONFIRMED STALE — do not use it.** It predates both the timezone-shift repair (13:27 ET) and the big dedup pass (14:07 ET, 9,037 duplicate rows removed) documented above. Re-dispatched to Gemini with the identical scope against the now-repaired `trades` table — **its headline "post-loss escalation" claim (over half of losing Mon/Fri days show dramatic size doubling, up to 15-16 lots) does NOT survive independent verification and should not be repeated or acted on.** Two real problems found: (1) Gemini's trade-by-trade walkthrough had genuine timestamp/quantity misattribution — summed PnL often matched the real `trades` rows but the reported per-trade timestamps/quantities frequently didn't (verified directly against raw rows for 2026-06-05); (2) independently reconstructed the actual population directly (grouping raw Sierra multi-line fills by exact timestamp, correct double `AT TIME ZONE` UTC→ET conversion — a single `AT TIME ZONE` on the naive `entry_time` column gives a wrong answer, confirmed by hand) and found only **N=6** Mon/Fri losing days even have a real pre-loss baseline to test escalation against (most losing days start with a loss on the very first trade). Of those 6, only 2 show a real spike; Wed/Thu (N=2, thinner still) showed the same or higher rate, undercutting the Mon/Fri-specific framing entirely. Recorded as the `mon_fri_post_loss_escalation_debunked` `RESEARCH_CLAIM` — a negative result, logged with the same weight as a positive one per Opus's convention. **Not checked**: the response's secondary "10:30-12:00 time-of-day clustering" claim — unverified either way, don't repeat it as fact without checking.

## 🆕 2026-07-16 (earlier in session): two forward-looking research ideas (not started) + 3 UI bugs found & fixed from live screenshots

**Idea 1 — regime-transition forecasting.** Not "is today choppy" (already live) but "is a regime *shift* coming, before it shows up in realized numbers." Real tools exist for this (`statsmodels` `MarkovRegression`/`MarkovAutoregression`, change-point detection via `ruptures`) but this is a genuinely harder problem than anything tested so far — honest expectation-setting given to the user: institutional quant shops work this exact problem without a clean solved answer, so treat as real research, not a guaranteed win.

**Idea 2 — per-level reversion signature by regime.** Combines two things already built: the per-level Hurst/ADF breakdown (`scratch/level_mean_reversion_findings.json` — found `MR2_FADE_LONG` as a standout, Hurst 0.53 + real live EV) and the live regime classifier (`volatilityRegimeService.js`). Untested question: does a level's mean-reversion strength change by regime (e.g. strong in NORMAL, weak in HIGH-VOL-CHOP)? Would let the system recommend different levels depending on today's regime instead of one static ranking. Well-scoped, natural next Gemini dispatch.

**Bugs found via user screenshot, fixed same session:**
- **Duplicate `SessionPulseCard`** — was rendering twice simultaneously (`App.jsx`'s always-visible sidebar copy + `ACDView.jsx`'s Dashboard-tab copy). Removed the `ACDView.jsx` instance; sidebar copy already covers every tab.
- **No tooltips on Session Pulse stats** — added an `InfoTooltip` on the card header explaining ROTATIONS/VOLUME/DELTA/STRUCTURE in plain language (each chip already had click-to-expand detail via `CHIP_DETAIL`, just no hover affordance signaling it existed).
- **Chip popover rendering far off-position** (`MarketPulseBar.jsx`'s `ChipPopover`, used by both `ContextChips`' own instance and the outer bar) — nested inside a sticky, flex-wrapping row; root CSS cause not fully isolated, fixed defensively by portaling the popover to `document.body` via `createPortal` so it's never subject to ancestor layout/stacking-context quirks regardless of the exact cause.
