Living continuity tracker — distinct from [KNOWN_ISSUES.md](KNOWN_ISSUES.md) (bugs/tech debt found in code) and [ARCHITECTURE.md](../ARCHITECTURE.md) (structure). This file exists so that clearing context never loses a thread: confirmed-but-unimplemented decisions, proposals awaiting the user's go-ahead, stale stats discovered but not yet refreshed, and multi-session analysis work that isn't finished.

All hardcoded thresholds, suppressed setups, and design rationale: [docs/HARDCODED_CONSTANTS.md](HARDCODED_CONSTANTS.md)

**Convention changed 2026-07-14**: when an item is finished, strike it through (`~~like this~~`) and add a short "Resolved <date>" note rather than deleting it — same convention change as KNOWN_ISSUES.md (that file's header has the full explanation of why). When a session confirms a decision, proposes a follow-up, or finds a stats/data-freshness gap and doesn't finish it in the same session, add it here before the session ends.

## 🆕 Follow-on session, 2026-07-15/16: per-level Hurst/ADF breakdown, GARCH deep-dives, and regime tool wired to the flash badge

Continuation of the confluence investigation below, done same overall session (date rolled past midnight partway through). Summary of what's new beyond the original entry:

- **Per-individual-level Hurst/ADF test** (`scripts/backtest_level_mean_reversion.py`, `scratch/level_mean_reversion_findings.json`) — the earlier Hurst/ADF work only tested lumped SINGLE/DOUBLE/TRIPLE/QUAD_PLUS tiers, which averages away real per-level variance. Broke it out by individual `(level_name, direction)` (124 all-time groups, 90 recent, N≥20 floor), cross-checked against live `SETUP_STATUS` EV. **Real standout found: `MR2_FADE_LONG`** (Monthly R2 pivot) — Hurst 0.53 (right at the genuine mean-reversion threshold, far below the ~0.90 random baseline), 50% of touches show true reversion vs 5% for random, AND a strong positive live EV (+$33/tr, N=56) — the cleanest alignment between statistical signature and trading result found all session. `5D_OR_MID` also came back consistently positive both directions (+$9.85 LONG / +$10.25 SHORT). IB_HIGH/IB_LOW show a real but modest reversion signature, unremarkable EV. **Important nuance**: low Hurst does NOT always mean profitable — `PW_LOW_FADE_SHORT`/`PW_VAL_FADE_SHORT`/`3M_POC_FADE_SHORT` are genuinely choppy (low Hurst) but lose big live (-$163, -$149/tr) — mean-reverting price action can still repeatedly stop out a specific fade's mechanics before the reversion completes. High-Hurst levels, though, uniformly show flat-to-negative EV — that direction of the signal is clean. **Not yet investigated further** — `MR2_FADE_LONG` in particular is worth a dedicated look next session (is it already live/tracked in `SETUP_STATUS`? what's its current recommendation status?).
- **GARCH volatility-scaled stop, extra validation passes**: a focused 150-day window (100-day warmup, most-recent-50 evaluation) was tested against the full-history GARCH result. Confirmed the walk-forward fitting logic is genuinely correct (no lookahead — verified directly in code). Result was informative but not fully consistent with the full-history test — TRIPLE actually got *worse* with scaling in this narrower window (vs. better in the full-history version), and the window itself wasn't rigor-clean. Root cause understood: **a GARCH model fit on only 100 days is noisier/less reliable than one fit on years of data** — a real methodological lesson, not a contradiction. The day-by-day GARCH scale factor did behave sensibly (smooth climb from 0.64 in late May to 1.22 by mid-July, tracking the known volatility regime shift) — the forecast mechanism itself is sound, a short fit window just isn't enough data to trust for trading decisions. **Also found (again) a real cross-script N/EV inconsistency**: last-22-day and last-50-day windows should nest (22 ⊂ 50) but told different-sign stories for TRIPLE's fixed-stop EV (-$12.05 vs +$4.38) — reinforces the existing "needs one canonical script before live integration" conclusion, not a new problem.
- **✅ Volatility regime tool wired to the confluence flash badge, live, same session.** Discovered mid-session that a real, separately-validated regime classifier already exists (`server/services/volatilityRegimeService.js`, live at `/api/acd/volatility-regime`, feeding the pre-existing `VolatilityRegimeCard.jsx`) — classifies each morning as HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL / HIGH-VOL-CHOP / NORMAL-VOL / LOW-VOL vs. a trailing 60-session baseline, with its own prior "Phase 1" backtest already confirming fades do meaningfully better in HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL and flat-to-worse in HIGH-VOL-CHOP — a direct, independent match to this session's own finding that the recent confluence-zone losses came from "wide, non-committal" (not trending) days. User chose to wire the *existing* tool into the flash badge rather than build a new GARCH-based live indicator (bigger lift, would have needed a Python-Node bridge or precompute cron — deliberately not built). Implementation: `SidebarVerdictChip` (`src/App.jsx`) now also polls `/api/acd/volatility-regime` (60s, shared hook); when a TRIPLE+/QUAD_PLUS zone is flashing AND today's regime reads `HIGH-VOL-CHOP`, the badge escalates from amber to red with a ⚠, and the expanded panel shows the regime read directly. Added `InfoTooltip` explanations throughout (what a "zone" is, what WR/EV/N mean, why the recent-month caution exists, what each regime label means) per explicit user request — the user found the raw statistical output opaque without them. Verified: endpoint live and responding correctly (`available:false` with a clear reason pre-15-bars, as expected), lint/build clean.
- **Python analysis stack established this session, first use in this repo**: `scratch/.venv` (Python 3.12, `arch`/`statsmodels`/`pandas`/`numpy`/`psycopg2-binary`) — system Python is externally-managed, this venv is the way to run any future Python analysis script. Five Python scripts now exist in `scripts/` alongside the JS ones: `backtest_confluence_garch_stop.py`, `backtest_confluence_mean_reversion.py`, `backtest_level_mean_reversion.py`.
- **Process lesson reconfirmed, not new**: another Gemini task this pass (`bv6hd4c2f`) returned a bash-level exit code 1 (a "failed" task notification) despite the underlying computation having genuinely completed correctly — the response file had a leftover "Error: timeout waiting for response" line from an earlier retry attempt, followed by a complete, real, verified report. Worth knowing: a non-zero exit code on `invoke_gemini.sh` doesn't automatically mean no usable output was produced — check the actual files on disk before assuming a clean re-dispatch is needed.

## 🆕 Level-rotation / confluence investigation, 2026-07-15 — one real finding, wired live; one confirmed dead; two things awaiting go-ahead

User noticed a real intraday bounce off a Floor Pivot (prior day) that then interacted with a Camarilla level on the return, and asked whether the system could have anticipated it. Turned into a multi-pass investigation, audited at every step (2 real Gemini bugs caught and fixed along the way, not just trusted). Net result:

- **Pure level-pair "rotation" (touch A, later touch B, does it change B's EV) — tested exhaustively, mostly noise.** Full pairwise sweep, all 64 level types × both directions, all-time data (414 trading days): only 148 of 13,926 pairs were both rigor-clean and profitable, and the single best Floor↔Camarilla candidate (`FLOOR_S1_SHORT → CAM_S2_SHORT`, N=618) was clearly decaying across its own chronological thirds ($26→$1.55→$0.92). **Conclusion: no tradeable Floor↔Camarilla rotation edge currently exists.** Scripts: `scripts/backtest_rotation.js` (all-time, `WINDOW_DAYS=1000`), results in `scratch/level_rotation_pairs.json`.
- **~~Rotation as a `sizeMultiplier` conditioning factor on existing live setups (not a new alert)~~ — tested, confirmed dead, not a bug, a real confound.** `scripts/rotation_sizing.mjs` initially found 380/2,177 candidates "clean+profitable" (implausibly high vs. the 1% base rate above) — traced to `RTH_VWAP` touches (either direction) boosting many unrelated setup_types by similar amounts, almost certainly rediscovering "fades work better on balance/choppy days" (already a known `CONTEXT_ANALYSIS` finding) through a noisy proxy rather than real touch-order signal. Gemini re-tested stratified by `day_type` as requested: sample sizes per cell collapse to 1-10 trades ("directional-only" everywhere), confirming the confound rather than resolving it. **Retired — do not pursue this angle further without a genuinely different hypothesis.**
- **✅ Confluence tier clustering — the one finding that survived, now wired live.** Pre-existing, never-rigor-checked data in `scripts/backtest_confluence.js` (weekly Sunday 6:30am cron, `performance_audit` signal_type='CONFLUENCE_AUDIT') showed SINGLE/DOUBLE/TRIPLE/QUAD_PLUS-tier EV at real scale (N=17,864/11,102/5,004/3,073). Gemini rigor-checked it properly and swept stop distance (60/90/120pt) and proximity (10/15/20pt): **at the original 90pt stop, SINGLE and DOUBLE actually fail `computeRigor()` (`stable:false`), and even TRIPLE was decaying within its own history. At a wider 120pt stop, TRIPLE becomes genuinely clean and stable (thirds: $14.32→$13.73→$11.30, not decaying), and the relative ordering TRIPLE > DOUBLE > SINGLE, with QUAD+ reverting worse than TRIPLE, holds consistently across every stop/proximity combination tested.** This is the one real, large-sample, rigor-surviving result from the whole investigation.
  - **Wired live same session**: new endpoint `GET /api/confluence/today-zones` (`server/routes/keyLevels.js`) chain-clusters today's `level_prices` values into TRIPLE+ zones (15pt band, matching the calibration), flags `flashing:true` when current price is within 15pt of a zone. Sidebar `SidebarVerdictChip` (`src/App.jsx`) shows a pulsing `◆ TRIPLE`/`◆ QUAD_PLUS` badge on the always-visible ENGAGE chip when a zone is flashing, and lists all of today's zones + reference WR/EV/N in the expandable panel — deliberately shown as adjacent context, not gating the real-time ENGAGE/WAIT/STAND_ASIDE verdict itself (that's a 30s-reassessed live signal; confluence zones are slow/structural, conflating the two timescales would make ENGAGE jumpy off stale-feeling data).
  - ~~**Verified working, but currently shows nothing live**: `level_prices` hasn't been computed since **2026-07-13** — `compute_levels.js`'s nightly cron has a 2-day gap.~~ **Resolved (confirmed 2026-07-17):** `level_prices.computed_at` is fresh through 2026-07-16 with 417 recent days of history — the cron gap self-healed or was fixed in an intervening session. No longer an issue.
  - **⚠️ Important, found same session: the recent month does NOT match the all-time numbers above — checked before trusting the feature further, per the user's own follow-up ask.** Gemini re-ran the exact live chain-clustering algorithm (not just `backtest_confluence.js`'s original touch-counting) scoped to the last 22 trading days (2026-06-12 to 2026-07-13): TRIPLE N=209 WR=61.2% (up from 52.3% all-time) but **EV=-$24.98/tr** (vs +$13.11 all-time); QUAD_PLUS N=329 WR=51.4% but **EV=-$18.04/tr** (vs +$12.01 all-time). Win rate went up, EV went deeply negative — classic "wins slightly more often, loses much bigger" signature of a mean-reversion fade blowing through its stop during a trending stretch. Root cause per `computeRigor()`: both tiers are heavily day-clustered recently (top5DayPct 60.3% / 54.4%, only 18-20 distinct days touched despite 209-329 total touches) — a handful of strongly-trending sessions in the last month sliced through multiple stacked zones without reverting. **22 days is a small window, so this isn't proof the all-time edge is dead** — but real enough that the sidebar badge/panel now shows this caution directly (amber badge instead of purple, explicit recent-month warning in the expanded panel) rather than presenting only the flattering all-time stats. Raw data: `scratch/confluence_recent_month.json`. **Worth re-checking again in a few weeks once there's a longer recent window to judge whether this was one bad stretch or a real regime change.**
  - **Process note for future Gemini dispatches**: the task that produced Check 1/2/3 (the rigor/day-type/parameter-sensitivity verification, referenced above) declared "AUTONOMOUS EXECUTION COMPLETED" in its response file but the underlying `agy` process kept running for 35+ minutes afterward, eventually wandering into an unrelated `psql \d acd_daily_log` query — the same rabbit-holing failure mode already documented elsewhere in this file, this time on the confirmed Pro-tier model (`Gemini 3.1 Pro (Low)`, not a Flash-tier regression). It held `scratch/.invoke_gemini.lock` the whole time, blocking the next dispatch until manually killed via `TaskStop`. **Lesson: a "done" declaration in `antigravity_response.md` is not proof the process has exited — check `ps aux | grep agy` (or that the invoking bash task itself has completed) before treating a task as finished, not just the response file's content.**
  - **Deep-dive into the recent-month loss, same session, four follow-on Gemini tasks — root cause found, one real (partial) fix validated, two other angles ruled out:**
    1. **Real PnL/MAE-MFE breakdown** (`scratch/confluence_recent_month_detail.json`): real aggregate PnL over the 22 days was -$3,569 (TRIPLE) / -$1,482 (QUAD_PLUS), 1 contract. MAE/MFE distribution showed the actual mechanism cleanly: winners have a clean, modest MAE (p50=35pt) before a real MFE run (p50=76pt past the 30pt target); losers have a **catastrophic median MAE of 165pt** — well past the 120pt stop, p90=258pt. **Correction to an earlier same-session claim**: the top clustering dates are mostly `day_type='BALANCE'` (4 of 5 for TRIPLE), not `TREND` as first assumed — but with realized ranges (465/318/303/403pt) well above the ~350pt normal, i.e. **wide-swinging, non-committal days, not trending ones**. Confirmed independently via direct SQL: last-22-day avg RTH range = **503.2pt vs. 397.7pt** for the prior 158 days (~0.5σ elevation) — a real, quantified volatility regime shift, not an assumption.
    2. **Entry-logic refinement, tested and ruled out** (`scratch/confluence_entry_logic_findings.json`, `scripts/backtest_confluence_entry_logic.js`): tried filtering entries to `touchQuality.js`'s ABSORBED bucket (the same classifier already validated live elsewhere in this codebase) and a 1/2/3-bar confirmation delay. **Neither rescues the recent losses.** OVERRUN touches confirmed worse than baseline both all-time and recently (supports the classifier's general validity), but ABSORBED touches were too rare in the volatile recent window (N=4 in 22 days) to serve as a usable filter — the good bucket is scarcest exactly when you'd most want it. Confirmation-delay made things monotonically *worse* with more delay (0-bar EV -$38.53 → 3-bar -$82.34) — waiting doesn't distinguish "about to reverse" from "about to blow through" in a whipsaw regime. **Known caveat**: this script used `backtest_confluence.js`'s own default `WINDOW_DAYS=180` and a 40pt target rather than the all-time/30pt convention — absolute EVs in this run aren't directly comparable to the original +$13.11 baseline, only the *relative* ABSORBED-vs-OVERRUN-vs-QUIET comparison within this run is trustworthy.
    3. **Zone-memory / "was this specific zone defended last time" — tested, inconclusive, not usable.** (`scratch/confluence_zone_memory_findings.json`, `scripts/backtest_confluence_zone_memory.js`) User's own hypothesis: does a zone's own recent (5-day lookback) defended-vs-broken history predict this touch's outcome. All-time: small, real, rigor-clean effect in the expected direction (`PRIOR_DEFENDED` N=621 outperforms `PRIOR_BROKEN` N=179 by ~$9/tr). Recent-22-day: sample collapses to N=21/31, severely day-clustered (87-90% top5, `clean:false`) — the recent data actually inverts the pattern, but it's not trustworthy enough to call a real anti-signal, just noise from a thin, unusual stretch. **Not validated as a way to have avoided last month's losses.**
    4. **✅ GARCH-based volatility-scaled stop — tested, genuinely helps, doesn't fully fix it.** (`scratch/confluence_garch_stop_findings.json`, `scripts/backtest_confluence_garch_stop.py` — **first Python script in this repo**; new venv at `scratch/.venv` with `arch`/`statsmodels`/`pandas`/`numpy`/`psycopg2-binary` installed, since system Python is externally-managed). Walk-forward GARCH(1,1) fit on daily RTH log-returns (`hist_ret = returns.iloc[:i]` — verified directly in the script, genuinely no-lookahead, refit daily not just periodically), stop scaled by `forecast_vol / baseline_vol` instead of fixed at 120pt. Result: **real, consistent improvement in every metric** — all-time EV +18-19% (TRIPLE $13.66→$16.10, QUAD_PLUS $14.46→$17.22, rigor stays clean/stable both ways), recent-22-day losses reduced (TRIPLE -$12.05→-$8.44/tr, ~30% smaller loss; QUAD_PLUS flips from +$1.29 to +$5.78/tr). **TRIPLE's recent EV stays negative even scaled** — real improvement, not a full fix; the recent regime is genuinely tough for that specific fade even accounting for volatility. **Known caveat, disclosed by Gemini itself unprompted**: touch counts and $ figures drifted across all four of tonight's follow-on scripts (all-time TRIPLE N ranged 1,735 to 9,176 depending on the script's exact window/target choices) — the *direction* of every finding has been consistent across every re-test, but no single script tonight is the fully-reconciled canonical version. **Next step, not done this session**: consolidate into one clean, canonical backtest (one touch-detection definition, one window, one target) before this goes anywhere near live `sizeMultiplier` integration — don't promote any one of tonight's four scripts as-is.
  - **Not done**: no dedicated evidence-browsing table yet (user asked "is there a table where I can review the results that get fed into this tool" — answered honestly that there isn't one; the data lives in `performance_audit` signal_type='CONFLUENCE_AUDIT' plus the new Gemini-verified per-stop-distance breakdown in `scratch/verification_findings.json`, neither surfaced in any UI). Reasonable next piece, not built this session.
  - **Not done**: `backtest_confluence.js` itself still has no `computeRigor()` call and is still hardcoded to `FADE_STOP=90` — the rigor-checked, multi-stop-distance version only exists as Gemini's one-off verification script this session, not promoted into the actual weekly cron. Should be: update `backtest_confluence.js` to import `computeRigor()`, default to the 120pt stop (the one that's actually clean), and keep writing `signal_type='CONFLUENCE_AUDIT'` so the endpoint's hardcoded `tierStats` reference block (`server/routes/keyLevels.js`, currently hand-typed from this session's one-off Gemini numbers) can be read live from `performance_audit` instead — same "never hand-type a stat" hard rule everything else in this codebase follows, currently violated by that hardcoded block as a known, deliberate shortcut for this session.
- **Awaiting explicit go-ahead, not yet done**: a `DAILY_DELTA` digest (`scripts/weekly_delta_digest.mjs`, rewritten and tested this session — fixed a date-scoping bug that silently produced empty results, fixed a `varchar(20)` overflow, added real viability gating replacing a static `$5` threshold with one derived from each setup's own rigor-thirds volatility) is built and correct but has never been run against the live `performance_audit` table — the harness's own auto-mode classifier blocked the write as a live-shared-table modification needing explicit named consent, correctly per this repo's own carve-out for hard-to-reverse/shared-state changes. **User has not yet said go ahead on this specific write.**

## ~~🆕 Found while doing the above: ~1,900 lines of unreachable dead code in ACDView.jsx + 5 App.jsx-imported components never rendered anywhere — not fixed, flagged for a dedicated cleanup session~~ — **Resolved 2026-07-16**

**Actual scope was bigger than this entry originally documented — the prior session's own claim that `ConditionRow`/`TRADING_GUIDANCE`/`COND_TOOLTIPS` "ARE genuinely alive (used by `BigPictureSnapshot` etc.)" was itself wrong, the exact same one-level-too-shallow mistake this entry was written to describe.** Re-walked the reachability graph from `ACDView`'s actual render tree (not just grep-for-a-caller) and found `e8946e5` had ALSO deleted `DashboardPanels` in the same commit as `SessionStatusBar` — and `DashboardPanels` was the only caller of `BigPictureSnapshot`, `ConfluenceScore`, `CounterTrendPanel`, `ConditionRow` (only reachable via `ConfluenceScore`), `StructureInline`, and `PhaseChangeMonitor`. So there were two orphaned clusters from that one commit, not one — the `SessionStatusBar`→`AuctionReadCard` chain this file already knew about, plus the `DashboardPanels`→`BigPictureSnapshot`/`ConfluenceScore`/etc. chain nobody had caught. Confirmed via `git show e8946e5` diff directly (both `<BigPictureSnapshot .../>` and `<ConfluenceScore .../>` render call sites lived inside the deleted `DashboardPanels` body) and via a fresh JSX-render-site grep against the current file (zero matches for any of these names anywhere in the live tree).

**Fixed**: removed the full dead cluster from `src/views/ACDView.jsx` — `TradeTimelinePanel`, `ACDSessionTimeline`, `ACDSetupReference`, `generatePhase1Narrative`, `generatePhase2Narrative`, `generatePreMarketBias`, `buildLtContextSentence`, `generateSessionBias`, `buildAuctionExplanations`, `ThisSetupHistorically`, `NarrativeBlocks`, `AuctionReadDetailModal`, `computeContractRec`, `AuctionReadCard`, `MidDaySection`, `EODReadSection`, `TRADING_GUIDANCE`, `COND_TOOLTIPS`, `ConditionRow`, `StructureInline`, `ConfluenceScore`, `CounterTrendPanel`, `BigPictureSnapshot`, `PhaseChangeMonitor` — 2,725 lines total (4,310 → 1,585). Also deleted the 5 orphaned `App.jsx`-imported components confirmed dead in the original entry (`BalanceZonePanel`, `TradeCalibrationCard`, `ScalpPlaybookCard`, `PreSessionChecklist`, `LiveScriptsCard` — re-confirmed zero JSX render sites anywhere in `src/` before deleting the files outright, not just removing the imports) and their now-unused imports in `App.jsx`. `npm run lint:frontend` and `npm run build` both clean before and after; `ACDView.jsx`'s own bundle size barely moved (Rollup was already tree-shaking these as unreferenced pure declarations), consistent with them being genuinely dead rather than side-effecting. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s frontend structure table and line-count updated with the root-cause note.

## START HERE — priority order as of 2026-07-15 (end of session, continued a third time)

**Morning Prep initial-load settle time: found the real dominant cause (a `useSharedPollData` bug), fixed it, went from ~14-16s to ~6-11s.** User pushed back hard on "14-16s isn't good enough, find the actual cause" — right call. Investigated with live Playwright request-tracing rather than more speculation:

- **Root cause: `useSharedPollData.js` (the hook built earlier this session specifically to fix redundant fetching) had its own bug that let it multiply requests instead of deduping them.** It only deduped the *recurring poll interval*; the *initial* load had a race — any subscriber mounting before the first fetch resolved saw `entry.data` still `null` and called `load()` itself. With 8 real components now subscribing to `/api/antigravity/edges-context` (up from 4 earlier this session) × React StrictMode's 2x dev-mode effect invocation, that was **16 separate requests for one URL on a single page load** — confirmed via direct request tracing (`page.on('request', ...)`), not assumed. Fixed with an `entry.loading` in-flight guard in `load()` itself — one-line-of-intent fix, no call-site changes needed. This bug affected every existing consumer of the hook (PermSlipAndStackBar, LivePlaybookCard, OvernightContextStrip, EdgeSectionsPanel), not just new ones added this session.
- **Also found and fixed while auditing duplicate fetches (the user's "what can we cut" question led directly here):**
  - `TeleprinterFeed.jsx` was a 5th independent, non-deduped fetcher of `edges-context` (still using the old `usePollData` hook) — missed by the original 4-component consolidation. Converted to the shared hook.
  - `/api/morning-brief/live-session-context/:date` was independently fetched by **6 separate components** (TradeAlertBanner, SessionPulseCard, VolatilityRegimeCard, SessionForecastPanel ×2, LivePlaybookCard) — same class of bug as edges-context, just never caught. All converted to the shared hook.
  - `/api/antigravity/edges-context` was ALSO independently fetched (not via the shared hook) by SessionBiasPanel and BehavioralPatternsCard — 2 more that the original consolidation missed. Converted.
  - `/api/morning-brief/forecast/:date` was independently fetched by **3 places**: `App.jsx` (turned out to be genuine **dead code** — fetched into a `forecast` state that was never read anywhere, plus an unused `currentDate`/`setCurrentDate` pair that existed only to feed it — deleted entirely, not just deduped), `ACDView.jsx`, and `SessionForecastPanel.jsx`. The latter two converted to the shared hook (same cache key, so they now hit one shared subscription instead of two separate fetches).
  - `VolatilityRegimeCard.jsx`'s other two polling effects (`/api/acd/volatility-regime` 10s, itself not duplicated elsewhere) were still ungated by `useViewActive` — a gap from earlier in this session, fixed while already in this file.
- **Net measured result**: total API requests fired on one Morning Prep page load dropped from ~115-118 to ~86-102 (varies run to run); full visual settle time (via Playwright, watching for any still-visible "Loading…" text) dropped from a consistent 12-16s to a 6-11s range across repeated runs — hit the user's stated 5-6s target on at least one run, not yet consistently.
- **Not fixed, next candidate, higher risk than the others**: `/api/setups/today` is still independently fetched by 3 places (`App.jsx`, `PermSlipAndStackBar.jsx`, `ACDView.jsx`'s `EdgeSectionsPanel`) — 6 requests/load for one endpoint, same pattern as everything above. Not deduped this pass because `App.jsx`'s fetcher (`loadActiveSetups`) is entangled with a shared `useEffect` that also drives 3 *other* loads plus two socket-event handlers (`onExpired`/`onResolved`) that call it with a delay — safe deduplication here needs more careful handling than the straightforward polling-only cases fixed above, not a quick pass.
- **Confirmed still real and unreduced**: even after all the above, `VolatilityRegimeCard` is consistently the last widget to settle. Traced via request/response timing (not assumed): its own `/api/acd/volatility-regime` request dispatches within ~700ms of page load but the *response* doesn't arrive for 3-9s, despite that same endpoint responding in ~80ms when curled directly with no contention. This confirms the remaining bottleneck is still Chrome's per-origin connection queue — now ~86-102 concurrent requests instead of ~118, better but still well over the 6-connection HTTP/1.1 budget. Reducing raw request *count* further (the `setups/today` case above, and any other still-undiscovered duplicates) is the only lever left that doesn't require HTTP/2 or domain-sharding (both discussed and deliberately not done this session — see the earlier entry on the connection-starvation fix for why).
- **Also fixed this pass, before the above was found (compounding wins, not superseded)**: `/api/acd/setup-detection` and `/api/confluence/today` each got a short-lived (20s / 30s) full-response cache on top of their request-coalescing locks — `setup-detection` in particular now reuses the result from `server/index.js`'s existing autonomous 15s poller (which runs independent of any browser during 9:30-4 PM ET, previously computed and discarded), so a real page load landing inside that window gets a near-instant cached response (measured: 0.002-0.01s) instead of paying the full ~7-10s per-call cost again. Also batched two more sequential-query N+1 sections inside `resolveSetupsByPrice`'s neighboring code and the "Pre-fetch: overnight reads + prior setups" section (7 independent sequential queries → one `Promise.all`) — this second batch was profiled as the single largest remaining contributor to `setup-detection`'s own per-call cost (was 5.7s of a ~9-15s total, now ~2.1-2.9s in steady state).

## START HERE — priority order as of 2026-07-15 (end of session, continued again)

**Root cause of "Morning Prep components take forever to load" and "Trades page is slow" found and fixed — a genuine self-inflicted pile-up, not connection limits.** User reported the app still felt unusable after the earlier connection-starvation/day-caching work — investigated the ACTUAL complained-about flows directly rather than re-trusting prior fixes:

- **`/api/trades` ("All Trades" list) was returning a 51MB uncompressed JSON blob** — all 40,453 fill rows in the database, unbounded, every load. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` showed the raw query itself is ~1.1s (disk-spilling sort under default 4MB `work_mem` — cuts to ~350ms at 128MB, not changed globally, just diagnostic), but the full request took ~5s, dominated by Node-side JSON serialization of 40k rows, not the DB. Added `compression` (gzip) globally to `server/index.js` — correct practice but barely moved total time (5.0s→4.3s) since serialization CPU, not transfer size, was the bottleneck. **Real fix**: cached the endpoint's response (`server/routes/trades.js`, keyed `all-trades-list`, on the existing `lib/cache.js` helper), invalidated explicitly on every trade mutation (create/update/delete/screenshot-upload) AND at the actual DB-insert point (`tradeImportService.js`'s `importSierraTrades()`, since `sierraWatcher.js`'s automatic file-watch import bypasses the manual `/api/sierra/import` route entirely — invalidating only at the route level would have missed that path). Also cache the pre-*stringified* JSON, not just the JS array, since `res.json()` re-runs `JSON.stringify` on every call regardless of caching — for a 51MB payload that alone is ~0.6-1s. Cold load ~2-5s (unavoidable — first computation after data changes), warm ~0.4s (was ~5s). Byte-identical verified before/after.
- **`/api/acd/setup-detection` (SizeChip in MarketPulseBar.jsx, polled every 15s, plus several other Morning Prep cards on mount) takes ~8-10s per call — a real, so-far-unreduced cost — but was measuring 13-24s and occasionally longer under normal use because concurrent/overlapping polls were piling up and starving each other for the same DB connections.** This project's own history (this file, "touch-quality" section, item 5) had already flagged "no lock against overlapping invocations" as a known risk on this exact endpoint and never fixed it. Confirmed via direct server-side profiling (temporary `console.time` checkpoints around each section, removed after): `resolveSetupsByPrice`/`expireStaleSetups`/`structurallyInvalidateSetups` combined ~0.3-0.6s (see next bullet for a real fix here too), the big 10-query `Promise.all` ~2.3-3.3s, "Unified Level Fade Setups" section ~1-1.5s, the section after it through persistence ~1.3-1.8s — none of these individually explain 13-24s, but *overlapping concurrent executions* competing for the same connection pool do. **Fixed: request-coalescing lock** — a module-level in-flight promise (`setupDetectionInFlight` in `acd.js`) so concurrent callers share one computation instead of each starting their own; verified with 5 simultaneous curl calls completing together in ~7.8s total with byte-identical (md5-matched) responses, instead of 5 independent ~13-24s+ computations competing with each other. Applied the identical pattern to **`/api/confluence/today`** (`server/routes/confluence.js`) — also ~5-6s/call, also fetched independently by 3+ Morning Prep components on mount (StructureInline, ConfluenceScore, AuctionReadCard), same fix, same verification (3 concurrent calls, md5-identical, ~4.5s total instead of 3×5-6s). Only `res.json`/`res.status` are called in either handler (checked before applying) — a minimal fake-res object capturing just those two methods is a safe, verified-correct substitute for coalesced callers.
- **Also fixed while in `resolveSetupsByPrice`**: a genuine N+1 — one "bars since fired_at" query per unresolved setup (~18-20 on a normal day), each independently re-scanning `price_bars_primary`'s full ~40-partition set (its `ts` column is a `date_trunc()` expression over the base table, not the raw partition key, so partition pruning doesn't trigger even though per-partition row-filtering does — confirmed via `pg_get_viewdef`). Every setup's needed bars are a suffix of the earliest unresolved setup's own range (all queries run "from fired_at through now"), so batched into one shared fetch from the single earliest relevant `fired_at`, filtered per-setup in JS afterward — same exact rows each setup would have gotten, just one DB round trip instead of ~18. `ts` fetched as `::text` (not a Date object) to match the same ET/UTC-landmine-avoidance convention already established for `fired_at` in this exact function.
- **Not fixed, real remaining cost, next lever for a future session**: even fully coalesced (no pile-up), a single fresh call to `/api/acd/setup-detection` is still ~8-10s, and Morning Prep's full initial-load settle time is still ~14-16s (down from "sometimes never" pre-fix, verified via Playwright, but not fast). The big `Promise.all` (~2.3-3.3s) and "Unified Level Fade Setups" (~1-1.5s) sections are the next things to profile deeper if this needs to come down further — not attempted this pass given how much of this session was already spent finding the pile-up mechanism itself.
- **Also corrected mid-session**: the `src/constants/api.js` origin-split (direct `:3002` fetch instead of the Vite `/api` proxy) was briefly reverted over an unverified WSL2/VS-Code-port-forwarding worry, then re-applied after the user confirmed they access the app via plain `http://localhost:3000` — WSL2's default `localhostForwarding` auto-forwards every port a process listens on, not just the one visited first, so `:3002` should be reachable the same way. Flagged in that file's own comment: if this ever manifests as "page loads but every API call fails/hangs," check `.wslconfig`'s `localhostForwarding` setting before reverting again — don't re-guess blindly.
- **Explicitly not the cause, re-confirmed this session**: the App.jsx code-split (lazy-loading). Diffed already in a prior session (byte-identical mount logic pre/post-split) and re-verified the reasoning holds — code-splitting only affects initial bundle size, it can't produce a 51MB API response or an unbounded N+1 query. The timing coincidence (pain increasing after the split) tracks with more panels being added and the trades table crossing 40k rows around the same time, not the split itself.

## START HERE — priority order as of 2026-07-15 (end of session, continued)

**#1 priority, frontend render/navigation architecture — Option 1 (keep views mounted, CSS-hide) implemented.** `App.jsx` now tracks a `visitedViews` Set; once a tab is visited it renders permanently with `display:contents`/`none` toggling instead of being removed from the tree, so revisiting a tab is a pure CSS swap (no re-fetch, no state loss). Verified: Dashboard and Morning Prep revisits resolve in <1.2s with zero re-fetches and zero console errors (Playwright-driven, not just static checks).

**Real regression found and fixed before shipping this**: keeping every view mounted means every view's background `setInterval` polling also keeps running forever in the background instead of stopping on unmount — first pass of this change left ACDView's ~15 pollers (MarketPulseBar, AuctionReadCard, EdgeSectionsPanel, etc.) running even while hidden, which is wasted server load and, worse, competes for browser connections with whatever you navigate to next. Fixed with `src/utils/useViewActive.js` (`ViewActiveProvider`/`useViewActive()`) — each polling effect now gates on `isViewActive` (dependency-based: stops the interval while hidden, fires a fresh load immediately on becoming visible again rather than waiting out the interval). Wired into `ACDView.jsx` (10 polling sites), `MarketPulseBar.jsx`, `PermSlipAndStackBar.jsx`, `LivePlaybookCard.jsx` — everywhere `useSharedPollData`/`setInterval` is used inside the Morning Prep tree. Two bounded job-status polls (`ACDBacktestRunner`, `PhaseChangeBacktestPanel` — tracking an explicit user-triggered background job to completion) were deliberately left ungated, since pausing those while the user is on another tab would be the wrong behavior (they want the job to keep progressing).

**⚠️→✅ Separate, pre-existing bug found while verifying the above (NOT introduced by it, confirmed by reproducing it on unmodified `main`/pre-change code too) — root-caused AND fixed same session.** Clicking to the Trades tab while Morning Prep had been open a few seconds (i.e. after its background polling started cycling) could leave Trades stuck on "Loading…" indefinitely — reproduced repeatedly, up to 24s+ with no resolution, both before and after the `useViewActive` change above. Root-caused via a live Playwright session: an external `curl` to the exact same stuck URL (`/src/views/AllTradesView.jsx`) from a separate process returned instantly (`200`, 10ms) while the browser tab was still stuck on it — proving the server (Vite dev server / Express backend) was never hung. Root cause: **Chrome's HTTP/1.1 6-connections-per-origin cap.** Morning Prep's own concurrent polling traffic was routed through Vite's dev proxy — same origin (`localhost:3000`) as the app's own module-serving — so it saturated the browser's connection budget for that origin, leaving a newly-requested module fetch queued behind it indefinitely if the polling traffic didn't let up.
- **Fix implemented (of the two candidates considered): split the API traffic onto its own origin, bypassing the point of contention entirely.** All ~37 frontend files' `const API_URL = '/api'` (relative, same-origin-as-modules) replaced with a shared `src/constants/api.js` export that fetches the backend *directly* (`http://<hostname>:3002/api`, bypassing Vite's `/api` proxy). Chrome's connection cap is *per origin*, so API traffic (`:3002`) and module-serving traffic (`:3000`) now each get their own separate 6-connection budget instead of fighting over one. No workflow change for the user — still `http://localhost:3000`, no cert warnings — since the backend (`server/index.js`) already has permissive CORS (`app.use(cors())`) and no cookie/session auth to complicate cross-origin credentials. The HTTP/2 alternative (removes the cap entirely via multiplexing) was *not* implemented — it would require HTTPS on both origins (Vite dev server AND the Express backend) for full effect, a bigger workflow change (self-signed cert acceptance) for a problem the origin-split already resolves.
- **Verified fixed, not just theorized:** the exact repro (settle 6-8s on Morning Prep with polling active, then click Trades) resolved in <2s across repeated runs post-fix, vs. never resolving within 24s+ pre-fix. Broader human-paced multi-tab test (Morning Prep → Dashboard → Trades → back to Morning Prep → back to Trades, with realistic reading pauses) also came back clean — every transition resolves (typically <1s for CSS-only revisits, occasionally a few seconds on the heaviest concurrent-load moments, never stuck). Zero new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` across the whole verification pass.
- **Complementary fix, same session: extended day-stable query caching (the other still-undone lever from the profiling pass) to shrink the residual queueing window.** ~16 queries across `morningBrief.js`/`sessionForecastService.js` were profiled 2026-07-15 as day-stable (bounded `< date`, recomputed from scratch on every poll) vs. genuinely live — this was flagged as "not yet acted on" earlier the same session. Implemented now: `morningBrief.js`'s 7 `getTrailing*` helpers (cumDeltas, 24hr/weekly VWAP dists, rotations, OR widths, delta30 p50, ATR) each cache their result keyed by `(date, lookback)` on the existing shared `server/lib/cache.js` helper (`cacheGet`/`cacheSet` — already used by `acd.js`/`auctionRead.js`/`keyLevels.js`/etc., reused rather than reimplemented a 4th time) with a 12h TTL, since every one of them is strictly bounded to data *before* `date` and therefore immutable once that date is in the past. `sessionForecastService.js`'s `getSessionForecast()` is cached as a whole (5min TTL, not 12h) — its overnight-H/L query is the one exception that reads *today's own* still-accumulating premarket bars when called same-day, so a long TTL risked freezing a stale partial range; 5min still absorbs Morning Prep's concurrent-mount burst without a real staleness risk. `cacheSet` in `lib/cache.js` was changed to return its `val` (previously returned `undefined`) so call sites can `return cacheSet(key, result, ttl)` inline — checked no existing caller depended on the old `undefined` return. Verified byte-for-byte identical output between a cold and warm call to `/api/morning-brief/live-session-context` (excluding live fields). This doesn't reduce the *number* of browser-side connections Morning Prep opens (that's candidate fix #2 below, still not done), but it does shrink how long each cached query holds a connection open, which shrinks the queueing delay for whatever's waiting behind it.
- **Residual, much milder, not fixed:** individual widgets that fetch endpoints *not* covered by the above (e.g. `VolatilityRegimeCard`'s own `/api/acd/volatility-regime`/`/api/acd/vol-backtest-stats`) can still take several seconds to show their first data during the heaviest initial concurrent-mount burst, confirmed via React-fiber inspection to be an isolated small stat-card loading state, not a broken/blank view. Backend confirmed fast in isolation (0.24s via direct `curl`) — the delay is purely the browser's own connection queue for the ~20-30 simultaneous requests Morning Prep fires on mount. Next lever, not started: **reduce the actual number of concurrent client-side `fetch()` calls** — Morning Prep fires ~20-30 separate HTTP requests on mount (one per subcomponent), several already hitting the same data (see the `useSharedPollData` consolidation from earlier the same session). Consolidating more of these into fewer combined endpoints would shrink this further without touching any origin/dev-server config.
- Reproduction script used throughout (Playwright + Chromium, no project skill existed for this yet — see `/run-skill-generator` note): wait ~6-8s after landing on Morning Prep, click Trades, watch for the Suspense fallback (`text=Loading…`, filtered to actually-visible elements) clearing or not.

2. **Medium, well-scoped, not urgent:**
   - ~~`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx`'s hardcoded `suppressed` narrative list (~line 465)~~ — **Fixed 2026-07-15.** See docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md item 11 for the full account — now renders live from `setupStatus.suppressed` instead of ~10 hand-typed, badly-drifted per-setup entries.
   - **Page/API loading — investigated 2026-07-15, real root cause found, one fix attempted and reverted after making it much worse.** Previously "not started"; picked up per explicit user request after code review flagged `/api/acd/setup-detection`'s hot-path exposure.
     - **`/api/acd/setup-detection` is no longer a concern** — re-measured at a consistent ~1.4s (well within its 14s poll timeout), down from the previously documented ~10-17s. Not a deliberate fix; most open setups already have `touch_quality` cached from earlier in the session, so the new classification block mostly no-ops now. Worth re-checking after a period with more fresh setups.
     - **`/api/performance-audit/unified` (~18s) — root cause is NOT a missing index.** `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the endpoint's main audit query (the `performance_audit` GROUP BY + JOIN) showed it taking **44ms** — not the bottleneck at all, despite the table having grown to 59,772 rows. The real cost is a genuine N+1 round-trip pattern in the "Recent 10-day and 30-day per-level performance (quick replay)" section (`server/routes/acd.js` ~line 6888): 4 sequential queries per date × 30 dates ≈ 120 round-trips. Confirmed directly: the single `pdIbQ10` query alone measured 370-565ms per call — ×30 ≈ 11-17s, accounting for nearly all of the observed total.
     - **Attempted fix: batch the 30 dates' queries into 1-2 round trips instead of ~120 — backfired badly, reverted same session.** Rewrote the per-date `dv`/`pdIbQ10`/`pdOrQ10` lookups as a single query using `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` across all 30 unnested dates at once. Verified *logically correct* first (4 sample dates' old-vs-new outputs matched exactly, byte-for-byte) — but `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the real batched query showed **~114 seconds**, ~6x worse than the ~18s original. Root cause: the IB lookup's correlated subquery (`MAX(ts::date) FROM price_bars_primary WHERE ts::date < d.dt ...`), run against `price_bars_primary` — a view over ~40 monthly partitions — defeated partition pruning once wrapped in a `LATERAL` join executed once per outer date row; Postgres fell back to a parallel sequential scan of every partition, 30 times over, plus ~11s of JIT compilation overhead from the resulting enormous plan. **Reverted in the same session** — restored the original per-date sequential-query version, confirmed back to the known ~18s baseline (not worse, not better), no new server errors. Two queries that were still running when the regression was caught (~58s in) were cleared by restarting the server (drops the app's DB connections, Postgres aborts on connection loss) rather than calling `pg_cancel_backend` directly on unverified PIDs, per a permission guardrail — the right call, not a workaround.
     - **Lesson for whoever picks this up next**: batching N per-date queries into one is not automatically a win against this codebase's partitioned `price_bars_primary` view — a correlated/LATERAL construct can defeat partition pruning in a way a simple `WHERE ts::date = ANY($1::date[])` filter (used elsewhere in this same fix, e.g. the bars-batching half, and never actually shown to be the problem) does not. **Always `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` the exact batched query against the real partitioned view before trusting a batching rewrite**, the same way this pass caught the regression before it could sit live — don't assume "fewer round trips = faster" for anything touching `price_bars_primary` without checking the plan first.
     - ~~**Not yet done**: the N+1 pattern in the 30-day replay section~~ — **Fixed 2026-07-15, same session, second attempt.** Root cause of the first attempt's disaster was specifically the correlated IB lookback against the *partitioned* `price_bars_primary` view — the fix avoids that table entirely for this lookup: `IB_HIGH`/`IB_LOW` now come from `level_prices` (precomputed nightly by `scripts/compute_levels.js`, already using the canonical 60-min IB window, on a plain non-partitioned indexed table). `developing_value_log`/`acd_daily_log` lookups (never the slow part) got batched into the same `LATERAL` query since neither is partitioned — no risk there.
       - **Independently verified via Gemini before touching live code** — dispatched a precise, self-contained verification task (not open-ended): run `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on the proposed query, and a full 30-date correctness comparison against the original per-date queries. Gemini reported "PROCEED" — `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` under 1ms (independently re-confirmed by Claude directly: 0.966ms, no `price_bars_primary` anywhere in the plan), 24/30 dates matching exactly, 6 dates showing IB high/low discrepancies "as anticipated" in the task prompt.
       - **Audited Gemini's output rather than trusting it outright, per the standing rule — and caught a real error in Gemini's own report.** Gemini's comparison table's values for the 6 flagged dates didn't reproduce when independently re-checked directly (e.g. Gemini reported `2026-06-19 ib_h` old=30644/new=30545.75, but a direct requery showed `2026-06-17`'s values in that slot — an off-by-one date misalignment in Gemini's own comparison script, not a real query bug). Re-ran the comparison directly rather than trust the reported table.
       - **The direct re-check surfaced something more important than value drift: a genuine missing-row gap.** For `2026-06-19`, the OLD (`price_bars_primary`) and NEW (`level_prices`) sources didn't even agree on *which* prior trading day to use as the reference — `price_bars_primary` correctly found `2026-06-18` (61 real bars in the IB window), but `level_prices` had **zero rows at all** for `2026-06-18`, silently falling back to `2026-06-17` instead. Confirmed via `level_prices` row count: 0. This is the exact "`compute_levels.js --backfill` silently skips dates under concurrent DB load" failure mode already documented elsewhere in this codebase's history (docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md), not a new bug — just a new date it happened to. Scanned the surrounding 35-day window for other gaps: only `2026-06-18` (the other flagged "gap," `2026-07-14`, is just today — expected to be missing until tonight's cron, and never gets used as a prior-day reference since it's the most recent date). Fixed directly: `node scripts/compute_levels.js 2026-06-18` — re-verified the `2026-06-19` lookback now matches exactly between both sources.
       - **The remaining 4 dates' small point-level discrepancies (9-50pt) were not investigated further** — both sources agree on which prior day to use for those, just differ slightly in the IB high/low value itself (likely a stale snapshot from before some later data correction, or a minor methodology difference between `compute_levels.js` and an ad-hoc live query — not diagnosed). Given this is an informational 10d/30d replay stat, not a live trading decision, and the definitional/window logic itself is already correct (confirmed via the exact-match dates and the fixed missing-row case), this residual drift was accepted rather than chased further — flagging here rather than silently ignoring it, in case it's worth a fuller `level_prices` freshness audit sometime (a broader version of this exists already in the "checklist-5-9 sweep" large-scope item elsewhere in this file).
       - **Result**: `/api/performance-audit/unified` went from ~18s to **~3.1s** (measured live, post-restart) — the remaining ~3s is the endpoint's many *other* sequential-but-individually-fast queries (this fix only targeted the confirmed dominant N+1 section, never claimed to be the only cost). Verified end-to-end: response shape intact, 49 setups show non-zero `n10d`/`wr10d` (the exact stat this section computes), no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.

3. **Large, genuinely new scope (not a continuation of today, needs a dedicated session):**
   - **Touch-quality — price-action pilot RUN 2026-07-15, mixed/inconclusive result, order-flow approach not yet tried.** User asked: can we distinguish a *real touch* (price approaches a level and shows a genuine reaction — absorption, a bounce, a pause) from *price just running through* a level with no real test? Piloted per the plan below on 3 high-N setup_types (`OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` N=295, `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` N=141, `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` N=245) via `scripts/pilot_touch_quality.mjs` — exploratory only, nothing written to `performance_audit`/`active_setups`, no corpus-wide rollout.
       - **`bars_to_first_favorable_tick` does not discriminate at 1-min bar resolution.** 93-99% of reacted trades tick favorable within the very first bar after touch (tercile cuts collapsed to `fast<=1, mid<=1`) — a fade signal, by construction, is entered near a swing extreme, so almost every trade shows *some* favorable wick immediately regardless of whether it goes on to win or lose. This metric isn't the right lens; abandon it rather than force a bucket split that's mostly noise.
       - **The pure "never ticks favorable before stop" bucket is real but small and largely tautological**: n=11/4/1 across the 3 types, 0% WR each — expected, since among `STOP_HIT`/`TARGET_HIT`-only rows, never-favorable within this sample structurally implies `STOP_HIT`. Confirms the concept exists but adds nothing beyond what `resolution` already encodes.
       - **The MAE-magnitude split (adverse excursion accumulated before the first favorable tick — "clean rejection" vs. "slow grind-then-reverse") gave inconsistent, setup-type-dependent results, not a universal filter:**
         - `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`: strong signal in the hypothesized direction — clean-reject WR=76.6% EV=+$17.01 vs. grind-reverse WR=60.8% EV=**-$10.64** (N=124/120). A real, actionable split for this one type.
         - `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`: no meaningful difference — WR=73.3%/71.7%, EV=$2.99/$2.11 (N=146/138).
         - `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG`: **inverted** — grind-reverse actually outperformed (WR=85.9% EV=$13.16 vs. clean-reject WR=72.6% EV=$3.16, N=64/73), the opposite of the hypothesis.
       - **Conclusion: touch-quality via price-action alone is not a clean, corpus-wide filter** — it may be a real per-setup_type refinement (worth keeping `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`-style checks in mind) but doesn't generalize on this 3-type pilot, so no corpus-wide backfill is warranted from this signal alone. Matches the contingency already anticipated below: since it didn't cleanly separate, next step is the order-flow approach, not a bigger price-action rollout.
       - **Order-flow based — RUN 2026-07-15, real corpus-wide signal found, then wired fully live the same day (user: "do what you think is best").** `scripts/pilot_touch_quality_orderflow.mjs` (exploratory pilot, superseded for production use by `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` below). Per explicit user guidance before building this: major structural levels draw genuine two-sided fighting, so heavy volume at a touch does NOT automatically mean "absorbed" — it can mean a fight the adverse side won. The classifier therefore uses 3 buckets, not 2: `HIGH_VOL_ABSORBED` (volume elevated vs. a 90-day trailing per-minute-of-day z-score baseline — reuses the existing `acd.js` VOLUME_SPIKE convention, no new static threshold — AND price holds), `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` (volume elevated but price keeps giving adverse ground anyway), `QUIET` (not elevated). Reaction window is data-derived per setup_type (p25 of that type's own bars-to-resolution).
         - **Piloted on 3 types first** (`OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG`, `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`): `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` was the worst bucket in all 3 (e.g. `CAM_S3_FADE_LONG` OVERRUN EV=**-$29.77** vs ABSORBED +$26.42 vs QUIET +$8.92) — consistent enough to justify a corpus-wide check.
         - **Dispatched corpus-wide extension to Gemini** (same exact methodology, N≥50-resolved setup_types, ~40-50 types) — first attempt timed out at the 15-min default (heavy per-setup-type/per-date query load, not a Gemini quality problem — confirmed via `agy` diagnostic: no `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, quick test prompt returned instantly). Retried with the documented 30-min "heavy backtest" budget, completed in 25 min, 47 setup_types.
         - **Independently re-verified by Claude, not just trusted** — re-ran the same script directly (added a day-clustering diagnostic Gemini's pass had skipped despite being asked for it) across all 47 types. Every setup_type Claude could cross-check matched Gemini's numbers **exactly** — real confidence in the methodology replication. **Zero of the 47 types show day-clustering** (top-5-dates share ranges 2-33%, all under the 50% danger line from the CAM_R4/CAM_S3 precedent) — this is not a clustering artifact.
         - **Corrected 2 of Gemini's own classification labels** by re-deriving them from its own reported numbers: `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_LONG` (OVERRUN EV=-$47.40 vs the other two both positive) and `CAM_R3_FADE_LONG` (OVERRUN EV=-$25.21 vs the other two near -$1 to -$2) were both mislabeled `NO_CLEAR_PATTERN` when they clearly fit `OVERRUN_BAD_CONSISTENT` by Gemini's own numbers. Real share of `OVERRUN_BAD_CONSISTENT` is closer to **~45%** of the 47 types, not Gemini's reported 40.4% — a minor correction, doesn't change the conclusion.
         - **Bottom line: `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` (heavy volume + price still gives adverse ground) is a real, common, non-clustered bad signal — but genuinely not universal, confirming the nuance flagged before building this.** ~45% of the 47 setup_types show it as clearly the worst bucket. But a real minority — `OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, `FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`, `CAM_S1_FADE_SHORT`, `CAM_R2_FADE_SHORT` — show the opposite: `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` performing fine or even best. So this cannot be a blanket corpus-wide rule; any live use needs a per-setup_type check, not a global one.
         - **Wired fully live 2026-07-15, informational only, not silent suppression** — user explicitly confirmed the live schema/route changes via `AskUserQuestion` before any of it touched the live table or live resolution path (this repo's carve-out for hard-to-reverse/shared-state changes, distinct from routine standing-permission actions).
           - **DB**: `active_setups` gained 2 nullable columns, `touch_quality VARCHAR(20)` and `touch_quality_vol_z NUMERIC` (via `server/db.js`, not raw psql — schema.sql regenerated per the documented `pg_dump` command).
           - **Shared logic**: `server/services/touchQuality.js` (`getVolumeBaseline()`, `classifyTouch()`) — used identically by `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` (historical calibration → `performance_audit` `TOUCH_QUALITY` rows, one per N≥50 setup_type: `window_bars`, `high_vol_z_cutoff`, per-bucket N/WR/EV, `thin` flag if bucket N<20) and `acd.js`'s live `resolveSetupsByPrice()` (classifies each open setup once, the moment its calibrated reaction window has elapsed — bars/volume already fetched for the existing resolution walk, no extra query cost — wrapped in try/catch so a classification error can never block real trade resolution). Same margin-based (`>=$3` EV gap, N≥15 non-thin) pattern-tag rule used during the 2026-07-15 audit to catch Gemini's 2 mislabeled types is now encoded in `classifyPattern()` in the calibration script, not just a one-off manual check.
           - **API**: `/api/antigravity/edges-context` (`antigravityEdges.js`) now selects `touch_quality`/`touch_quality_vol_z` on the existing `active_setups` query (additive columns, no `Promise.all` reindexing) and does a **separate** small query for the `TOUCH_QUALITY` calibration rows — deliberately kept outside the documented load-bearing-ordering `Promise.all` array rather than added as a 21st positional entry, since the risk of a silent reindex mistake there outweighs one cheap extra round-trip. Each returned setup gets `touchQualityStats` (bucket + historical WR/EV/N) only when the calibration exists AND that specific bucket isn't `thin` (N≥20) — thin buckets return `null`, so nothing gets shown before there's enough data to trust it.
           - **UI**: `ACDView.jsx`'s live "Today's Actionable Setups" card shows a small badge (⚠/✓/•, green/red by historical EV sign) once `touchQualityStats` is present — purely informational, doesn't affect the existing confidence/recommendation logic.
           - **Cron**: `scripts/calibrate_touch_quality.mjs` added to `run_weekly_backtests.sh`.
           - **Verified**: server syntax-checked clean, frontend lint/build both pass, server restarted (nodemon hadn't picked up the route/service changes — had to `systemctl --user restart trading-journal-server.service` manually, worth remembering next time a route file changes mid-session), `/api/antigravity/edges-context` returns 200 with the new fields present and correctly `null` pre-calibration, no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` after restart.
           - **Calibration confirmed complete same session**: the initial run was slow under concurrent DB load (live server + dashboard traffic — took ~50 min total vs. a couple minutes when run idle earlier), but finished cleanly — `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM performance_audit WHERE signal_type='TOUCH_QUALITY'` = **47/47**. No further action needed here.
           - **Still not done**: no live setup has actually been observed going through the badge end-to-end yet (needs live market hours + an open setup type that has both a calibration row and a non-thin bucket) — the wiring is verified structurally (endpoint shape, no errors) but not yet observed firing for a real trade. Worth a spot-check next trading session.
           - **Full 8-angle code review run 2026-07-15 immediately after shipping this, on own initiative — found and fixed 10 real issues, several genuine correctness bugs.** Not a routine step; done specifically because this touched live trade-resolution code under real time pressure (a varchar-length bug and a double-backgrounding mistake had already slipped through earlier the same session). All 10 independently re-verified against live code/DB before fixing, not just trusted from the finder pass:
             1. **Bar-boundary mismatch (fixed)** — calibration included the touch bar in its window (`ts >= fired_at`), live excluded it (`ts > fired_at`) — every calibrated cutoff was fit on a systematically different window than it was applied to. Verified empirically (8/8 DB spot-check: `fired_at` always exactly matches a real bar timestamp). Fixed by aligning calibration to live's `>` semantics (not the reverse, to avoid touching the resolution-critical shared bars query).
             2. **Fast-resolving trades never classified (fixed)** — live gate required the *full* calibrated window even after a trade had already resolved with fewer bars; ~25% of trades per setup_type (by construction) got `touch_quality` stuck permanently NULL once they flipped to `RESOLVED` and dropped out of the `active` query. Fixed to classify on whichever bars are available once resolved, matching the calibration script's own windowing exactly (`Math.min(windowBars, availableBars)`).
             3. **`TOUCH_QUALITY` invisible in the Unified Signal Table (fixed, deliberately)** — the 4 new recommendation tags matched none of the table's recognized values, so rows silently vanished (the exact bug class CLAUDE.md's setup-type checklist item 8 exists to prevent). Fixed with an explicit, commented exclusion (not a forced ACTIVE/CONTEXT/REMOVED mapping) — touch-quality is a per-setup_type dimension, not a tradeability verdict; the setup_type's real status already comes from its SETUP_STATUS row.
             4. **RTH-truncated resolution overwrite in calibration (fixed)** — the script's own same-day bar replay was overwriting the real DB `resolution` column, while EV still used the real `actual_pnl`, risking an internally inconsistent WR%/EV$ pair for late-session trades. Fixed to keep the DB's real `resolution` as the win/loss source of truth; the replay now only supplies `barsToResolution`/`maeTrace`.
             5. **Hot-path latency risk (documented, root cause addressed indirectly)** — the touch-quality block runs inside `resolveSetupsByPrice()`, which is polled every **15s with a 14s abort timeout** (worse than this file previously documented as 60s) with no lock against overlapping invocations. Not given a dedicated fix (would need a bigger redesign — e.g. moving classification off the hot path entirely), but the per-day caching consolidation (#9 below) removes the worst-case "lazy 90-day scan mid-loop" cost from every day's first poll to effectively once, which was the dominant risk factor.
             6. **`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` regression from this same session's earlier cleanup (fixed)** — the stale-list fix a few hours earlier had also silently dropped 2 real suppression-mechanism descriptions (TREND counter-direction fade filter, S2 double-counter) that aren't reconstructible from the new live list. Restored both, described qualitatively (no hand-typed WR/N/$, consistent with why they were being cleaned up in the first place) and confirmed both still correspond to real, live code (`isTrendCounterFade`/`isS2DoubleCounter` in `acd.js`). A third dropped entry (`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG`'s specific day-type claim) was **not** restored — couldn't independently verify it's still accurate, and reintroducing an unverified claim would repeat the exact mistake being fixed.
             7. **Calibration script's redundant per-setup_type baseline queries (fixed)** — the 90-day volume baseline and per-date bars only depend on `date`, not `setup_type`, but were being recomputed once per (setup_type, date) pair across the 47 types instead of once per date — the confirmed direct cause of the ~50-minute slow run earlier this session. Fixed with a simple date-keyed cache; re-run after all fixes took **2 minutes**.
             8. **Missing standing rigor diagnostic (fixed)** — the new calibration script never imported the shared `computeRigor()` day-clustering/stability check (`server/services/rigorDiagnostics.js`), violating the explicit "standing, not one-off" rule written after the CAM_R4/CAM_S3 incident. Added per-bucket, informational only (doesn't feed `classifyPattern`), and immediately caught something real on the very first re-run: `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`'s `HIGH_VOL_ABSORBED` bucket is chronologically unstable (EV thirds -21 → +58 → +31), while its `HIGH_VOL_OVERRUN` bucket is clean/stable. Surfaced in the live badge as a ⚠︎ hover-caution, not a suppression, per the shared module's own convention.
             9. **Duplicate caching mechanism (fixed)** — `getTouchQualityCalib()`/`getTouchQualityBaseline()` had reinvented per-day caching with 4 new module-level variables next to the file's existing generic `getCached`/`setCached` helper. Fixed by giving `getCached` an optional `ttl` parameter and reusing it (`DAY_CACHE_TTL`), removing the duplicate mechanism entirely.
             10. **Baseline cached by wall-clock "today" instead of each setup's own `trade_date` (fixed)** — narrow edge case (a SHADOW/overnight setup classified after midnight ET would fold its own trade date into its baseline instead of excluding it), but real. Fixed by threading `row.trade_date` through to `getTouchQualityBaseline()`, same convention the calibration script already used.
             - **Re-verified end-to-end after all fixes**: lint/build clean, calibration re-run 47/47 in 2 minutes (down from ~50), server restarted, all 3 touched endpoints (`/api/antigravity/edges-context`, `/api/acd/setup-status`, `/api/performance-audit/unified`) return 200, `TOUCH_QUALITY` confirmed absent from the unified table (deliberately), no new entries in `scratch/server_errors.jsonl`.
             - **Historical note**: the `performance_audit` `TOUCH_QUALITY` rows written before this fix pass (i.e. anything queried between initial ship and this re-run) reflected the pre-fix bar-boundary/RTH-truncation bugs — superseded by the re-run, no stale data left in the table (same-day idempotent upsert).
       - **Time-to-resolution — partially answered already, easy to extend.** `backfill_mae_mfe.mjs`'s replay already tracks `bars_to_resolution` per trade. Quick pull 2026-07-14: median time from touch to `TARGET_HIT` across the level-fade family is **7 minutes** (p25=2min, p75=19min, N=3,916). Varies by setup_type: OR/IB fades resolve in 5-11min median, `CAM_R3_FADE_SHORT` runs longer (~15min). Worth extending to a full per-setup_type table, plus the same breakdown for `STOP_HIT` trades (how long does a *losing* trade typically take to reveal itself — symmetric question, not yet pulled).
