## 🔶 2026-07-28: VWAP built out to full parity with every other level — 8 new setup_types, historically backfilled

User pushed back hard and repeatedly ("they are a level like every other level that gets interacted with every day... same for all setups... those are trades too") after Setup Reference's UI fixes surfaced that VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT had only ever accumulated forward touches (N=17/3), never been backfilled, and that a 24hr/Globex VWAP had no real tracking at all (only a passive morningBrief.js text alert). Built out fully, not just backfilled:

- **Two distinct trigger mechanisms, each with an RTH and a Globex sibling (8 setup_types total)**:
  - `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT` (RTH, pre-existing) / `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT` (new) — far-away sigma-distance trigger (≥1.5σ from the running VWAP), mean-reverts back toward it.
  - `RTH_VWAP_FADE_LONG/SHORT` / `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG/SHORT` (both new) — ordinary close-range (15pt) touch, the SAME proximity mechanism every other level in `keepLevelsAll`/`detectGlobexSetup()` uses. Built after the user explicitly flagged that the magnet-only trigger missed the far more common ordinary-touch case ("what about fades off the vwap? those are trades too").
  - RTH_VWAP_FADE wired directly into `keepLevelsAll` (inherits the full sizeMultiplier stack, confluence, S2/trend-counter suppression, bar-6 checkpoint, delta-confirmation — the exact same machinery every other RTH level gets, for free). GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE wired into `detectGlobexSetup()`'s candidates array (minimal pair-bonus-only sizing, matching the other Globex-fired levels), direction dynamic from current price side (matching `PD_POC`'s `pocDir` convention for a symmetric central-tendency level).
- **Shared math extracted, not reimplemented a 3rd/4th time**: `computeRunningVwapSeries` (`developingValueService.js`), `getTrailing24hrVwapStd`/`getGlobex24hrBars` (`queries.js`, itself a relocation of morningBrief.js's own already-validated 24hr-VWAP logic) — both live code and all 4 new backtest scripts (`scripts/backtest_vwap_magnet.mjs`, `backtest_globex_vwap_magnet.mjs`, `backtest_rth_vwap_fade.mjs`, `backtest_globex_vwap_fade.mjs`) call the same functions.
- **All 8 historically backfilled and calibrated** through the standard pipeline (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`/`update_optimal_stops.mjs`, no new cron entry needed — both auto-discover new setup_types): N=193 (RTH_VWAP_FADE_SHORT) to N=2942 (GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG). All 8 correctly gate `THIN_N`/`SUPPRESS` — real (non-backfill) forward N is 0 or near-0 for every one, so none fire as live alerts yet; they accumulate real data going forward like every other setup_type.
- **Real bug found and fixed along the way**: `getDeltaConfirmationCategory()` (`deltaConfirmation.js`) would have silently misclassified `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG/SHORT` against the RTH-calibrated delta threshold (p25=143) instead of Globex's genuinely different scale (p25=31), purely because its name shape (`..._FADE_LONG`) matched the RTH regex — caught before it shipped, now explicitly excluded.
- **Real finding, not a bug, flagged separately**: `OPEN_DECISION` `vwap_magnet_repeated_whipsaw_on_trend_days` — on an extreme trend day (verified against the real 2025-11-20 session, a genuine 1200+pt RTH crash), the magnet setups can re-fire many times in a row as each tight stop gets blown through by the continuing move — one single day produced 107 of `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG`'s 1158 backfilled fires (9.2%). Still open — needs a product decision (rate limit vs. rely on the standard rigor pipeline's day-clustering check) before fully trusting the magnet family's aggregate EV.
- **Caution on the `update_optimal_stops.mjs` "corrected-resim" target figures for the magnet family**: the resweep found dramatically wider targets than the 20pt fixed target the backfill scripts used (e.g. `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` t1=333.3pt, EV=$370 vs. raw avg pnl=-$2) — this jump is large enough, and close enough to the whipsaw-day concern above, that it should be treated as unverified rather than a real edge until re-examined specifically for contamination from the same extreme-day cluster. The RAW avg-pnl figures (shown alongside every corrected-resim row in the tool's own output) are the more trustworthy number for now.
- `OPEN_DECISION` `globex_24hr_vwap_never_tracked_as_real_setup` resolved (superseded — this whole entry is the resolution).

## ~~🔴 HIGH PRIORITY — does acting on a bad delta-confirmation signal actually improve EV?~~ Resolved 2026-07-28 — NO

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Setup Reference UI fixes + the 24hr VWAP fade is a real dead end (not built)

Four direct user requests off the Setup Reference/Setup Log pages, all fixed: (1) a real bug in the column-order persistence — `colOrder` was saved to `localStorage` but only ever *filtered* against the current column set, never *merged*, so any column added after a user's last drag (the new `misc` column, in this case) silently vanished forever for anyone with a saved order; fixed in both `SetupReferenceView.jsx` and `SetupHistoryView.jsx` to reconcile missing keys back in at load time. (2) The per-row date links in Setup Reference's touch-detail panel navigated away to the Chart Review tab instead of opening a true modal like Key Levels does; factored `KeyLevelBT`'s inline chart-modal JSX into a shared `ChartModalOverlay` component (`BacktestView.jsx`) and reused it for Setup Reference's own `srChartModal` state. (3) Setup Log defaulted to Live-only (`shadow: 'hide'`) on open — changed default to `'both'` to match the user's expectation of seeing everything first.

(4) **"Why don't we have a fade off RTH VWAP?"** — we do: `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT` is exactly that (σ-based distance-from-VWAP mean-reversion, `server/routes/acd.js` ~line 5310), it was just one of the 30 undocumented setup_types (no criteria text, so it read as an unrecognizable mystery row) — added real criteria to `setupDefinitions.js`, verified against the live detection code directly.

**"Should we have a 24hr/Globex VWAP too?" — it already exists, and it's a real, live instance of the no-dead-ends rule.** `server/routes/morningBrief.js` computes a genuine 24hr VWAP (`vwap24`, spanning the prior Globex open) and fires a text alert ("Fade LONG/SHORT toward vwap24hr") at ≥1.5σ — but it never inserts into `active_setups`, never gets a stop/target simulated, never gets WR/EV calibrated, and therefore can never appear in Setup Reference. `OPEN_DECISION` `globex_24hr_vwap_never_tracked_as_real_setup` (MEDIUM) — the natural fix mirrors `VWAP_MAGNET`'s own build (SHADOW-insert with a real stop/target, let the standard calibration pipeline pick it up over time), but this sits in tension with the same-day Opus Audit #5 recommendation to freeze new informational-signal building given the system is data-starved (only 1 of 182 setup_types has ever cleared real N≥20) — user needs to decide whether to build it now or park it.

## ~~🔴 HIGH PRIORITY — does acting on a bad delta-confirmation signal actually improve EV?~~ Resolved 2026-07-28 — NO

`delta_confirmation_state` (wired live 2026-07-28, see the full arc below) is validated as a DESCRIPTIVE split — `CONFIRMATION` trades really did average far better than `PRICE_ONLY_CONTROL` trades, historically, across all three validated categories (real fades, Globex fades, `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE` breakouts). But that's a fact about what already happened, not yet an action. Three actions were tested this session, all on the same signal, all failed: an entry filter (no edge), widening the target on CONFIRMATION (destroys EV), and — the last untested one, dispatched to Gemini and audited same-session — **exiting early (mark-to-market at bar 10) when a trade shows `PRICE_ONLY_CONTROL`/`NO_EFFORT`.**

**Result: negative.** 3-arm test (Arm A baseline hold / Arm B blind unconditional bar-10 exit control / Arm C conditional exit-on-bad-signal-only), restricted to trades that survived to bar 10, across `FADE`/`FADE_GLOBEX`/`BREAKOUT`. Audited directly: population totals cross-check exactly against the earlier validated `pilot_delta_fade_confirmation.mjs` FADE count (5819 survivors + 4464 excluded = 10283, an exact match), and the bar10-exit-price/classification-window boundary was verified to align with no off-by-one leak. Pooled N=18261: Arm A EV=-$7.92 (43.3% WR), Arm B EV=-$9.70 (43.9% WR), Arm C EV=-$9.66 (36.6% WR) — Arm C does not beat Arm A in any category (FADE -$25.24 vs -$23.08, FADE_GLOBEX -$5.02 vs -$1.52, BREAKOUT $0.02 vs -$0.21), and doesn't clearly beat Arm B either. The bad-signal sub-population really is a loser (EV -$21.37 pooled, confirmed), but exiting it early at bar 10 makes things flat-to-slightly-worse (-$23.95), not better — by bar 10 the adverse move has typically already happened, so a mark-to-market exit tends to lock in roughly the same or a worse loss than the original stop would have. One caveat noted, not chased further: Gemini's BREAKOUT arm used an ad hoc invented stop/target (the real `calibrateBreakout()` only returns cumDelta, not stop/target) — BREAKOUT's absolute EVs are less trustworthy than FADE/FADE_GLOBEX's, though its Arm A/B/C *internal* comparison (same stop/target across all three arms) still directionally agrees with the other two categories.

`RESEARCH_CLAIM` `delta_confirmation_early_exit_fails_to_beat_baseline` (`CONFIRMED`). `OPEN_DECISION` `test_early_exit_on_price_only_control` resolved. **Net for this whole arc: 3 of 3 tested actions on `delta_confirmation_state` have failed — the signal stays informational-only (the live badge), with no currently-known way to convert it into a live EV improvement.** Full script/results: `scratch/pilot_delta_early_exit_control.mjs` / `scratch/gemini_delta_early_exit_control_results.md`.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Cumulative delta adds real information beyond price action for breakout-continuation trades — not yet wired

User's own order-flow framework, adapted from a DOM-absorption description (no live DOM data in this system, but `price_bars_primary`'s `ask_volume`/`bid_volume` already supports the trade-based version): does cumulative delta (a running sum since entry, not the single-bar snapshot ratio `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE` already tests) predict whether a breakout-continuation trade actually continues, distinct from the already-tested pre-entry gate (which failed `computeReplication()` earlier the same session).

Dispatched to Gemini with a 3-bucket design (CONFIRMATION/DIVERGENCE/NO_EFFORT); audited before trusting and found two real problems: (1) the `NO_EFFORT` filter had a tautological clause (`!x || x`, always true) that silently no-opped its own price-action exclusion; (2) no control bucket existed to isolate whether the reported CONFIRMATION-vs-NO_EFFORT gap was a genuine delta effect or just "price already moved in your favor" (a very low, near-tautological bar — `newExtreme` = any tick beyond entry within the window). Fixed directly (not re-dispatched — a diagnosed bug + one added control bucket, not new mining) into a clean, exhaustive 2×2 split on `(cumDelta >= p25) × (newExtreme)`.

**Result, unusually for this session, the control CONFIRMED rather than debunked the hypothesis**: holding price action equal (both buckets have `newExtreme=true`), whether delta also built alongside it splits `CONFIRMATION` (N=3768, WR=61.0%, EV=+$40.24) from `PRICE_ONLY_CONTROL` (N=6214, WR=30.4%, EV=-$24.50) — consistent across K=5 and K=10, `computeRigor` clean/stable/not-clustered on both. The rare pure-exhaustion signature (`DIVERGENCE`: delta builds, price never progresses) stayed too thin to use standalone (N=8-11), matching the first pass. Recorded: `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `cumulative_delta_confirms_breakout_beyond_price_alone` (`PROVISIONAL`).

**Not yet wired anywhere** — `OPEN_DECISION` `wire_cumulative_delta_confirmation_signal` (MEDIUM) is open on scope: a post-entry confirm/exit read (`bar6_checkpoint`-style, doesn't gate entry) vs. an entry-quality filter (which the user has separately pushed back on while the system is real-N-starved) vs. something else. Resolves an earlier low-priority backlog item, `test_cumulative_delta_divergence_breakout_exhaustion` (now `RESOLVED` with this outcome).

**Same-day follow-up: user asked for both an entry-side version AND a target-extension use of this finding — both tested negative.** Dispatched together with explicit "don't be myopic" instructions (proper controls built in from the start this time). Audited the response and found Gemini's own summary was directionally wrong on one point — corrected before recording:
- **Entry filter** (`pre_entry_cumulative_delta_no_entry_edge`, `PROVISIONAL`): pre-entry cumulative delta (same backward-scan window used to confirm the level break, ending at entry) checked against a pre-entry price-momentum control, LONG/SHORT/pooled. No signal on LONG (all 4 buckets negative-or-near-zero EV, zero rigor passes). Thin, marginal edge on SHORT only (N=2642, WR=53.7%, EV=$1.90, the one rigor-clean row) that barely beats momentum alone (EV=$0.80) — not a usable filter. Confirms the entry-side question is genuinely different from (and doesn't inherit) the validated post-entry effect.
- **Target extension** (`target_extension_on_confirmation_not_actionable`, `PROVISIONAL`): does CONFIRMATION justify widening T1 past the calibrated target, vs. baseline and a blind-widen-everyone-who-survived control (the control that actually matters — isolates whether CONFIRMATION adds anything over "any trade that's lasted this long has more room"). **Corrected Gemini's own claim of a blanket "widening destroys EV" result — it only holds for LONG.** LONG: baseline beats both widen variants (K=10 baseline EV=$4.76 vs confirm-widen $2.80 vs blind-widen $1.52) — don't widen. SHORT: reversed — baseline is the *worst* of the three (K=10: -$6.95 vs confirm-widen -$5.95 vs blind-widen -$2.76) — but nothing here is attractive, since the whole SHORT population is net-negative at every target tested (likely a separate, uninvestigated SHORT-target calibration gap in this ungated population, not a delta effect — not chased further, out of scope for this thread). Also found the reported "~75% give-back rate" is largely a **mechanical artifact**, not a discovery: the widened target was set at the 75th percentile of winners' own MFE, so ~75% of any population sitting at/below its own 75th percentile is close to guaranteed by construction, independent of cumulative delta.

**Net: the only thing actually validated after all three tests is the original post-entry confirm/exit read.** Neither the entry-filter angle nor the target-extension angle produced anything to build. `wire_cumulative_delta_confirmation_signal` updated with this outcome, still open on scope.

**Extended same day to fades (the dominant setup family) and Globex, per direct user follow-up ("can this be used for all setups?").** Two more dispatches:
- **Real level fades** (`cumulative_delta_confirms_fades_stronger_than_breakout`, `PROVISIONAL`): same corrected 2×2, reusing the real `detectLevelFades`/`resolve` (`backtest_unified.js`) rather than reimplementing. K=10 pooled: `CONFIRMATION` N=3591 WR=83.2% EV=+$42.67 vs `PRICE_ONLY_CONTROL` N=6383 WR=53.5% EV=-$24.22 — an even larger gap than breakouts, consistent across LONG/SHORT and every level family checked. Audited for the same bug classes already found twice — none present. The result's near-total uniformity across every cut was itself treated as a "look closer" signal per standing practice; no confound found, but noted explicitly rather than silently accepted.
- **Globex/overnight fades** (`cumulative_delta_confirms_globex_fades_too`, `PROVISIONAL`): extended with `detectGlobexLevelFades` (an audited, faithful adaptation of `detectLevelFades` with the RTH-only gate removed — Globex has no RTH window to gate against). N=1568/2809, rigor-clean, consistent LONG/SHORT, but a genuinely different calibration floor (p25=31 vs RTH's p25=143 at K=10) — confirmed, not assumed, that Globex needs its own calibration category.
- **The "OTHER" session-structure family tested and found NOT validated** (`cumulative_delta_other_family_too_thin_not_validated`, `PROVISIONAL`): `IB_BULLISH/BEARISH`, `TRT`, `C_STANDALONE`, `OPEN_TEST_DRIVE`, `VALUE_AREA_RESPONSIVE`, `BRACKET_BREAKOUT`, `VWAP_MAGNET`, `STOP_SWEEP` — real detection functions (`detectIB`/`detectCStandalone`/etc.) newly exported from `backtest_unified.js` and reused, no reimplementation bug. The dispatched summary claimed the effect "generalizes universally" — that did not survive audit: most of these setup_types have far too little real history to trust (`C_STANDALONE` total N=7 reported a $294-522 EV — noise, not signal), and the one family with a real sample size, `BRACKET_BREAKOUT` (N=61), showed no effect at all, directly contradicting "universal."

**Wired live the same day** — `server/services/deltaConfirmation.js` (shared `classifyDeltaConfirmation()`/`getDeltaConfirmationCategory()`, reused by both `resolveSetupsByPrice()` and `scripts/calibrate_delta_confirmation.mjs`), new `active_setups.delta_confirmation_state` column, computed at bar 10 since entry, same "compute once, never overwrite" convention as `bar6_checkpoint`. Scoped to exactly what validated (`FADE`/`FADE_GLOBEX`/`BREAKOUT` categories) — `getDeltaConfirmationCategory()` returns `null` for the OTHER family, a silent no-op rather than a guess. Threshold is a real rolling 25th-percentile floor per category, recalibrated weekly over a trailing 200 days (never a static number). Verified live end-to-end: real fade setups show the correct classification on both the live setup card and the HA feed. `wire_cumulative_delta_confirmation_signal` resolved. New `OPEN_DECISION` `delta_confirmation_session_timeline_gap` (LOW) — deliberately not added to `App.jsx`'s Session Timeline, whose badge-rendering path (including `bar6_checkpoint` itself) is scoped to a different population (`isCaseEngine` types) than what this signal covers.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Opus Audit #5 — the system is data-starved, not miscalibrated; stop building new signals

Full deliverable: `scratch/opus_audit_5_results.md`. Dispatched per direct user request after the day's three research threads (DOW gate, re-fire gate, rolling-window health gate) left them feeling scattered — see `docs/OPUS_AUDIT_PROMPT_5.md`.

**Headline**: only **1 of 182** tracked setup_types has EVER cleared N≥20 on real (`ACTIVE`/`SHADOW`-origin) trades (`IB_BEARISH`, real_n=44) — the whole `active_setups` table holds **302 real resolved trades** total (83% of all rows are synthetic `BACKFILL`). The user's "repetitive, same few" observation is literally accurate: after removing 2 orphaned calibration rows that never fire (`MOMENTUM_60m_60m_*`) and a 1-fire variant (`WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP`), the genuinely-firing-with-real-support live roster is **essentially `IB_BEARISH` alone**. This is a data-supply problem, not a threshold-tuning problem — loosening the N≥20 bar would just re-admit the backfill contamination this codebase already spent weeks purging.

**Confirmed, still-live bug (answers the pending `IB_BULLISH` question directly, more precisely than my own earlier finding)**: the live day-type firing gate was rewired 2026-07-24 to read `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA` dynamically (not the `SETUP_STATUS` day-type breakdown I was checking) — `server/routes/acd.js:4663-4673` applies N≥20/EV<-$5 to the **blended** `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA` cell, which has no `origin_status` filter (`scripts/backtest_day_type_alpha.js`, same class of gap `PROMOTE_MIN_REAL_N` fixed elsewhere on 2026-07-20, never applied here). Confirmed both directions: `IB_BULLISH` fires live on `TREND` where real support is ~0 (all `UNKNOWN`-origin, 3 weeks stale, blended EV +$16.3 is illusory); `IB_BEARISH` fires on `TURBULENT` where real EV is **-$10.33** (blended +$72.1 is backfill-driven) but correctly fires on `TREND` where real EV is genuinely positive (+$9.84, N=31). **Fix** (scoped, mirrors the existing `PROMOTE_MIN_REAL_N` pattern): add real_n/real_ev per `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA` cell, gate firing on real support not blended. Predicted effect: `IB_BULLISH` goes fully dark (removes a -$25.50-blended live exposure); `IB_BEARISH` narrows to `TREND`-only (its one genuinely profitable day-type). Not yet implemented — next action.

**Informational-signal family verdict: clutter, not validated.** `exhaustion_signal_at_detection` is populated on **0 of 11,098 rows** — built, documented as "wired live," never once written. The other 5 (`bar6_checkpoint`, `bar6_exit_recommended`, `touch_quality`, `BIGMOVE_LIVE_SIGNAL`, `SIGMA_CONTINUATION_LIVE`, `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE`) have zero linkage anywhere to a recorded decision or outcome — the outcome-logging tables that would prove use exist (`trade_feedback` 14 rows, `trade_annotations` 10 rows last touched 7 weeks ago) but are abandoned. Recommendation: retire the dead `exhaustion_signal_at_detection` column, freeze new informational-signal building (park `volume_sigma_leading_indicator_worth_researching` pending this), and build a one-tap outcome-logging loop on the live setup card before adding a 7th signal — the only lever that both grows real-N (the Q1 bottleneck) and would ever answer whether any badge earns its keep.

**sizeMultiplier**: still effectively binary (0.5/1.0) post the 2026-07-27 origin-scoping fix — the ~20-factor stack doesn't produce a spectrum because individual factors rarely deviate from 1.0. Recommendation: instrument per-factor before building anything further; low-priority cleanup, not a build.

**Today's 3 research threads, in Opus's own words**: "reactive scatter that each rediscovered an already-handled effect" — confirms the rolling-window health gate's own regime-confound finding independently. `OPEN_DECISION`/`RESEARCH_CLAIM` backlog itself is healthy (25 pending/94 resolved, oldest 7 days, 0 overdue claims) — not the problem.

**Ranked next actions (Opus's own prioritization, by "makes the live decision more trustworthy," not research interest)**: (1) fix the day-type real-N gap above [HIGH]; (2) build the outcome-logging loop before any new signal [HIGH]; (3) retire `exhaustion_signal_at_detection`, instrument-or-simplify sizeMultiplier, wire the already-flagged `wire_ib_bearish_refire_cooldown` [MEDIUM]. Explicitly NOT recommended: the full sizeMultiplier joint model, a blanket re-fire-gate rollout, any new order-flow/candle/volume signal, or further rolling-window-health-gate work until tested within a single day_type.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Rolling-trade-count dynamic health gate tested — promising design, but likely rediscovering a known regime effect, not setup-specific fatigue

User proposed a new mechanism, distinct from the day's other two tests: instead of `SETUP_STATUS`'s existing 90-*calendar*-day batch-recomputed-daily SUPPRESS/PROMOTE, track a rolling *trade-count* window (last N fires) per setup_type and dynamically move it between `ACTIVE`/`SHADOW` as a rolling win-rate/EV metric "cools off" or "warms back up" — checked continuously, not once/day.

Found a real data constraint before designing the test: `active_setups` has only 302 total real (`ACTIVE`/`SHADOW`-origin) resolved trades across the ENTIRE table — nowhere near enough per setup_type to test a rolling-20-trade window on live history. Used the same validated bar-walk resimulation as the day's other two tests instead (real calibrated stop/target, same 21 setup_types, EXPIRED handling correctly matched the now-established template — no repeat of either earlier bug). Design: thresholds are data-derived (25th-percentile "cool off," 50th-percentile "warm back up" — real hysteresis, not a flat picked percentage), calibrated on the first 65% of each setup's chronological history and tested only on the remaining 35%.

**Result: 5 of 21 setup_types (`CAM_S1_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG`, `ONH_FADE_SHORT`, `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_LONG`) show a rigor-clean positive EV improvement from dynamic gating; 16 show none.** But two real caveats surfaced on audit, neither reported by the dispatched script itself:
1. **No replication/held-out check was run on the best-of-8-candidates-per-setup selection** — an oversight in how this test was scoped (not just the response), and exactly the largest-of-K risk the standing confound checklist exists to catch. These 5 survivors are weaker evidence than the equivalent ones in the day's other two tests, which did get a real replication check.
2. **The 5 winning setups' shadow-period date ranges overlap heavily in calendar time** (`ONH_FADE_SHORT`/`OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` both shadow ~2026-02-13 to 02-19; `CAM_S1_FADE_SHORT`/`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG` both shadow late May 2026). Checked directly against `acd_daily_log`: `TURBULENT` days are elevated during these overlap windows (32% vs. 21% full-history baseline — thin, N=25, but directionally real). This is consistent with the mechanism rediscovering an already-known, already-handled market-regime effect (`DAY_TYPE_ALPHA`/`backtest_day_type_alpha.js` already suppresses by day_type) rather than detecting genuine independent per-setup fatigue cycles — a materially different, less novel result than what was being tested for.

Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `rolling_window_health_gate_regime_confound_unvalidated` (`PROVISIONAL`). The hysteresis/percentile-derivation design itself is sound and reusable — not abandoning the idea — but it needs a real replication check plus an explicit day_type-controlled test (does the rolling gate still help *within* a single day_type, or does its whole effect vanish once regime is conditioned out) before it's trustworthy. No `OPEN_DECISION` flagged — not ready to build.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Real volZ bug found in the 18/21-setup re-fire test — reverses the "cooldown wins" conclusion, still no universal winner

Found while extending the test to the final 3 setup_types for full DOW-universe coverage (`IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_LONG`). Every single `volZ` cutoff (all 9, both directions) showed byte-identical N/EV for every one of the 18 already-tested setup_types too — impossible if the gate were actually discriminating by volume. Root cause: `pilot_dow_freed_refire_gate.mjs` looked up its volume baseline via `baselineMap.get(b.tod)`, where `b.tod` (from `loadData()`) comes back as a STRING (`EXTRACT(hour)*60+EXTRACT(minute)::int` only casts the minute term due to `::` operator precedence, leaving the whole expression a `double` that node-postgres returns as a string) against a `Map` keyed by real JS numbers (`getVolumeBaseline`'s `mod` column, a genuine whole-expression cast). `Map.get()` requires an exact type match, so the lookup silently failed for every single bar — `volZ` stayed `null` forever, and every volZ gate collapsed to "reject every re-fire," identical regardless of cutoff. `pilot_ib_refire_volz_gate.mjs` (the original `IB_BEARISH` test) already coerced `Number(b.tod)` correctly and is unaffected — only this extended script was broken, and it was broken for the ENTIRE prior run (the "cooldown wins in 12/18" conclusion reported earlier this session was built entirely on a volZ mechanism that was never actually tested).

Fixed with a `Number(b.tod)` coercion, re-ran all 21 setup_types (18 original + the 3 new ones). **The correction reverses the earlier conclusion, not just adjusts it**: with `volZ` actually computed, its raw improvement is numerically LARGER than the best cooldown's raw improvement in 16 of 21 setup_types — volume information is NOT just a proxy for time-separation, contrary to what the broken run implied. But the real bar (rigor-clean AND replicating) is only cleared by 3 setup_types with one mechanism unambiguously ahead on both magnitude and cleanliness: `WEEKLY_OPEN_FADE_LONG` and `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` favor `volZ`; `FLOOR_PIVOT_FADE_SHORT` favors cooldown (`IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT` also has a clean+replicating cooldown result, though `volZ`'s own unclean number is larger there). 7 of 21 show any rigor-clean improvement at all; 11 of 21 replicate on their own best cutoff. **Still no universal winner** — mechanism preference remains genuinely setup-specific, just not in the direction previously reported. The original `IB_BEARISH` finding (cooldown beats volZ there specifically) stands unchanged, since that script was never affected by this bug.

Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `dow_freed_refire_gate_volz_bug_corrected_still_mixed`, explicitly correcting (not silently replacing) the prior `dow_freed_setups_refire_gate_mixed_setup_specific` claim, which should no longer be trusted for its cooldown-vs-volZ comparison. No blanket `OPEN_DECISION` flagged for either mechanism — same reasoning as before, just with corrected numbers: if anything gets built, it's scoped to the handful of setup_types with a real, validated, setup-specific result, not a codebase-wide default.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: Extended re-fire gate test to 18 level-fade setups — NOT a universal fix, mixed/setup-specific result, and a fabricated summary claim caught

Direct follow-up to the `IB_BEARISH` re-fire test below. User asked to extend the same volZ-vs-cooldown test to the 18 distinct setup_types behind the DOW-audit's 29 freed cells (excluding `IB_BEARISH`, already covered), with an explicit stated goal of eventually applying whatever works to the full DOW-tracked setup universe if it holds up.

**The delivered summary contained a false claim, caught before trusting it.** Both the results file's "Conclusions" section and the `antigravity_response.md` headline asserted "the vast majority of these 18 setups average <0.2 re-fires per triggered day... structurally minimal, gating is largely immaterial except for `IB_BEARISH`." This is flatly false — directly contradicted by the same report's OWN rollup table: average 1.72 re-fires/triggered-day across all 18, **zero** below 0.2, only 3 of 18 below 1.0. Level-fade re-fires are common (several types re-fire MORE per day than `IB_BEARISH` itself), not a rare edge case. The underlying per-type simulation numbers were internally consistent and the two known EXPIRED-handling bugs from the prior test were correctly avoided this time (verified via direct code read) — this was a narrative/summary defect, not a math bug, but exactly the kind of thing that gets caught by re-deriving the headline from the raw table rather than trusting the prose.

**Corrected result: no universal fix, mechanism and effect are setup_type-specific.** Of the 18: only 6 show ANY rigor-clean positive improvement from either mechanism (`FLOOR_PIVOT_FADE_LONG/SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`, `ONH_FADE_SHORT`, `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG`, `WEEKLY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`); of those, only 4 also pass a per-setup_type replication check (`FLOOR_PIVOT_FADE_SHORT`, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_SHORT`, `ONH_FADE_SHORT`, `WEEKLY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`). Even among those 4, the winning mechanism isn't consistent: 3 favor a blind cooldown (matching the `IB_BEARISH` pattern), but `ONH_FADE_SHORT` is a genuine exception where the `volZ` gate clearly beats its own best cooldown — volume information does add real value there specifically. Also found a real, actionable negative: `IB_LOW_FADE_LONG` shows a rigor-clean NEGATIVE effect from a volume gate (requiring elevated volume before re-firing reliably hurts it). **12 of 18 show no validated benefit from either mechanism at all.**

Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `dow_freed_setups_refire_gate_mixed_setup_specific` (`PROVISIONAL`, with the corrected framing, not the false one). No `OPEN_DECISION` flagged for a blanket rollout — the data doesn't support one; if anything gets built, it should be scoped to the 4 setup_types that show a real, replicating effect, each with its own mechanism choice, not a single universal recipe applied to the full DOW-tracked roster as originally hoped. Remaining gap to full DOW-universe coverage (per the user's stated eventual goal): `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT`, `PD_SESSION_MID_FADE_LONG` (the only setup_types in the still-DOW-blocked-10 group not covered by this test or the `IB_BEARISH`/`IB_BULLISH` one) — not yet tested.

## 🔶 2026-07-28: IB_BEARISH has no re-fire limit — volZ vs. blind cooldown tested, cooldown wins, not yet wired

Follow-up to the DOW-gate audit below, which surfaced `IB_BEARISH` firing 16 times in one real day. User asked whether a volume z-score (`volZ`) could slow the re-fire rate and improve EV — explicitly not a blunt hardcoded cutoff, and explicitly NOT the 60-min-lagged `SIGMA_CONTINUATION_LIVE` (same instruction as the earlier counter-trend-suppression thread).

Dispatched to Gemini with a real chronological bar-walk resimulation (reusing `resolve()`/`getVolumeBaseline()`/`loadData()`, real calibrated stop/target — IB_BEARISH 87/50pt, IB_BULLISH 79/50pt — not the code's stale 50/35 fallback), sweeping 9 `volZ` cutoffs (both directions) plus 6 blind time-cooldowns as the control for "is this really about volume, or just about firing less."

**Two real, confirmed bugs caught across the standing 2-strikes budget, both found by direct code read before trusting any number:**
1. First pass: baseline `IB_BEARISH` EV came back **+$3.60** — the opposite sign from its real calibrated -$16.04 EV. Root cause: `resolve()`'s `EXPIRED` case (`pnl: 0`) was used directly with zero mark-to-market handling anywhere in the script, despite this being explicitly required in the task — the exact bug class this session already hit twice (`pilot_at_touch_volume_fade_filter.mjs`, and the historical `breakeven_then_trail_single_position_2026_07_19` flagship flip). Sent back a targeted correction.
2. Second pass "fixed" the EXPIRED handling but introduced a NEW bug in the same edit: it computed `exitBarIdx = Math.min(entryIdx+240, bars.length-1)` **unconditionally for every trade**, not just `EXPIRED` ones — meaning even a 2-bar `STOP_HIT` now jumped the scan pointer ~240 bars ahead (to near end of day) before resuming. This silently turned "immediate re-fire" into "one fire, then a forced multi-hour blackout regardless of outcome" — confirmed by the population collapsing from N=1073 to N=265. Per the standing 2-strikes rule, fixed this directly myself (moved the capped-exit-index computation inside the `EXPIRED`-only branch, used the real `entryIdx + res.barsHeld` for STOP_HIT/TARGET_HIT) and re-ran locally — verified the fix restored N=1073 and flipped the baseline back to a sane **-$0.64 EV**, matching the real calibrated number's sign.

**Result, on the corrected run**: a real, structural finding independent of any gate — EV degrades by within-day fire number (Fire1=$13.88 → Fire2=$4.05 → Fire3=-$9.75 → Fire4+=-$3.43). Both a `volZ<=-0.5` quiet-volume gate (N=566, +$4.43/trade, rigor-clean, consistent across nearby cutoffs — not a single lucky cell) and a blind 30-minute cooldown (N=579, +$4.98/trade, rigor-clean) show comparable real improvements over the unlimited-refire baseline. **The blind cooldown is not worse than the volume gate** — directly answering the user's question: no, this isn't really about volume specifically, a plain cooldown does the same job. `IB_BULLISH` (already globally `SUPPRESS`ed, tested anyway for completeness) showed the opposite volume relationship (quiet-volume gate hurt it) while cooldown helped it even more — reinforcing "frequency, not volume" and showing this isn't a one-size-fits-all number across both setup types. The single largest number in the entire 30-candidate sweep (`IB_BULLISH` cooldown-45, +$6.90) was checked via `computeReplication` and **failed** — held-out pooled delta collapsed to +$0.31 (a ~95% collapse) with a 50/50 favorable split, a clean example of the "biggest of K" overfitting trap this codebase's confound checklist exists to catch. Flagged as such, not reported as the headline.

Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `ib_bearish_refire_cooldown_beats_volz_gate` (`PROVISIONAL` — rigor-clean but not independently replicated, unlike the failed global-best candidate which WAS replication-tested). `OPEN_DECISION` `wire_ib_bearish_refire_cooldown` (MEDIUM) flagged for the actual live-wiring decision — not built yet, since this is a real behavior change to a currently-firing live setup and needs an explicit call on mechanism/value/scope, not a backtest-and-ship.

## ✅ 2026-07-28: SETUP_STATUS_DOW rigor gate added — 29 of 39 day-of-week suppressions were unaudited noise

Triggered by a direct user question after I manually refreshed `WS1_FADE_SHORT_OVERNIGHT`'s stale SETUP_STATUS row: "which setups are day of week," then "why did we gate these by DOW, I'm wondering if we should pause the gating."

Checked the actual mechanism rather than assume it was fine: `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s per-DOW suppression pass (`SETUP_STATUS_DOW`) tests every `(setup_type, weekday)` combination at N≥20/EV<-$5, same floor as the main per-setup_type check — but unlike that main check (and unlike `mine_minutebar_conditions.mjs`/`mineLevelFades()`/`mine_tod_patterns.mjs`), it had **never had `computeRigor` applied to it at all** — confirmed by reading the code (`computeRigor` is imported once, called once, only for the main pass). With ~180 `(setup_type × weekday)` cells tested every run and zero multiple-comparison correction, this is exactly the overfitting exposure this codebase's own standing rules warn about elsewhere.

Audited all 39 then-currently-suppressed DOW cells directly (re-derived, not assumed): only **10 (26%) were chronologically stable and non-clustered**. `IB_BEARISH_DOW_1` was a textbook red flag — 68% of its N sat in just 5 days (a handful of bad Mondays, not a real Monday effect).

Presented the finding and asked the user how to proceed (pause everything / narrow to the 10 clean cells / build the check first / do nothing) — user chose to narrow to the 10 rigor-clean cells now. Built this as a real, standing pipeline fix rather than a one-off manual override (would have just reverted at the next weekly cron otherwise): `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s DOW pass now requires `computeRigor(...).clean === true` in addition to the N/EV threshold to actually write `recommendation='SUPPRESS'`; a candidate that clears N/EV but fails rigor still gets a real, visible row (`recommendation='ACTIVE'`, `notes.failed_rigor=true` + the rigor stats) instead of silently vanishing, logged as `DOW_RIGOR_FAIL` in the run output. Removed a latent duplicate-logic-drift risk found while making this change: the script's own "clear stale suppression" pass at the bottom used to independently recompute its own copy of the N/EV qualification check rather than reusing the main loop's decision — consolidated to one computation.

Re-ran live the same session: 29 of 39 cells flipped `SUPPRESS`→`ACTIVE` immediately (verified directly against `performance_audit`, matches the audit exactly), 10 remain suppressed. `test_invariants.mjs`/lint clean (pre-existing failure/warning confirmed unrelated). Documented as a deliberate, narrow exception to the standing "rigor diagnostics are informational-only, never feed SUPPRESS/ACTIVE logic" rule — see `CLAUDE.md`'s "Rigor diagnostics are standing, not one-off" entry for the full reasoning (the DOW layer directly blocks live entries on a specific weekday, a materially higher cost than the other pipelines' informational stability badge). Self-corrects going forward: the pipeline recomputes fully from scratch every run (daily 20:20 ET weekdays + weekly), so any of the 29 un-suppressed cells will automatically re-suppress the moment its own real trade population passes the rigor bar, and any of the 10 currently-suppressed cells will automatically clear if its population stops being stable.

## ✅ 2026-07-27 (new session): suppress_counter_trend_fades_after_realtime_bigmove_signal — tested with a genuine fast trigger, NOT supported

Picked up right after `promote_stackvol_to_tracked_setup`. User was explicit that the trigger must be genuinely real-time, not `SIGMA_CONTINUATION_LIVE`'s 60-minute-lagged realized-vol lookback ("that was a waste") — so this reused the same fast methodology just validated for `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE`'s `paceZ` factor (`getPaceBaseline()`, a 5-bar net displacement vs a rolling 20-trading-day per-minute-of-day baseline, exported from `acd.js` specifically for this), computed as a standalone trigger independent of any level break.

Dispatched to Gemini with a same-selection-minus-signal control requirement (COUNTER-trend fades after a trigger vs. COUNTER fades with no recent trigger — not just "is post-trigger EV negative," which proves nothing on its own), a 24-cell grid (4 z-cutoffs × 6 candidate suppression windows 30–240min), and an explicit tie-back to the already-resolved `fade_against_trend_blind_exit_does_not_generalize` null result.

**First pass had a real, confirmed bug** (1 of 2 allowed correction attempts): its own results footnote claimed "trigger detection runs on the full 23h session," but `loadData()`'s hardcoded RTH-only bar filter (imported unmodified from `backtest_unified.js`) meant the trigger could mathematically never fire on a Globex-hours bar — confirmed directly by reading the code (its own `isGlobex` field could never evaluate true given the data it was fed), not by distrusting the summary alone. Sent back a targeted correction: pull a separate continuous, gap-guarded 23h bar series for the trigger only, keep `detectLevelFades()` RTH-only as designed, and add a sanity-check count proving Globex-originated triggers actually reach early-RTH classifications.

**Corrected pass verified independently before trusting it**: 41% of SIGNAL classifications (71,079 of 172,849) were driven by a trigger that fired during Globex hours — plausible and non-zero, unlike the mathematically-impossible zero from the first pass. I additionally ran my own consistency check (not reported by Gemini): all 24 cells sum to exactly N=9,486 classified touches regardless of cutoff/window, confirming no touches were silently dropped or duplicated across the grid.

**Result: NOT SUPPORTED.** SIGNAL (post-trigger) COUNTER EV ranged a modest -$0.03 to -$4.57 across all 24 cells — nothing like the dramatic suppression the hypothesis predicted. CONTROL (no-trigger) COUNTER EV was often even MORE negative (e.g. cutoff=2/window=240m: SIGNAL=-$2.15 N=6180 vs CONTROL=-$34.04 N=203) — the opposite direction, most likely because at wide-window/low-cutoff combinations the residual untriggered CONTROL population shrinks to a thin, unusual subset (quiet/rangebound periods), not real evidence that calm markets are more dangerous to fade. **Zero of the 24 SIGNAL-COUNTER cells passed `computeRigor`** (all unstable/messy). `computeReplication` on the single largest-gap cell (cutoff=3.5/window=30m) also failed (`replicates=false`, `favorableFrac=0.50` — a coin flip). A structural stop/target-distance confound was directly ruled out (SIGNAL avg stop=82.11pt/target=37.01pt vs CONTROL 81.55pt/36.84pt — essentially identical). Does not overturn `fade_against_trend_blind_exit_does_not_generalize` — reinforces it, this time with genuine Globex coverage confirmed rather than assumed.

Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM` `fast_sigma_trigger_counter_trend_suppression_no_edge` (status `CONFIRMED` — the negative result itself is what's confirmed). `OPEN_DECISION` `suppress_counter_trend_fades_after_realtime_bigmove_signal` resolved with the full corrected account. No suppression mechanism was built or wired live — the hypothesis simply didn't survive a properly-controlled test. Working script: `scratch/pilot_fast_sigma_counter_trend_suppression.mjs` (scratch, not committed).

## 🔶 2026-07-27: missed-continuation-move research arc — 2 signals wired live, 1 real infra bug fixed, 1 persistence gap found (not fixed), stop-placement test in flight

Triggered by a direct user report: a large 2026-07-27 morning flush (RTH open ~9:20 through ~10:41 ET, ~500+ points) that the live system caught essentially none of, plus a sharp follow-up challenge — "are you just looking for simple touches or actual interactions?" (echoing a phrase from an earlier 2026-07-27 thread, see the level-agnostic-absorption entries above) — that kept each finding honest rather than accepted at face value. This is the full arc; sub-items below have their own `RESEARCH_CLAIM`/`OPEN_DECISION` rows, this entry is the connective narrative CLAUDE.md's docs-maintenance rule asks for.

**1. Diagnosed why nothing caught it.** `SIGMA_CONTINUATION_LIVE` (60-min trailing lookback) fired at 10:30:17 ET — by then the move was ~29 minutes past its structural break and within ~11 minutes of its actual low. `BIGMOVE_LIVE_SIGNAL` needs 250pt already covered. Neither can fire near the START of a move by design. Every level-fade `setup_type` in this codebase is mean-reversion; none bet on continuation. This is why the user's own two annotated shorts (5-min OR-low + G-Line + PW_VAH stack; VWAP + PW_LOW) never fired in any form.

**2. Built and wired `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE`** (stack break + volume/delta confirmation), through two real methodology bugs caught on audit before trusting anything:
   - First pass (`multilevel_stack_break_no_edge_over_control`): a plain >=2-level break, no volume, tested against a single-level control — **no edge either way.**
   - Second pass added a volume/delta sigma-spike gate on the same break bar. Gemini's first script had a real bar-ORDER bug (classified win/loss via `mfe>=target && mae<stop` checked as independent facts over the whole window, not a bar-by-bar walk — could count a trade that hit its stop FIRST as a win if price later wandered back to target). Fixed directly (`resolveOutcome()`, now the shared bar-by-bar resolver every later pass in this arc imports). Corrected result: 3 rigor-clean configs (RTH `volZ>=2,OSR>=0.55` N=552 EV=+$8.86; Globex two configs, N=582/$2.28 and N=426/$4.23). `RESEARCH_CLAIM stack_break_volume_confirmation_promising_not_confirmed`. **Wired live 2026-07-27**, informational-only, same family as `BIGMOVE_LIVE_SIGNAL`/`SIGMA_CONTINUATION_LIVE`.

**3. Root-caused and fixed a real, standing infra gap found while checking today's own level values**: `level_prices` had **zero rows for 2026-07-27's own trade_date** until run manually mid-session. Root cause: `compute_levels.js` (the only writer of this table anywhere in the codebase) had exactly ONE scheduled trigger — Sunday 9:30 PM ET only. Every Mon-Fri weekday had zero automated refresh; historical weekday rows that did exist were leftover manual runs, not standing automation. **Fixed**: added a Mon-Fri 8:00 AM ET cron (`server/index.js`, `COMPUTE_LEVELS_WEEKDAY`). Left one sub-question open rather than guessed at: whether the Sunday cron should key its row to the upcoming Monday's trade_date instead of Sunday's own calendar date (`sunday_compute_levels_cron_date_semantics_unclear`, MEDIUM).

**4. User pushed back sharply on the volume-magnitude framing**: "I thought we understood this reaction or tussles between buyers and sellers... which create volume nodes." Correct catch — this exact idea (order-flow "battle intensity" at a touch) was already tested earlier the same day (`pilot_level_agnostic_touch_battle_quality.mjs`) and found to add NOTHING once you control for whether price actually held ground (`level_agnostic_touch_battle_volume_adds_no_signal`) — a real negative, not an oversight. Corrected the `volume_pulse_reaction_as_fade_entry_gate` decision I'd flagged too naively; narrowed to the one genuinely untested sliver (`wait_for_held_ground_confirmation_before_fade_entry` — waiting for confirmation BEFORE entering, not just observing it after, which has a real cautionary precedent elsewhere in this codebase of failing against a blind-delay control). Not yet built.

**5. User separately described a real-time pace/acceleration pattern** ("rapid bar speed, volume, no countermovement... pace accelerates with price and volume") — checked directly against real 09:42 ET data: 5 of 5 trailing bars closed down, net 5-bar pace 135.8pt (session's largest reading to that point, paceZ=3.63 vs a real 20-day per-minute-of-day baseline). Built `getPaceBaseline()` and tested it combined with a LOOSENED confluence definition (single-level breaks count, not just >=2-level stacks; developing VWAP added as a candidate level — it wasn't in `level_prices` at all before this, confirmed via direct grep that no other live code persists it structurally either, see item 7) and a lower volume bar (0.5, previously shown too noisy alone — 19.1% of all RTH bars, checked directly). Gemini's script omitted the `computeReplication` check I'd explicitly asked for (imported, never called) — added it myself and re-ran. Result: RTH `STACK_BREAK(>=1) + volZ>=0.5 + OSR>=0.55 + pace(>=4/5 same-direction bars, paceZ>=1)` at stop40/target40: EV +$6.70/trade, N=481, WR 45.3%, clean and consistent across all 3 tested paceZ thresholds (a real plateau, not one lucky cell) — but the strict replication check's individual-trade favorable-fraction bar (0.50) wasn't quite cleared (0.51/0.41/0.42 across the 3 tiers), so this is genuinely PROVISIONAL, not CONFIRMED. Globex showed nothing clean in this family. `RESEARCH_CLAIM loose_confluence_pace_rth_promising_not_confirmed`. **Wired live 2026-07-27** for RTH only (Globex kept on its item-2 threshold, unchanged) — `definitionVersion=2` tagged in the persisted notes so future N/EV analysis doesn't silently blend the two different definitions' fires together.

**6. Verified end-to-end against real 2026-07-27 + prior-week data** (not just trusted the backtest number): the new recipe fires at 09:42 ET on 2026-07-27 — 19 minutes before the old (item-2) threshold's first fire at 10:01, and confirmed via direct bar check (volZ=0.63, OSR=0.55, 5/5 down bars, paceZ=3.63) rather than assumed. Full listing, 8 fires today (net +$62, 4W/3L/1 expired at the fixed 40/40 exit) and 4 fires the prior week (all Monday 07-20, Tue-Fri that week were genuinely quiet by this signal's own definition) — see the line-item tables in this session's transcript; not re-pasted here since they're reproducible directly from `/tmp/scan_recipe.mjs`'s logic (not committed — a throwaway verification script, the real logic lives in the live-wired `acd.js` block and in `pilot_loose_confluence_pace.mjs`).

**7. Real persistence gap found, NOT yet fixed**: VWAP is a genuine daily factor (confirmed already live and real in two places — `earlyVwap` in `acd.js` powers `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT`, `vwap24` in `morningBrief.js` powers the "24H VWAP σ" dashboard chip) but unlike every NAMED level (`PD_HIGH`, `PW_VAH`, etc., which get real structured tracking via `active_setups.confluence_score_at_detection`/`confluence_levels_at_detection` on every insert), VWAP's value is never captured in a structured, queryable field anywhere — only ever embedded in `VWAP_MAGNET`'s free-text `description` string (regex-extractable at best) or, for the new `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE` signal specifically, in that signal's own `performance_audit` notes JSON (real, but scoped only to this one signal, not the general `active_setups` confluence-tracking mechanism every OTHER level participates in). **Not fixed this session** — flag as the next concrete step: add VWAP into the same `confluence_levels_at_detection` write path every other level already uses, for every candidate insert, not just this one signal.

**8. User's follow-up, checked directly and confirmed real, then tested properly and wired.** At the fixed 40pt stop, the 09:42 trade itself LOST (-$81) — max adverse excursion was 45.8pt (at 09:52 ET), just past the 40pt stop, before the real 227.5pt favorable move started. The broken cluster's own far edge was 55.3pt away — a stop placed there would have survived. Tested 6 structural-stop variants (`scratch/pilot_structural_stop_placement.mjs`, Gemini-mined) against `FIXED_40` and a `FIXED_AT_MEDIAN` control. **Two real bugs caught on audit before trusting anything**: (1) the script included `level_prices`' `RTH_VWAP` — an end-of-session snapshot, the exact lookahead `backtest_confluence.js` already excludes for this reason — fixed, N shifted 481→491; (2) the script was missing an `import.meta.url` guard (the same class of bug `backtest_confluence.js` hit on 2026-07-22), so a supplementary rigor-check script that imported one of its functions silently re-triggered its *entire* backtest as a side effect and got killed mid-flight by that run's own `process.exit(0)` — cost two extra failed reimplementation attempts (814, then 751 events, neither matching the validated 491) before adding the rigor check directly into the already-correct script instead of reimplementing a fourth time. **Result**: `LEVEL_NEXT` (stop at the next real level beyond the one just broken, not the immediate edge) wins — N=482, EV +$8.26/trade at target=40pt (WR 48.3%), clean on rigor at two target sizes, and beats `FIXED_AT_MEDIAN` ($5.12 — worse than plain fixed), confirming the edge is genuinely about level placement, not just added width. **An initial verbal report to the user overclaimed the cluster-density story ("denser confluence is real and strong") before the rigor check had actually run — corrected in the same turn once the numbers came back**: only cluster sizes 1 and 3 are rigor-clean at target=40; size 2 and size 4+ are not, so no confluence-count-based stop logic was built. `RESEARCH_CLAIM structural_next_level_stop_beats_fixed_and_median_control`. **Wired live 2026-07-27** (RTH only, `definitionVersion=3`, falls back to fixed 40pt when no level exists within 200pt, ~2% of cases). `computeReplication` on the `LEVEL_NEXT` best cell hit a structural mismatch (its held-out population has no valid data by construction, produced a nonsense -$30,321 pooled value) — discarded as inapplicable rather than reported as a negative; the chronological-stability half of `computeRigor` is what's actually being trusted here.

**9. Exit-research arc, prompted by a direct challenge: "40pt when the market falls 500 is criminal."** Extensive follow-on testing, several real self-caught mistakes along the way:
   - **Diligent target calibration, not a raw grid pick**: used `computeCorrectedTarget()` (`targetCalibrationService.js`) — the same thin-tail/OOS-split/plateau/rigor-guardrailed pipeline every other setup_type is calibrated through — rather than eyeballing the best cell from an ad hoc sweep. Result, direction-specific: LONG clears every guardrail at **70pt** (N=215, oosEv=+$1.74, rigor-clean, though the most recent third shows the edge thinning). SHORT **failed** calibration outright (oosEv=-$24.73 despite looking good in-sample) — widening SHORT would have been a real mistake a naive grid pick would not have caught. Wired live, direction-specific, superseding the informal 150/200/250pt numbers floated earlier in the session.
   - **Tested "does it work better with a strong entry volume spike" — hypothesis refuted.** Bucketing by entry-bar volZ strength showed no consistent relationship; if anything the signal's value over blind timing was highest in the WEAKEST volume bucket. Clean negative, closed.
   - **Tested capture RATIO instead of average EV** (the user's real objective is asymmetric payoff, not average EV) — on the top-decile biggest moves specifically, a plain flat 150pt target captured 42.3% of the available move vs 14-26% for every trailing/volume-based design tested. Mechanistically sensible: trailing stops get shaken out by normal chop on the way to a big move; a patient fixed level doesn't react to chop at all. **Real regression caught and fixed mid-test**: a re-dispatch of this exact test silently re-capped the shared `processStackBreak()`'s eligibility window back to 30 bars while "fixing" what looked like a bug, corrupting the population (N=482 instead of the correct 374) — caught by the same re-verification discipline used throughout this arc, reverted, reconfirmed, re-dispatched with an explicit no-touch warning that held on the next 2 runs.
   - **Tested whether the QUALITY of the path to the target predicts worthwhile continuation.** Bars-to-target (not give-back/MAE-fraction, which was noisy and unreliable) is the real signal: fast arrivals (<=9 bars) are climax spikes — extending them loses a median $135/trade; grinding arrivals (10-25 bars) are real trends — extending them gains a rigor-clean +$34-39/trade.
   - **Combined system, tested specifically on the cohort where extending is worthwhile**: adding volume-climax logic on top of the grinding-arrival cohort made things WORSE, not better (median -$4.75 vs the flat target's +$95, and even a blind delay beat the volume-climax design on tail capture). Confirms the capture-ratio finding on a second, independent population cut.
   - **Wired**: the live signal's qualitative guidance now reflects this full synthesis (bank fast arrivals, extend grinding ones toward a wide target, not a trail). ~~**Deliberately NOT built as an enforced, dynamically-updating mechanism** — this signal is still a momentary, fire-once flag, not a tracked position walked bar-by-bar the way real setups are. `OPEN_DECISION` `promote_stackvol_to_tracked_setup` covers doing that properly (real `active_setups` integration, `resolveSetupsByPrice()` wiring, and this codebase's own N>=20-real-trades-before-beyond-SHADOW rule) rather than rushing a half-built version live.~~ **Resolved 2026-07-27 (new session) — see the entry below ("promote_stackvol_to_tracked_setup — built the real active_setups mechanism").**
   - **Also flagged, not chased down**: `gemini_modified_shared_scratch_fn_silent_regression` (LOW) — the general lesson from the regression incident, for future sessions reusing shared scratch/ helpers.

**10. Follow-up session, same day: fixed a real Globex-hours dead-code bug in `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE`.** The signal had `isGlobexNow` branches throughout implying it ran in both session modes, but physically sat AFTER the Globex branch's early-return in `runSetupDetection` — during 6PM-8:30AM ET the route always exited before reaching it, so the field was silently absent from the live JSON response entirely (confirmed live via curl, not just inferred). Extracted into `computeStackVolSignal(todayET)`, called from both branches. Verified the extraction didn't alter any logic via a whitespace-normalized diff against the pre-edit committed version (byte-identical, 178/178 lines) before trusting it, then confirmed live in both modes. Separately widened level coverage from 69 to ~71-72 (added `PD2_VAH`/`PD2_VAL`/`2D_POC`, the 3 meta-levels the main RTH fade engine also tracks but `level_prices` doesn't store directly) and found + fixed a real live data-quality bug: `RTH_VWAP` (one of the original 69) was being scanned despite being a full-session average only valid after RTH close — `backtest_confluence.js` already excludes it for this reason; confirmed via `computed_at` that today's row was a mid-session (14:40 ET) partial sum masquerading as the full VWAP, frozen for the rest of the day by the 12h cache. Both fixes committed.

**11. Tested the user's actual proposed mechanism: a real-time, single-bar, zero-lookahead volume spike AT a fade level's touch bar, stop placed beyond the level — genuinely different from item 4's already-debunked post-touch "battle intensity" measurement (which starts at `entryIdx+1`, never the touch bar itself).** Tested against the FULL unified level-fade population (`detectLevelFades()`, all ~60 level names, SHADOW/THIN_N/SUPPRESS/ACTIVE alike — not restricted to live-firing setup_types, confirmed this addresses the user's "everything on the unified table" framing directly). **One real bug caught on audit and corrected (1 of 2 allowed Gemini correction attempts)**: the first pass silently dropped `EXPIRED` trades (7.2% of the population, 690/9539) instead of counting them — the same bug class that already flipped a flagship finding in this codebase (`breakeven_then_trail_single_position_2026_07_19`). Confirmed it was a real confound, not just theoretical, by checking directly: dropped EXPIRED trades' mean `touchVolZ` (0.295) was far below resolved trades' (0.886), biasing the control group. Sent back for a mark-to-market fix (verified the correction was genuinely implemented, not just claimed — checked the actual diff: `PT`/`COMM` properly exported at their correct MNQ values, not a second hardcoded copy). **Result, N=9863 post-fix: NOT supported.** The only cell that passes `computeRigor`'s chronological stability check (Solo touches, volZ>=80th pctile) shows high touch-bar volume making fade EV WORSE, not better (SIGNAL EV=-$5.98 vs CONTROL EV=-$1.95, N=637). Confluence-zone cells show a bigger apparent gap (SIGNAL EV=+$1.52 vs CONTROL EV=-$5.15 at Z>=1.5, N=1594) but fail the same stability check, and their own held-out replication check only clears a thin +$0.52/trade. Combined with item 4's finding, this closes out BOTH tested formulations of "volume at a fade touch predicts outcome" with negative/unconfirmed results. `RESEARCH_CLAIM at_touch_volume_spike_fade_entry_filter_no_edge`.

**12. Ran a 1-year walk-forward prop simulation of the fully-combined live-wired mechanism (entry + `LEVEL_NEXT` stop + calibrated target) — the first time all three pieces have been measured together, not separately.** Pure backtest (N=346, never fired live), reused only already-validated shared functions. Total simulated P&L $4,473/year (no DLL), 60.4% WR, avg $12.93/trade (notably better than the $6.70/trade the entry alone was validated at with a flat 40/40 grid — because this uses the real calibrated stop/target). Real, useful breakdown found: solo-level breaks (`clusterSize=1`, ~1/3 of all trades) contribute almost nothing ($0.22/trade avg) vs $12-35/trade for confluence breaks of 2-7 levels — a real pattern, not yet independently rigor-checked as its own hypothesis. Per-level-name breakdown mostly too thin to trust (only `RTH_VWAP` N=29 approaches the N≥20 floor) — flagged explicitly rather than let noisy small-N cells (`MR1` -$731 on N=4, etc.) look like findings. **Important asymmetry found**: wins are capped at a fixed dollar amount (target-size), losses vary with the real stop distance and can run larger (up to -$347 seen) — the opposite shape from what this mechanism exists to fix, since the walk-forward only models the fixed-target exit, not the not-yet-built "extend grinding winners toward ~150pt" mechanism.
