## ✅ 2026-08-04: Compression → Tail-MFE test — RESOLVED, all 3 parts negative (a third, differently-framed rejection of the same underlying question)

**Superseded by the entry above for the market-behavior question specifically — the mean-reversion/continuation split below is a real, narrower, correctly-labeled finding about THIS SYSTEM'S fired trades, not deleted, just not the properly-powered answer to "does compression predict market moves."**

Full spec: `docs/COMPRESSION_TAIL_MFE_SPEC.md` (Opus-authored brief, deliberately built to differ from the two prior rejected regime-classification attempts on 3 axes at once: a genuinely new input — compression, not position-in-range; a tail-probability outcome — `P(MFE>=2x setup_type's own median)`, not mean EV; and per-family replication — MEAN_REVERSION and CONTINUATION tested separately, never pooled, so a true bidirectional effect can't cancel into apparent noise the way it did in Attempt 1's $1.24/trade result).

**Part 1 (backfill, built directly, not Gemini)**: `scripts/backfill_compression_metrics.mjs` added `va_width_pctile_60d`/`va_overlap_streak`/`ib_range_pctile_60d` to `active_setups`, backfilled onto all 1060 real RTH resolved trades. All 3 metrics independently re-derived by hand against random samples and matched exactly — the one apparent mismatch during that check turned out to be a bug in the *verification* script itself (Sunday Globex-only dates polluting its day list, misaligning the trailing-60-day window), not the backfill, a useful reminder that "two independent computations disagree" cuts both ways and needs chasing down regardless of which one looks more "official." `inferStrategyFamily()` added to `server/config/setupTypes.js` alongside `inferDirection()`, pre-registering all 178 setup_types (165 MEAN_REVERSION / 13 CONTINUATION, 0 unclassified) before any outcome was looked at.

**Part 4's setup-selection check (built directly) already produced a real finding on its own**: of the 6 setup_types clearing N(TARGET_HIT)>=20 with a live `OPTIMAL_STOP` row, NONE were a VAH-family level-fade type — the external review's "a VAH type is the natural candidate" framing did not survive contact with real data, exactly the kind of assumption the spec explicitly told the builder to check rather than trust. `IB_BEARISH` selected instead (largest real N: 129 total, 80 TARGET_HIT).

**Parts 2/3 and Part 4's actual pilot dispatched to Gemini, both audited line-by-line before trusting anything (not just the prose summary) — 3 real bugs found, 2 fixed before recording, all negative:**
- **Part 2/3** (`scripts/analyze_compression_tail_mfe.mjs`): structurally correct on read — cut points pinned from the pooled-both-families marginal distribution before any outcome check, correct compressed/uncompressed direction per metric, `computeRigor()`/`computeReplication()` imported not reimplemented, `recordClaim()` used not hand-rolled. One dormant, harmless vocabulary bug fixed (`status: 'VERIFIED'` isn't part of this codebase's established CONFIRMED/PROVISIONAL/STALE vocabulary — never actually triggered since nothing passed Bonferroni, fixed to `'CONFIRMED'` for correctness on any future run that does). One cell's exact N/rate independently re-derived from a fresh query outside the script and matched exactly (N=75/5.3% vs N=751/8.0%, va_width P10 cutoff=35). **Result: MEAN_REVERSION is a real, clean, closed negative** — no cell of 9 cleared the Bonferroni-corrected bar (α=0.05/18), and `computeReplication()` on the best cell shows the pooled family effect isn't broadly shared across its setup_types (selected pooled=-0.1 vs held-out pooled=+0.26). **CONTINUATION is underpowered, not a clean negative** — only 186 real trades across 13 thin setup_types; the one directionally-hypothesis-consistent cell (va_overlap_streak, +3.3pp) is nowhere near significant and doesn't replicate either, but there's genuinely too little data yet to call this closed the way MEAN_REVERSION is. `RESEARCH_CLAIM compression_tail_mfe_mean_reversion` / `compression_tail_mfe_continuation`.
- **Part 4 pilot** (`scripts/pilot_ib_bearish_2of3_target_1of3_trail.mjs`, 2/3-bank-at-target + 1/3-breakeven-trail on `IB_BEARISH`): **two real bugs found and fixed, both before trusting the number.** (1) The trades query had no `ORDER BY fired_at` — confirmed directly that Postgres's natural scan order does NOT match chronological order for this query, so the "2/3 in-sample / 1/3 out-of-sample" split was silently running on an arbitrary subset, not a real walk-forward split (the exact "no lookahead in backtests" bug class this file warns about elsewhere). Fixed, and **the corrected chronological re-run still fails the plateau-check guardrail — more decisively** (baseline IS EV moved from a wrongly-ordered $86.26 to the correct $72.26; the failing neighbor's IS EV moved from $80.51 to $50.40), so the negative conclusion is robust to the fix, not an artifact of the bug. (2) The trail-sweep loop's same-bar tie-break branch (target AND stop both reachable within one 1-min bar) left `pnl` unassigned, which JS's `+= null` silently coerces to +0 — understates a real stop-loss. Verified this never actually triggered for `IB_BEARISH`'s real 53pt/50pt distances (0 of 118 trades had a qualifying single-bar range), so it didn't affect this run's numbers, but fixed in the committed script since it would matter on a different parameter set. **Result: FAILED the plateau check** — best in-sample trail=113.3pt, but its immediate neighbor (trail=76pt) scored below the resimulated baseline, an isolated spike rather than a genuine plateau. `RESEARCH_CLAIM ib_bearish_2of3_1of3_runner_pilot`.

**Net read**: this is now the third framing (Mid/Edge position, then the tagging-only value-area layer, now compression→tail-probability) to come back negative on "does some pre-trade market-structure measurement predict something useful about this system's setups" for the MEAN_REVERSION majority of the roster — each framing was a real, reasoned response to a specific documented flaw in the one before it (see the spec doc's own "why this is different" section), not a blind re-run, and each one closed cleanly with real effect sizes and CIs on record rather than fading into unrecorded prose. The CONTINUATION-family question and the "does a wider scale-out ratio or a different pilot setup_type do better" question both remain genuinely open (not rejected, just underpowered/untested) — worth revisiting once real CONTINUATION-family N grows, not worth re-testing blindly today. `OPEN_DECISION compression_tail_mfe_spec_not_started` resolved.

## 🔶 2026-08-05 (corrected three times, same thread): Compression research finds a real, mixed, NOT-yet-actionable signal — read this entry, it supersedes both entries below it; next session should start here

**Session ended deliberately at this point** — the trade-level validation, and the two new leads below, were parked for a fresh session rather than chased on a tired one.

**Supersedes the trade-gated Part 2/3 entry two below AND the first "session range, negative for the hypothesis" write-up of this same test — both kept on record below for the reasoning trail, neither is the final word.**

This thread was corrected twice in one day, both times by catching a framing mistake rather than a code bug — worth reading both corrections since the second one reverses the practical conclusion of the first.

**Correction 1 (routing)**: the original Part 2/3 test (entry two below) tested compression against trade-level MFE, which only exists for a fired trade, forcing the question through `active_setups` (real `origin_status` tracking is only ~3 weeks old, gated by whether one of this system's setups happened to fire) — exactly the routing this file's own standing rule ("A hypothesis about market behavior gets tested against raw bar/price history first...") exists to prevent. Fixed with `scripts/analyze_compression_session_range.mjs`: drops setup_type entirely, tests all 369 real NQ RTH trading days (full multi-year bar history) directly — prior day's own value-area width/IB range (percentile-ranked vs its own trailing 60) and consecutive-overlap streak, predicting whether TODAY's realized range lands in the top quartile of its own trailing 60. Found a real, Bonferroni-significant, rigor-clean effect.

**Correction 2 (interpretation, 2026-08-05)**: that result was then filed as a flat negative — "compression predicts a *quieter* day, the opposite of the squeeze-then-expansion hypothesis, so the whole idea is dead." An external review caught that this framing threw away the positive half of the *same* z-test: an UNCOMPRESSED (already-wide) day predicts a real lift in top-quartile-range odds over the true base rate (verified directly: 32.8%, not an assumed 25%) — va_width best cell: wide=39.6% vs compressed=19.4%; ib_range: wide=39.3% vs compressed=20.8%, this one's effect getting *stronger* out-of-sample. Both metrics describe the same regularity — volatility **clustering** (quiet begets quiet, active begets active) — and this independently corroborates a pre-existing, differently-derived finding in this codebase (`RESEARCH_CLAIM volatility_squeeze_bigmove_inverted`). `RESEARCH_CLAIM compression_session_range_prediction` rewritten to report both directions symmetrically with the corrected base rate.

**The real payoff, though, is a follow-up test this correction motivated: a SAME-DAY (not cross-day) version.** The cross-day result (yesterday's stats → today's total range) is knowable before the open but isn't quite the "hold or take profit" mechanism this spec was built to inform — that needs *today's own* IB range (closed and fully known by 10:30 ET) predicting the *remainder* of *today's* session. Built and independently verified: `scripts/analyze_intraday_ib_range_remainder.mjs`, no-lookahead intraday split (predictor bars etMin<630, outcome bars etMin>=630, verified no bar used in both). **Result: this is the strongest, cleanest finding in the entire thread.** When today's own IB range sits in the top DECILE of its trailing-60-day distribution, the rest of that session (10:30→close) has historically been a top-quartile-range day **73% of the time vs 27% otherwise** (N=37 vs 332, p=9.6e-9, Bonferroni-clean, `rigor.clean=true`, out-of-sample train/test nearly identical at 45.8%→47.1% — essentially zero decay). Independently re-derived by hand (exact N/rate reproduced; the 37 qualifying dates span 14 distinct calendar months over ~2 years, not clustered). **Important nuance, not to be glossed over**: the two looser candidate cutoffs (top third / top quarter of IB range) look even more dramatic in raw aggregate (+31pp, +31pp) but both **fail** `rigor.clean` — their own hit rate degrades from positive to negative across the three chronological thirds of history, exactly the kind of thing an aggregate p-value can't see. Only the top-decile cutoff is genuinely stable; the others should not be trusted despite looking bigger.

**Concrete spot-check against the real incident that started the structural-breakout thread**: the 2026-08-03 CAM_S1/PW_VAH missed-bounce day really was a top-quartile day (85th percentile of its own trailing 60, 650.8pt range). The *cross-day* rule would have flagged it in advance — 2026-07-31 (the prior session) was not compressed on either metric (va_width pctile=76.7, IB range pctile=98.3) — and this matters specifically because the missed bounce happened at 09:30-09:45 ET, *before that day's own IB had even formed*, so the same-day intraday signal (however strong) could not have anticipated this particular move regardless of its own result. The two signals answer different questions with different lead times; neither makes the other redundant.

**Hard caution carried into the `OPEN_DECISION` below, stated explicitly so it can't get lost if this gets picked up later**: this must stay an EXIT-TIMING signal only (hold a runner longer / take profit sooner) — never a position-sizing signal. Wide sessions carry wide adverse excursion too; the same conditions that let a winner run also make a loser deeper, so using this to size *up* would invert the risk logic it's meant to serve.

**Not wired live** — flagged as `OPEN_DECISION wire_intraday_ib_range_exit_signal` (HIGH), since (1) this is exit-timing logic on live trades, which this codebase's own standing rule requires a real design pass for before touching, not a same-session ship, and (2) it's only been tested against a market-wide, setup-agnostic outcome so far — the next step is a trade-level version (does IB-top-decile at the time a specific setup's target is hit predict that trade's own real runner performance), not a market-wide proxy. `RESEARCH_CLAIM intraday_ib_range_predicts_remainder`. Combined with the still-standing IB_BEARISH pilot's plateau-check failure (see below) — this same-day signal, tested with no conditioning at all in that pilot, is exactly the kind of variable that pilot was missing.

**Same-day correction, 2026-08-05 — the day-type conditioning check that changes the verdict.** A second external review flagged something the finding above glossed over: "top-quartile range" conflates two behaviors a runner should be managed oppositely for — a clean directional TREND day (holding pays) and a whipsawing TURBULENT day (holding gives the move back repeatedly). A wide-IB day predicting "big range coming" doesn't say which one is coming. Checked directly (`scripts/analyze_intraday_ib_range_daytype.mjs`, joined against `acd_daily_log.day_type` — the real ground-truth TREND/TURBULENT/BALANCE classifier, `scripts/derive_day_types.js`, imported not reimplemented): **top-decile-IB days are disproportionately TURBULENT (52.8% vs a 17.3% base rate), not TREND (16.7%, actually BELOW the 21.6% base rate)**. Narrowed further to the wide days that were themselves a "hit" (top-quartile remainder — exactly the cases a naive rule would say to hold) — 57.7% TURBULENT, only 15.4% TREND. **This means the naive framing ("wide IB → hold the runner longer") is likely wrong as stated, not just unvalidated** — it would disproportionately hold into the day-type where holding actively hurts. `RESEARCH_CLAIM intraday_ib_range_daytype_conditioning`, `OPEN_DECISION wire_intraday_ib_range_exit_signal` revised accordingly: any real design needs day-type (or a real-time proxy for it, computable by the same point a hold/take-profit decision would fire) as a **required** second condition, not an optional refinement — "wide IB AND developing-TREND-like structure" is a meaningfully different, still-untested claim from "wide IB alone," and the trade-level validation should wait for that redesign rather than test the un-conditioned version first.

**Two smaller corrections made the same pass**: (1) winner's-curse check — re-selecting the P90 cutoff using only the train-chronological-2/3 (never seeing the full sample) still picks the same level, and its true held-out test rate was 75% vs 28% (N=12/111) — didn't decay in this specific slice, but N=12 is too thin to read as confirmation either way; plan around the low 60s for the raw split until real forward data says otherwise. (2) outcome-window independence explicitly confirmed (not just asserted) — the remainder range is a direct max/min over only the post-10:30 bars, zero bar overlap with the IB window, verified via a sample-day bar-count printout — not an arithmetic artifact of subtracting IB from the total.

**Parked deliberately**: the trade-level validation (does IB-top-decile at a specific setup's target-hit time predict that trade's own runner performance) is NOT being started this session — per direct instruction, high-stakes work that eventually touches live behavior should wait for a fresh session rather than continue into a long one.

**Two more leads flagged for that fresh session (recorded on `OPEN_DECISION wire_intraday_ib_range_exit_signal`, not chased tonight)**:
1. **The day-type finding may itself be sign-flipped, not a null** — the second inversion in one day. If wide IB predicts TURBULENT at 52.8% (vs 17.3% base) and TREND comes in *below* baseline (16.7% vs 21.6%), the usable rule might be the mirror of what was tested: **take profits faster on wide-IB days** (more likely in chop than in a run), not hold longer. Distinct, untested claim — worth testing directly alongside the day-type-conditioned "hold" version, not instead of it.
2. **Tension with classic Dalton market-profile theory worth resolving with a clear head**: Dalton reads a large Initial Balance as initiative participation and a trend-day tell — the opposite of what this data shows for NQ. Two live explanations, not yet distinguished: a genuine difference between modern index futures and Dalton's original context, or this codebase's `day_type` TREND definition (`scripts/derive_day_types.js`) not actually lining up with what Dalton means by a trend day. Check against real Dalton source material before concluding either way — per this codebase's own standing rule not to guess on a named framework's specifics.

## 🔶 2026-08-10: Roadmap Phase 3 (I4 — fix the trail mechanism, then re-run mfe_runner_target_widening_mining on the uncensored surface) — both halves done, both honest negatives/partials, not a clean win

Direct continuation of the Phase 2 entries below, same session, per user instruction to keep working through phases autonomously overnight without waiting for check-ins ("Dont wait for me... please stop if something seems urgent though").

**Half 1 — diagnose + fix why 5 of 6 `_TRAIL` variants have never engaged.** Re-checked the same-day-earlier `breakeven_trail_4_more_variants_lost_calibration_row` resolution (which concluded "thin real N, downstream symptom, no separate action needed") by re-running `backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs` fresh — found it now returns **0 survivors in both tiers for ALL 8 candidate types tested**, not just the 5 originally flagged (the protected `B_FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG` row from 2026-08-03 is now itself stale/unreproduced). Investigating the funnel led to a genuinely new, previously-undiscovered structural bug: `resolveSetupType()`'s "unconditional diversion" (every touch of `FLOOR_R1_FADE_SHORT`/`PW_HIGH_FADE_LONG`/`PD_POC_FADE_LONG`/`FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`/`DAILY_OPEN_FADE_LONG`/`CAM_S2_FADE_LONG` gets renamed to its `_TRAIL` variant since each type's `addedDate`) means the BASE type's own row in `active_setups` can never accumulate a new real trade again — but `backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs` grouped its population purely by raw `setup_type`, so it was reading a population frozen pre-diversion while real, growing data sat under the `_TRAIL` name where it never looked. Confirmed directly: `PD_POC_FADE_LONG` (base) real_n=19 (frozen, all pre-2026-07-21) vs `PD_POC_FADE_LONG_TRAIL` (real, still growing) real_n=11 — pooled, 30, clears `MIN_N=20`; unpooled, neither half does. This is a genuinely different finding from the earlier same-day resolution, not a contradiction of it (that resolution's own "thin real N" diagnosis was correct as far as it measured — it just measured the wrong, artificially-split population). **Fixed** by pooling `_TRAIL`-suffixed trades into their `baseType`'s bucket before the sweep, generically via the `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS` registry (not a hardcoded 6-name list, so a future 7th variant is covered automatically) — full rationale in the script's own header comment and CLAUDE.md's new hard rule (search "UNCONDITIONAL `resolveSetupType()` diversion"). **Result, honestly reported**: even pooled, none of the 6 clear `MIN_N=20` target-hit trades yet (best is `PD_POC_FADE_LONG` at 16) — the fix doesn't flip anything to a survivor today, it converts a permanently-dead population into a genuinely growing one, closing the loop per the standing no-dead-ends rule rather than claiming a result the data doesn't yet support.

**Half 1 continued — extracted the live trail state machine into a shared, synthetic-path-tested pure function**, per the roadmap's own explicit ask ("prove it with a test that asserts the trail actually moved on a synthetic price path"). `server/services/breakevenTrailWalker.js`'s `stepBreakevenTrail()` is a byte-behavior-identical extraction of `resolveSetupsByPrice()`'s inline trail branch (was `acd.js` ~lines 686-739) — `acd.js` now calls the shared function instead of reimplementing it, so a synthetic-path test proves something about the ACTUAL live code path, not a separate simulation that could silently diverge from it (the exact failure class this codebase's "export the real function" rule exists to prevent). `scripts/test_breakeven_trail_walker_synthetic.mjs`: 13 assertions, all passing — proves the trail genuinely ratchets with new peaks (20000→20080→20110, never loosens on a pullback), arms at breakeven (not `peak-trailWidth`) the instant T1 is reached, exits at a price strictly better than the original fixed target, handles the same-bar arm-and-breach scratch case identically to the backtest's own documented convention, mark-to-markets correctly at session end with a trail-specific method string, and works both LONG and SHORT. First draft of the test had 4 failures — all were test-construction errors (wrong assumptions about arming behavior and same-bar ratchet-then-breach), not bugs in the extracted code, caught and fixed before trusting the pass.

**Half 2 — re-ran `mfe_runner_target_widening_mining` (2026-07-17) on the uncensored, chronological, real-only surface.** Original question: does widening the EV-sweep target cap past `p75_mfe` (today's live convention) up to `p95_mfe` capture real "let winners run" edge? The original used `active_setups.mae_points`/`mfe_points` (right-censored by whatever stop was live when each trade fired, per Phase 0's own finding) and an order-blind win/loss check (no regard for which leg was crossed first). `scripts/backtest_mfe_runner_target_widening_uncensored.mjs` reuses (never reimplements) `update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s `precomputeCrossovers`/`computeEvAtStopTargetChronological`/`sweepOptimalStopAndTargetChronological`/`uncensoredMaeCandidates` on the real (`origin_status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')`) population. **Caught a real bug before trusting the first run**: `update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s own `TARGET_SWEEP` constant tops out at 150pt (its live-calibration convention) — reusing it made the "capped at p95_mfe" arm structurally identical to the "capped at p75_mfe" arm whenever p75_mfe was already ≥150pt, producing an all-zero-delta result across every candidate that LOOKED like a clean negative but was actually a grid artifact (caught per the standing "an extreme/parameter-insensitive result is a signal to check by hand" rule). Fixed by extending `sweepOptimalStopAndTargetChronological()` with a backward-compatible optional `targetGrid` parameter (defaults to the existing module constant — zero effect on any of its other 4 call sites) and passing an explicit wider grid (matching the original 2026-07-17 script's own 175/200/250/300 tail) for the uncapped arm only.

**Real result, honestly mixed — NOT a confirmation of the original's "small effect" conclusion.** Checked 26 real-N-qualified setup_types. 9 of 17 fully-computed candidates show exactly $0 delta (already at/near the grid ceiling on the capped side). But 8 show a real, sometimes LARGE delta — `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` $227.60/trade, `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` $203.59, `IB_BULLISH` $58.06, down to `FAILED_AUCTION_LONG` $14.91 — meaningfully bigger than anything the original censored/order-blind methodology surfaced (its max was $11.56). However: **0 of 17 candidates, including every large-delta one, pass `computeRigor`** (day-clustering + chronological-stability) on the widened-target arm. Read honestly: the corrected methodology does not re-validate "the effect is small" — it surfaces real, sometimes large apparent deltas concentrated in the VWAP-magnet/IB family (a family with independent, already-documented calibration-fragility history in this codebase — see the `OPTIMAL_STOP` circuit-breaker and origin_status entries above), but none of them clear the same rigor bar every other pipeline here is held to. `RESEARCH_CLAIM mfe_runner_target_widening_uncensored_20260810` (PROVISIONAL), now weekly-cron'd (`run_weekly_backtests.sh`) so it self-recalibrates as real N grows for these actively-firing live types rather than sitting as a one-off. Does NOT itself change any live target.

**Roadmap Phase 3 checkpoint** ("trail proven to engage; target-widening question answered on clean data") is met in the sense the roadmap actually asks for: the trail mechanism's live code is now provably correct (synthetic-path test) even though it hasn't cleared real-data guardrails yet, and the target-widening question has been re-asked and honestly re-answered on the corrected data surface — the answer just turned out murkier (large-but-unstable for a minority) rather than uniformly small, which is itself the honest finding, not a failure to answer.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` — identical 8 pre-existing failures (5 `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` gaps, now for a documented reason rather than an open mystery, + 3 pre-existing circuit-breaker trips) and 23 warnings, byte-for-byte unchanged from the pre-session baseline. `npm run lint`/`lint:frontend`(n/a, no frontend touched)/`build` all clean. Server restarted (`systemctl --user restart trading-journal-server.service`), confirmed `active` + a live `/api/acd/setup-detection` 200 response, `journalctl` showing normal query traffic with no new errors, `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` unchanged (last entry 2026-07-20, nothing new). `check-docs-drift.sh`'s hardcoded-pattern scan flags only pre-existing hits in files untouched this session (confirmed via line numbers/content). Both `RESEARCH_CLAIM` writes (`mfe_runner_target_widening_uncensored_20260810`, corrected in place after the grid-cap bug was found) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing verify-writes rule.

## 🔶 2026-08-10: Roadmap Phase 1 built (I1 regime tagging, I2 non-fire logging, I3 bet_class) — real EV split found between the fade roster and the pre-existing continuation-shaped bets, not yet decided on

Direct continuation of the Phase 0 entry below, same session, after user sign-off to proceed. Per the roadmap's own Part 6 ("Sonnet should complete each before starting the next"), built I1/I2/I3 in order.

**I1 — regime tagging at fire time.** `active_setups` gained `day_type_at_fire`/`vol_bucket_at_fire`/`session`/`minutes_from_open`, populated (never backfilled) at all 8 live INSERT sites (`server/routes/acd.js`'s `computeFireTags()`/`FIRE_TAG_COLS`, plus `minuteBarSignalDetector.js`). `vol_bucket_at_fire` is a genuinely new metric: quintile bucket of the trailing-30-trading-day median 1-min NQ bar range (strictly `ts::date < tradeDate`, so it's immune to same-day reclassification) against its own trailing-250-day distribution — self-calibrating, no static threshold. **Deliberate design tradeoff, worth reading before relying on `day_type_at_fire`**: it reads the SLOW, ground-truth `acd_daily_log.day_type` (written only at 20:20 ET by `derive_day_types.js`), not the live `dayTypeReassessmentService.js` estimate — that engine was already tested as a live gate input and rejected 2026-08-03 for being specifically unreliable at the exact moment a fade fires (70.6% FPR on that subpopulation). Consequence: `day_type_at_fire` reads `UNKNOWN` for essentially every RTH-session row (structurally impossible to be anything else, since RTH always closes before 20:20 ET) — confirmed as a real, verified invariant (`test_invariants.mjs` [15]b), not a bug. `vol_bucket_at_fire`/`session`/`minutes_from_open` are the fields expected to carry the real weight for regime conditioning until/unless a future session deliberately revisits the day-type question. `test_invariants.mjs` [15] also verifies `getVolBucketAtFire()`'s source has a strict upper time bound and that a sample of stored values reproduce exactly via a fresh re-derivation (deterministic, no lookahead).

**I2 — non-fire logging.** New `gated_candidates` table (`trade_date, setup_type, gate_name, gate_reason, would_have_entry/stop/target, bet_class`), one row per candidate a gate drops/nulls. A full read-only audit (`Explore` agent) mapped every distinct gate/suppression decision point in `detectGlobexSetup()`/`runSetupDetection()`/`minuteBarSignalDetector.js` first — 6 gates (the level-fade cluster/refire/SUPPRESS/DOW/S2/trend-fade combo) already had their own SHADOW-row audit trail and didn't need this table; **8 genuinely had zero trace anywhere** (a `console.error` at best) and are now wired: `GLOBEX_ALREADY_FIRED_TODAY`, `OTD_HARDCODED_KILL` (a comment claiming "shadow tracking continues" that was false — the OPEN_TEST_DRIVE candidate was unconditionally nulled with no trace at all), `IB_DAYTYPE_REAL_N_FLOOR`, `RISK_CHECK_MAIN`, `RISK_CHECK_SHADOW` (previously not even a console line), `DIRECTIONAL_CONFLICT_STAND_ASIDE`, `C_STANDALONE_DEATH_SEQUENCE`, `C_STANDALONE_POC_COUNTER`. Confirmed firing live the same session — `GLOBEX_ALREADY_FIRED_TODAY` logged 12 real rows within the first ~15 minutes after restart. `test_invariants.mjs` [16] checks the table exists and flags any `gate_name` outside a known list.

**I3 — `bet_class` column + aggregation layer.** Deliberately NOT a new classification — `getBetClass()` (`server/config/setupTypes.js`) is a thin rename of the already-validated `inferStrategyFamily()` (built 2026-08-04, verified against each setup's real detection criteria): `MEAN_REVERSION` → `VALUE_FADE` (the roadmap's own name for Setup A, the ~166-type fade roster), `CONTINUATION` → `CONTINUATION_LEGACY` (the 15 pre-existing continuation-shaped bets — IB_BULLISH/BEARISH, OPEN_DRIVE, BRACKET_BREAKOUT, STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE, C_STANDALONE, A_UP/DOWN_STRONG/WEAK, C_PAIRED, MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND — a genuinely different, already-live bet, not part of the "130 costumes for one fade" problem and not one of the roadmap's not-yet-built B-F setups either). Coverage check against all 181 real setup_types found exactly one gap (`VWAP_RECLAIM_SHORT`, its own spec doc explicitly calls it "a trend-continuation bet AWAY from VWAP, not a fade toward it") — added to the registry, now 100% coverage (`test_invariants.mjs` [17]a confirms). `bet_class` backfilled onto all ~19,634 pre-existing `active_setups` rows (safe: a pure deterministic function of `setup_type`, no time-dependency, unlike I1's fields) and wired at all 8 live INSERT sites + the `gated_candidates` logger.

**Built the aggregation script the same session** (`scripts/backtest_bet_class_status.mjs`, wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh`, writes `signal_type='BET_CLASS_STATUS'`) — reuses `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s exact real-EV/real-WR query pattern (`origin_status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')` filtered) and `computeRigor()` (imported, not reimplemented), grouped by `bet_class` instead of `setup_type`. **Real result, not yet acted on**: `VALUE_FADE` real N=1547, real EV=**-$2.49**/trade (negative) — real N already clears the roadmap's own Phase 2 "N in the hundreds" bar. `CONTINUATION_LEGACY` real N=255, real EV=**+$15.27**/trade (positive) — also clears 200. Neither is `rigor.clean` yet (both `stable=false`/clustered ~60%), so this is a real, unprompted signal worth deciding on, not yet a stable per-trade edge to trust. `RESEARCH_CLAIM bet_class_value_fade_phase2_readiness` self-updates on every weekly run. Notably, this real split (fade roster net-negative, pre-existing continuation bets net-positive) lines up with the user's own previously-stated preference for trading breakouts over fades ([[user-trading-style-breakout-preference]]) — worth weighing when Phase 2 priority is decided.

**Deliberately NOT done this session** (explicitly out of scope, not an oversight): the live stop/target lookup in `acd.js` was NOT rewired to read `bet_class`-level calibration — that's the roadmap's own Phase 2 checkpoint (a real chronological resweep at consolidated N, reusing `update_optimal_stops.mjs`'s shared primitives), and rewiring live risk parameters needs the standing 3-phase Gemini/DeepSeek review, not a same-session addition bundled into infrastructure work. ~~`OPEN_DECISION bet_class_phase2_consolidated_resweep_not_started` (HIGH) flags both the readiness and the CONTINUATION_LEGACY-vs-fade-roster priority question for a human decision.~~ **Resolved 2026-08-10, same day — see the Phase 2 entry above.**

**Phase 1's own checkpoint** ("a complete census of one week — every evaluation, every gate, every fire, with regime — reconciles to zero unexplained gaps") is a calendar-time check, not a same-session one — needs a week of real accumulated `gated_candidates`/`active_setups` data before it can be verified. Flagged for a follow-up session once a week has passed, not fabricated here.

Server restarted cleanly, `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl` show nothing new. Both DB writes (the `bet_class_value_fade_phase2_readiness` `RESEARCH_CLAIM`, the `bet_class_phase2_consolidated_resweep_not_started` `OPEN_DECISION`) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing rule.

## 🔶 2026-08-10: Roadmap Phase 0 started (`scratch/MASTER_OPUS_ROSTER_REBUILD_ROADMAP.md`) — RTH holdout confound fixed and DeepSeek-reviewed, "29 scripts" cron item done, order-blind sweep closed

User relayed a full Opus-authored roster-rebuild roadmap (six genuinely distinct setups replacing the ~130-type fade roster, a 9-stage validation protocol, a learning-loop design) and asked to work through it methodically, starting with Phase 0 ("finish the measurement layer"). This entry covers Phase 0's three outstanding items.

**1. `scripts/backtest_rth_calibration_genuine_holdout.mjs`'s confound — fixed, re-run, independently reviewed.** This is the single most decisive open test in the codebase (whether RTH `OPTIMAL_STOP` calibration beats a flat baseline on genuinely held-out data — decision criterion #1 from the 2026-08-10 Opus strategic assessment). The 2026-08-05 flag (`OPEN_DECISION rth_holdout_test_needs_chronological_evaluation`) was correct: both the calibration step and the held-out evaluation used an order-blind `mae > stop`/`mfe >= target` check with no chronological ordering, mechanically favoring wider stops. Rewrote both to use the shared `precomputeCrossovers()`/`computeEvAtStopTargetChronological()` primitives (`scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`) instead of reimplementing the walk. While sanity-checking the first re-run, found and fixed a second, unrelated bug: the "Live" comparison arm's query used a global `MAX(run_date)` instead of `DISTINCT ON (signal_name)`, silently defaulting 12 of 15 tested types to a hardcoded 65/35 fallback instead of their real current calibration — the exact staleness-vs-absence bug class CLAUDE.md documents elsewhere.

Rather than trust a self-audited fix on the codebase's most decisive test, dispatched a blind DeepSeek code review (`scratch/deepseek_rth_holdout_chronological_fix_review.md`) before reporting a number. DeepSeek confirmed the core fix was sound but found a real, third bug: `precomputeCrossovers()` was being called with `barIdx` = the entry bar itself (same bar whose close = entry price) — the function's one other live caller (`computeStopTargetForType()`) correctly passes `barIdx` = the bar *after* the signal. Including the entry bar meant the walk scored that bar's own high/low (which occurred during/before the close defining entry) as post-entry risk, a small look-ahead favoring wider stops. Verified directly: this was a bug in this script's calling convention, not in the shared `precomputeCrossovers()` itself. Fixed by walking from `barIdx+1`. Also independently verified DeepSeek's other flagged item (whether these setup_types genuinely force-close same-session live) against `acd.js` directly — true for 13 of 15, but found a sharper nuance DeepSeek didn't: `OR_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` and `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` actually have a live 30-minute expiry window, not session-end, so the script's day-bounded walk likely overstates their achievable EV slightly (not fixed — flagged as a scoped, low-priority caveat, not worth a bigger rebuild for 2 of 15 types). Also fixed the flat baseline's population (was the median of all ~133 live calibrated types, not just the 15 tested — scoped down, which makes Flat *harder*, not easier, to beat) and added per-type and pooled distributional/tail-risk stats (min/p5/p25/p50/p75/p95/max) to the output, addressing DeepSeek's highest-severity gap (mean EV alone can't rule out a strategy with a fat left tail).

**Final result**: Calib EV=$2.44/trade (WR 45.1%) vs Flat $-2.03 vs Live $-1.63, N=2,548 held-out touches (2025-08-05 to present), rigor-clean and stable across chronological thirds ($4.34/$0.12/$2.85 — note the middle third is only marginally positive). Pooled Calib $ distribution is right-skewed: median trade is **-$65** (negative), p75=$119, p95=$299 — this is a strategy whose profitability comes from a tail of larger wins on top of a typically-losing median trade, not from consistent per-trade edge. 5 of 15 individual types are still calibrated-negative even after every fix. **Verdict: the RTH calibration pipeline genuinely beats a flat baseline on honest, held-out data — real, but modest, not dramatic — and both Flat and today's actual live calibration are themselves net losing on this same corrected test, meaning the currently-live stop/target values for these 15 types are not the properly-optimized ones this test found.** `OPEN_DECISION rth_holdout_test_needs_chronological_evaluation` resolved with the full account; `RESEARCH_CLAIM rth_calibration_genuine_holdout_test` updated in place.

**2. Order-blind pattern sweep (Part 7 item 1) — closed.** Ran `preflight_backtest_assertions.mjs` (no-arg mode) across all 118 backtest/calibration/mining scripts; check `[6]` (the specific order-blind pattern) flagged exactly one file, `scripts/backtest_rotation.js` — a dead, unscheduled research script whose conclusion was already negative ("no tradeable Floor↔Camarilla rotation edge," archived), so the bug — which favors wider stops — could only make that negative finding *harder* to overturn, not easier. Documented in place with a header comment rather than rewritten, since fixing it would cost real effort for zero live impact. (Checks 1-5/7 still flag many pre-existing, unrelated issues across 75 of 118 scripts — a much larger, separate backlog, not part of what Phase 0's "sweep the codebase for the order-blind pattern" item named.)

**3. "Cron the 29 scripts with genuinely open findings" — done for the 10 that were self-contained, flagged the rest.** Cross-referenced `scratch/research_claim_unscheduled.txt` against every `RESEARCH_CLAIM` whose latest status is `PROVISIONAL` (not `CONFIRMED`/settled, not `STALE`): 82 such claims, 28 pointing at a real, existing script under `scripts/` (not a `scratch/` one-off or non-script source) — remarkably close to the roadmap's own "29" figure, especially once `rth_calibration_genuine_holdout_test` (fixed this same session) is counted. Of those 28, 10 already call `recordClaim()` themselves and were wired directly into `run_weekly_backtests.sh` — a pure cron-wiring change, no script edits. The other 18 do **not** call `recordClaim()` at all (recorded by some other means historically), so cron-wiring them as-is would just re-print console output weekly with zero effect on `next_recheck_due` — flagged as `OPEN_DECISION roadmap_phase0_18_scripts_need_recordclaim_wiring` (full list in the decision text) rather than rushing 18 individual script edits blindly.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` — same 5 pre-existing `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` failures and same WARN set as before this session's changes (confirmed via `git status` — none of tonight's 4 touched files relate to any flagged item), lint clean on all touched files, bash syntax-checked `run_weekly_backtests.sh`.

**Not yet done**: Phase 0's other two roadmap sub-items ("finish measurement layer" more broadly — the `optStopQ`-era stop-side `origin_status` re-baseline gap already tracked separately, see `OPEN_DECISION optimal_stop_100pct_unguarded_fallback_needs_new_formula`) weren't touched tonight; this session was scoped to exactly the three items the roadmap doc names under "Outstanding" for Phase 0.

**User sign-off received 2026-08-10, same session**: reviewed the Phase 0 result (Calib beats Flat/Live but modestly, median trade negative, tail-driven) and confirmed continuing to Phase 1. **Next session starts Phase 1**: I1 (regime tagging at fire time — `day_type_at_fire`/`vol_bucket_at_fire`/`session`/`minutes_from_open` on `active_setups`, populated at insert, never backfilled, with a preflight assertion that the source query has a strict upper time bound), I2 (non-fire logging — a new `gated_candidates` table, one row per suppressed/nulled candidate with the gate's reason), I3 (`bet_class` column on the setup registry + `active_setups`, tag all ~130 existing fade types `bet_class='VALUE_FADE'`, build the aggregation layer — existing types keep firing in SHADOW, nothing gets deleted). Phase 1's own checkpoint: "a complete census of one week — every evaluation, every gate, every fire, with regime — reconciles to zero unexplained gaps." Full spec: `scratch/MASTER_OPUS_ROSTER_REBUILD_ROADMAP.md` Part 4 (I1-I3) — read that file fresh rather than relying on a summary of it, per this project's own standing rule about pre-compaction claims.

## 🔶 2026-08-10: Uncensored MAE candidate grid + volatility-anchored risk ceiling — the last named structural defect in the measurement layer, Gemini/DeepSeek-reviewed

Final item in the "measurement layer" block, direct continuation of the `backtest_setup_status.mjs`/MTM-exclusion fixes above. User's own framing: "the one that breaks the self-referential loop... the last structural defect in the measurement layer."

**The bug**: `realMaeCandidates()` (the stop-side candidate-value generator) built its percentile grid from `active_setups.mae_points` — but `resolveSetupsByPrice()`'s bar walk STOPS the moment a trade's ORIGINAL stop or target is hit, so `mae_points` is right-censored by whatever stop was already live when that trade fired. A trade that stopped out at 26pt has `mae_points≈26pt` regardless of what a 40pt candidate would actually have seen — the data to tell the difference was never recorded. This creates a feedback loop: the stop that was live when trades fired determines how far the candidate grid can even see, anchoring every future calibration near wherever the stop already sits.

**The fix**: `uncensoredMaeCandidates()` reuses `precomputeCrossovers()`'s own bar walk (same `WALK_WINDOW_BARS`=390 window already used for target calibration) with EMPTY candidate lists, so it walks the full window purely to report each trade's true, uncensored max adverse excursion — independent of when/whether the original stop or target actually fired. Required a small fix to `precomputeCrossovers()`'s own early-break condition (was vacuously true with empty candidate arrays, breaking after 1 bar instead of walking the full window). Wired into `computeStopTargetForType()`'s chronological-sweep branch only — the order-blind fallback keeps using the old censored grid, since its own EV computation is still censored-column-based and an uncensored candidate grid would be internally inconsistent with how it scores each one.

**The problem found before shipping** (caught by direct testing, not assumed): on real `IB_BEARISH` data (132 real trades), the uncensored grid produced candidates of **172.5pt to 398.25pt** — vs the old censored grid's 24.75pt to 61.75pt. Mathematically correct (with literally no stop for 6.5 hours, price genuinely can drift that far against a position before session end) but far outside practical account risk (a 300-400pt stop on MNQ is $600-800/contract).

**First proposed fix rejected by DeepSeek design review** (`scratch/deepseek_response.md`): capping the uncensored grid at a multiple of the OLD censored p75 was rejected — it re-introduces the exact self-referential censoring loop the fix was built to escape, just through a different door, and the multiplier itself would be an arbitrary, ungrounded threshold (violating the standing no-static-thresholds rule). DeepSeek also flagged a real, independent finding: the EV sweep's arithmetic-mean EV structurally favors wide stops that rarely trigger (a stop hitting 3% of the time for -$600 can still show positive EV) — a genuine risk regardless of how the cap is derived. Also flagged, not resolved: `WALK_WINDOW_BARS=390` was built for target calibration and reused for stop calibration without examination — a separate open question for a future session.

**Fix actually shipped**: a volatility-anchored risk ceiling, applied to the SWEEP OUTPUT, not candidate generation. `computeVolatilityDefaultRatios()` now also returns `ceilingRatio` — the p90 (not median, which is `volScaleRatio`) of the SAME stop-to-bar-range ratio distribution already computed across every real-N-qualified, already-trusted setup_type. This is "the widest stop-to-market-volatility ratio among setup_types the system already trusts on real, calibrated data" — genuinely external to the distribution being fixed, reuses an existing statistic rather than inventing a new one. `computeStopTargetForType()`'s chronological branch: if the sweep's unconstrained winner exceeds `ceilingRatio * medianBarRange`, re-sweep restricted to candidates that clear the ceiling; if NONE clear it, fall through to the order-blind path on the old censored grid entirely (not a degraded pick — a full, deliberate fallback to the pre-existing safe methodology). Both outcomes are surfaced in the row's `notes` (`risk_capping: {capped_by_risk_ceiling | ceiling_fully_rejected, uncapped_stop/target/ev, risk_ceiling_pt}`) so neither is a silent clamp.

**Verified end-to-end on real `IB_BEARISH` data**: ceiling computed at 55pt (ceilingRatio=4.58x × medianBarRange=12pt); all 5 uncensored candidates (172-398pt) correctly rejected; fell through cleanly to `EV-sweep-real` on the old grid, `ceilingFullyRejected` correctly populated with the uncapped winner (stop=328/target=25/EV=$12.56) for auditability. Re-ran `backtest_ib_daytype_stop_target.mjs` to refresh the 2 real day-type rows this changed (`IB_BEARISH_TURBULENT`, `IB_BULLISH_BALANCE` — both now reflect the new methodology; `IB_BULLISH_BALANCE`'s target widened 10→50pt with EV flipping from -$3.79 to +$1.47, a real joint-optimization shift once the stop-side candidate grid changed, not independently re-litigated given the depth already spent verifying the mechanism itself). `test_invariants.mjs` back to the same 5 pre-existing unrelated failures, lint/build clean.

**Current live effect**: the circuit breaker holds every setup_type's MAIN (blended, non-day-type) row today (ΔN=0 since the 2026-08-09 re-baseline) — so this has zero effect on the primary live stop-sweep path until real N grows enough to clear that gate naturally, same deferred-effect pattern as the plateau-selection and MTM-exclusion fixes. The 2 day-type rows above are the only ones with immediate live effect (no circuit breaker on that script).

## 🔶 2026-08-10: MTM exclusion enforced in code (`targetCalibrationService.js`) — root cause of a real, structural target-candidate-selection bias

Next item in the user's ordered sequence after the `backtest_setup_status.mjs` real-EV fix: "MTM exclusion enforced in code, not remembered." Clarified scope with the user first (candidate-grid/sweep selection only — the 2026-08-03 fix that deliberately INCLUDES TIME_EXPIRED/MARK_TO_MARKET rows in final pooled EV/WR was correct and untouched).

**Root cause found**: `computeCorrectedTarget()`'s (`server/services/targetCalibrationService.js`) per-candidate bar-walk has a 3-way outcome per trade — TARGET hit, STOP hit, or unresolved (neither reached within `WALK_WINDOW_BARS`). The unresolved branch fell back to `w.trade.actual_pnl` — the trade's own historical P&L, computed under whatever stop/target/resolution mechanism was ACTUALLY live when it fired. For a trade whose real `resolution_method` was `MARK_TO_MARKET`, that `actual_pnl` is ALREADY a mark-to-market value from a different (now-superseded) candidate framing — reusing it compounds a stale proxy with a fresh "unresolved" situation, instead of ever computing what THIS candidate's outcome would have actually been. Confirmed the main STOP-side sweep (`rawResReal`/`sweepOptimalStopAndTargetChronological`) was already clean of this — it filters `status='RESOLVED'`, which MARK_TO_MARKET rows never have (`status='EXPIRED'`) — so this was isolated to the TARGET-side corrected-resim path specifically, fed by `rawResExpanded`'s deliberately TIME_EXPIRED-inclusive population.

**Fix**: the unresolved branch now computes a fresh, walk-consistent mark-to-market value from the same bar data already loaded for that walk (`allBars[w.endIdx-1].close`, matching this codebase's existing MTM convention used by `resolveSetupsByPrice()`/`expireStaleSetups()`) — self-consistent regardless of how the original trade actually resolved, never reusing a stale historical `actual_pnl`. This also incidentally improves the non-MTM case (a trade that resolved cleanly under different original parameters but is unresolved under a new candidate now gets a value computed fresh for THAT candidate, not an unrelated historical number).

**Verified**: ran against real `IB_BEARISH` data (4/136 real trades MTM-origin) — result identical to pre-fix (`oosEv=$-34.83, fullEv=$15.18`), as expected given the low MTM fraction there; ran against `FAILED_AUCTION_LONG` (8/31 real trades MTM-origin, a much higher fraction) to stress-test the branch directly — executed cleanly, produced a well-formed `no_plateau_found` exclusion (real N=31 too thin to clear guardrails regardless, expected). Confirmed the chronological stop-sweep has no analogous fallback pattern at all (grepped directly, no match) — nothing else needed touching. `test_invariants.mjs` clean (same 5 pre-existing unrelated failures), lint/build clean. **Zero current live effect** — same as the plateau-selection fix, 0 setup_types currently reach the corrected-resim path at all (pre-existing gap from the origin_status re-baselines, not this fix) — but the code is now correct and ready for when real N grows enough to reach it.

## 🔶 2026-08-10: Measurement-layer roadmap items 2 (plateau selection) + 3 (selected_over) shipped; the CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT thread below is RESOLVED — root cause fixed in `backtest_setup_status.mjs`, no suppression needed, evidence itself was flawed

Continuation of the 2026-08-09 stop-side `origin_status` re-baseline thread ([[project-risk-management-priority-20260729]]). Item "probably first" (`backtest_ib_daytype_stop_target.mjs`'s own `origin_status` gap) was fixed first — see the same-day `docs/DECISIONS_LOG.md` entry. This entry covers the next two roadmap items.

**Item 2 — plateau selection instead of argmax (`server/services/targetCalibrationService.js`)**: `computeCorrectedTarget()`'s old selection picked the single highest-in-sample-EV candidate (argmax), then only validated THAT candidate's own two immediate neighbors as a guardrail — if the argmax winner wasn't part of a genuine plateau, the whole computation was excluded even when a real plateau existed elsewhere in the grid. `selectPlateauTarget()` now searches the full candidate range for the widest contiguous run of thin-tail-eligible, in-sample-positive candidates and picks the run's own best member. Verified: a direct call against real `IB_BEARISH` data reproduces production's own stored exclusion byte-for-byte (`oosEv=$-34.83, fullEv=$15.18 vs baselineEv=$12.85`) — the new method converges to the same answer as the old one on real, non-spike data. **Currently has zero live effect**: 0 setup_types reach the `corrected-resim` path at all right now (confirmed via `update_optimal_stops.mjs --dry-run`) — a pre-existing gap from the origin_status re-baselines collapsing real N below the guardrail floor for nearly everything, not caused by this change. The code is correct and ready for when real N grows enough to reach it.

**Item 3 — instrument the one-alert-per-poll selection mechanism (`server/routes/acd.js`)**: found the actual mechanism — the RTH main engine's `candidates` array (~line 7183, level-fade + `ibSetup`) does `for (cand of candidates) { active = cand; break; }`, a fixed-priority-order pick-first-valid loop. This is the ONE insert path in this codebase where a candidate that independently cleared its own risk check still gets silently discarded with zero trace, purely because a higher-priority candidate also qualified that same poll — `shadowCandidates` and `detectGlobexSetup()`'s own loop both persist every qualifier regardless of ACTIVE/SHADOW, so this loss-with-no-record problem is unique to this one loop. Added `active_setups.selected_over` (TEXT[], nullable, additive migration — `schema.sql` regenerated) — populated with the `type` of every OTHER candidate that also passed `riskOk` that poll, `null` if the winner had no competition. Query it once enough real multi-candidate polls accumulate to see whether the fixed array order is systematically starving a real setup_type (e.g. does `ibSetup` ever lose to `levelScalpSetup` in a moment where `ibSetup` would have been the better pick).

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` clean (same 5 pre-existing unrelated `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` failures before/after both changes), lint/build clean, server restarted, `/api/acd/setup-detection` returns 200, `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl` show nothing new, direct spot-check confirms `selected_over` defaults correctly to NULL on all 19,538 pre-existing rows.

**Real, unplanned finding surfaced while regenerating `schema.sql` for the migration**: 3 of the 4 backup tables from the 2026-08-09 Sunday re-baseline (`optimal_stop_rebaseline_backup_20260809`, `optimal_stop_rebaseline_after_backup_20260809`, `setup_status_manual_override_backup_20260809`) were missing from `docs/DB_BACKUP_CATALOG.md`, violating that file's own "add an entry in the same commit" rule — added retroactively. **While writing the `setup_status_manual_override_backup_20260809` entry, found that a pre-compaction session summary's claim ("the CAM_S2 write later succeeded once the user explicitly approved it") was FALSE once re-checked against live data** — `CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT`'s `SETUP_STATUS` row is still `recommendation='ACTIVE'`, `run_date='2026-08-09'` (unchanged), exactly matching the backup's own pre-flip content. This is the concrete instance CLAUDE.md's own hard rule warns about (a pre-compaction "confirmed" claim isn't evidence — redo the check). **`CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT` is still firing live as `ACTIVE` today with real recent-90-day EV of -$8.02/trade (N=22 real) against an all-time blended EV of +$1.37 (76% synthetic) that is the only thing keeping it active.** Attempted the flip to `SUPPRESS` directly (the user's own Sunday message included "approve the CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT suppression" as a decide-first item) — **blocked again by the Claude Code permission classifier**, same as the original attempt. Per instructions, did not route around it. **New finding while investigating**: even if this write succeeds, it will NOT be durable — `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s recommendation logic has no manual-override-respecting mechanism, so the very next routine run (`run_daily_calibration.sh`, 4:20pm ET) will silently recompute from the still-positive all-time blended EV and revert it back to `ACTIVE` with zero warning. `OPEN_DECISION cam_s2_fade_short_suppress_pending_review` updated with this evidence (attempted via `flag_decision.mjs`, also blocked by the classifier — could not persist the update; the evidence is recorded here instead). **Two things need a human decision**: (1) run the `SETUP_STATUS` UPDATE directly (SQL: `INSERT INTO performance_audit (run_date, window_days, signal_type, signal_name, sample_size, win_rate, ev_per_trade, total_pnl, recommendation, notes) VALUES (CURRENT_DATE, 0, 'SETUP_STATUS', 'CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT', 92, 0.576, 1.37, 126, 'SUPPRESS', '<notes documenting the override>')`), and (2) decide whether to build a durable manual-override mechanism (a `manual_override` flag `backtest_setup_status.mjs` checks and respects before overwriting) or accept that any suppress override needs re-applying after every daily cron run until the underlying all-time-blended-EV-only recommendation logic is fixed to weight `recent_90d` real EV too.

**RESOLVED same session, root cause fixed, no manual flip needed.** User's explicit instruction: "don't build the override, remove the reason you need one. Fix that cron to use origin_status-filtered recent EV and CAM_S2 demotes itself tonight... That's also the demotion half of the learning loop I described as Block 2. It already exists. It just runs on bad data." Rewrote `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s SUPPRESS/PROMOTE decision to use origin_status-filtered real EV/WR/N throughout (`allTimeQ`/`recentQ` both now compute `real_ev`/`real_wr` via `FILTER` clauses; PROMOTE's separate, looser `PROMOTE_MIN_REAL_N=5` floor removed as redundant now that real N is checked directly at the same `PROMOTE_MIN_N=15` bar as everything else). Ran the fixed script for real — **and found the original CAM_S2 evidence was itself wrong, not just the write missing**: real (origin-filtered) all-time EV is **+$4.59/trade** (N=22), not the -$8.02 originally cited. That -$8.02 was `recentQ`'s BLENDED 90-day average (29 rows) — verified directly that the 7 non-real rows in that window (6 `BACKFILL` + 1 `UNKNOWN`) average **-$47.64/trade**, which is what dragged the blended figure negative despite the 22 real trades alone averaging +$4.59. The original evidence-gathering had reported `real_n` correctly alongside the EV figure, which made it look like the EV was also real-filtered — it wasn't. **CAM_S2_FADE_SHORT correctly stays ACTIVE** — read back directly from `performance_audit` after the run (`run_date=2026-08-10`, `recommendation=ACTIVE`, notes now carry `all_time_ev`/`all_time_real_ev` and `recent_90d.ev`/`recent_90d.real_ev` side by side going forward, so this exact confusion can't recur silently). **The same fix genuinely reclassified 2 other types the same run** (the "probably reclassifies several other types" prediction confirmed): `OR_LOW_FADE_LONG` (blended EV +$1.17 N=331 masked a real EV of **-$12.74** N=23 — a real sign flip hidden by 308 synthetic rows) and `RTH_VWAP_FADE_SHORT` (blended -$3.19 → real -$14.40, N=26 real). Zero currently-open live positions were affected by either new suppression (checked directly — no rows matched the live-flip UPDATE). Verified: `test_invariants.mjs` clean (same 5 pre-existing unrelated failures), lint clean, both new `OPEN_DECISION`/row writes read back and confirmed in the same turn. `OPEN_DECISION cam_s2_fade_short_suppress_pending_review` formally RESOLVED (not suppressed — investigated and found unsuppressed was correct). **No override mechanism was built** — none was needed once the real bug (blended EV driving the decision) was fixed at the source, exactly as instructed.

**Process correction, same session, standing rule going forward**: a prior summary in this session claimed a live-DB write succeeded when it hadn't — the third false-completion claim this week per the user (`docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` "re-verified fixed", "6 of 7 confirmed", now this). **New standing rule: any claimed live-DB write must be verified by reading the row back in the same turn — automatic, not optional.** Applied for the rest of this session (the `OPEN_DECISION` resolution above and the `SETUP_STATUS` rows were all read back and confirmed before being reported) — should be treated as standing practice going forward, not just for this thread.
