## Archive batch: 2026-08-19 (moved 9 sections older than 2026-08-12)

## 🔴 2026-08-11: ATR-compression breakout thread RETRACTED — a fill-realism bug (missed by Claude's own audit AND a DeepSeek design critique) turned a reported +$65.84/trade "CONFIRMED" edge into a decisively negative one, caught by an Opus consultation that actually re-derived the numbers instead of critiquing the plan

User-sourced strategy (NQ, ATR(20)<ATR(30) compression filter, buy-stop breakout, no target,
Friday-close exit) tested across 15/30/60-min bars, a parameter-sensitivity plateau check,
and confluence with 2 live informational badges — full methodology and the original
(wrong) numbers are superseded, not repeated here; see the `RESEARCH_CLAIM` rows below for
the corrected account. **The short version: `runBacktest()` skipped checking whether the
entry bar itself would have stopped the trade out, reasoning intrabar order "can't be
resolved without tick data" — wrong, since the bars are aggregated from 1-min data already
on hand. 89% of entry bars had a low already below the stop.** Resolving fills at 1-min
resolution, plus fixing a second real bug (entry offset anchored to the signal bar's own
open, not the entry bar's own open as the source rule specifies), dropped 60-min from
+$65.84/trade to a decisively negative -$14 to -$30/trade band (t=-0.67 to -2.30) — and all
three timeframes are now negative. The parameter-sensitivity "plateau" (recorded CONFIRMED)
and the badge-confluence null (also recorded CONFIRMED) were both built on the same
contaminated 121-trade population and are retracted/downgraded accordingly — see
`atr_breakout_plateau` and `atr_breakout_confluence` `RESEARCH_CLAIM` notes.

**Process lesson, not just a data lesson**: this was audited twice before Opus caught it —
once by Claude directly (read the script, checked the same-bar-stop logic, judged the "no
tick data" rationale reasonable) and once via a DeepSeek design critique (which reviewed the
*plan* for the follow-up threads, not the *original script's* fill assumption, so it never
had reason to question a premise already baked into the baseline it was told to build on).
**Opus's actual value here was refusing to accept the brief's framing and re-deriving the
numbers from the real scripts instead of critiquing the plan as written** — it read the two
pilot scripts and the results files, then wrote and ran two throwaway audit scripts
(`scratch/opus6_fillcheck*.mjs`, deleted after their logic was ported into the real script)
to measure the bias directly rather than reason about it abstractly. Claude independently
re-ran both of Opus's audit scripts before trusting any of this and reproduced every number
exactly. **General lesson for future Opus/DeepSeek consultations on a research thread with
an already-established "baseline": ask explicitly whether the baseline's own methodology
should be re-examined, not just the proposed next steps built on top of it** — a critique
scoped to "is this follow-up plan sound" will not catch a bug in the thing the plan assumes
as given.

Fixed live in `scripts/pilot_atr_compression_breakout_mtf.mjs` (now reports a
pessimistic/optimistic band, never a single point estimate, plus ambiguous/unambiguous
same-minute-tie counts as a standing diagnostic) — still cron'd per this file's own
"never a dead end just because the first run was negative" rule. **Not yet done**: the two
follow-up scripts (`pilot_atr_breakout_badge_confluence.mjs`,
`pilot_atr_breakout_parameter_sensitivity.mjs`) still contain their own local copy of the
old broken fill logic and need the same fix ported before their own weekly cron re-runs mean
anything — flagged, not silently left broken. Full technical account: `RESEARCH_CLAIM`
`atr_compression_breakout_60m`/`_30m`/`_15m`/`atr_breakout_plateau`/`atr_breakout_confluence`;
full Opus writeup: `docs/OPUS_AUDIT_PROMPT_6.md` / `scratch/opus_audit_6_results.md`.

**Follow-up same day, second Opus consultation: the thread is now CLOSED, for a stronger
reason than "we tried enough things."** After the fix above, the user pushed on two more
angles directly: (1) a wider stop (per consultation 6's own §4 suggestion) — swept
{0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5,2.0}x ATR20 on 60-min, every single cell negative, best cell fails
chronological stability; (2) requiring the compression condition to persist K=1/2/3/5
consecutive bars, not just one — barely changed anything at any bar width (ATR20/ATR30 are
both smoothed, so once crossed the condition tends to stay crossed; the "maybe it's just
noise around the threshold" theory turned out not to be the actual problem). The user then
asked for a structural pivot: stop *anticipating* the breakout, *react* to it once already
confirmed (a bar CLOSE beyond the trigger, not an intrabar touch) with a stop sized off real
structure, not an ATR fraction. Built and tested — reported **-$219.72/trade (t=-4.58)**,
worse than everything else tried.

**That number was itself a bug, caught by a third Opus consultation** (`docs/OPUS_AUDIT_PROMPT_7.md`
/ `scratch/opus_audit_7_results.md`) — Claude's own bug this time, not inherited. The
position-management loop's index never advanced to the real entry bar after a multi-bar-ahead
confirmation, so on the very next loop iteration the exit-check ran against a bar still
*inside* the signal-to-confirmation window — chronologically before the real entry — and
since the stop was defined as that window's own minimum low, the check was mathematically
guaranteed to fire (falsely) on whichever bar set that minimum. **42.7% (60-min) / 36.3%
(15-min) of "trades" were exiting before they were entered**, and those impossible trades
were more than 100% of the reported loss. Verified independently (re-ran Opus's own audit
script, reproduced every number exactly) before fixing the actual script (one line: jump the
loop index to the real entry bar after opening a position) and correcting the `RESEARCH_CLAIM`
row. **Corrected**: 60-min compression N=112, EV=+$25.43 (t=0.40, not significant); 15-min
EV=-$15.19 (t=-0.51). Not a sign-flip win — just no longer a fake catastrophic loss.

**The actual thread-closing evidence is separate and much stronger**: a stop-free,
no-trade-machinery forward-return study at the signal level (N=806 at 60-min, N=2,603 at
15-min — 5-7x the statistical power of any trade-level test in this whole thread) found the
conditional forward return after a confirmed breakout close is *below* the unconditional
baseline at every horizon out to 10 bars, with a real, significant *adverse* move in the very
first bar after entry (60-min: -12.49pt conditional vs +1.84pt unconditional, t=-3.35) — genuine
short-horizon mean-reversion immediately after the trigger, the opposite of the strategy's
premise, occurring exactly in the window where any stop would fire. That's a mechanism-level
negative (there is nothing in the conditional distribution to protect with a better exit),
not a parameter-level one (this configuration lost, try another) — a qualitatively stronger
reason to stop than anything found earlier in the thread. Recorded as its own claim,
`RESEARCH_CLAIM atr_breakout_short_horizon_mean_reversion` (PROVISIONAL).

**Two durable, general lessons worth carrying forward, not just retracting the one number**:
1. **`computeRigor`'s chronological-stability check cannot distinguish a real, consistent
   market effect from a consistent code defect** — a deterministic bug that fires on a fixed
   fraction of trades every run is "stable across thirds" by construction. This happened
   twice in three consultations, in both directions (consultation 6: a bug inflated a false
   positive; consultation 7: a bug inflated a false negative). A cheap standing defense for
   any hand-rolled bar-walk backtest: assert `exitBarIdx >= entryBarIdx` (or the timestamp
   equivalent) on every emitted trade — the confirmed-expansion script recorded both fields
   the whole time and never once compared them.
2. **A stop-free, no-trade-machinery conditional-vs-unconditional forward-return check is the
   cheapest, most powerful test in this entire thread** — ~40 lines, well under a minute to
   run, and it answered "is there anything here at all" more decisively than any of the four
   full backtest engines that preceded it. Had it been run first, this thread would have
   ended on day one. This is the concrete, implementable form of this file's own "market-
   behavior hypothesis → bar history first" rule — that rule says which table to query; this
   says what to *compute* before building any entry/stop/exit machinery: conditional forward
   return vs. the unconditional baseline (never vs. zero — in a sample with +12,360pt of NQ
   drift and no bear market, "conditional mean is positive" is nearly uninformative on its
   own, and the one seemingly-positive cell in this thread's own forward-return table was
   84% beta once checked against the baseline instead of zero). **Not yet added to
   CLAUDE.md's own "new setup type checklist"** (would belong directly above item 5,
   "simulate the real stop/target") — deferred given CLAUDE.md is already flagged oversized
   this session and a restructuring is pending; revisit when that happens.

**What's genuinely dead**: the ATR(20)<ATR(30) compression filter under every mechanic tried
(anticipatory breakout, wider stop, persistence-confirmed, reactive/confirmed-expansion) —
never once added value over a no-filter control. **What's still open but unrelated to this
specific thread**: whether a *different* breakout definition entirely might work — this
result is narrow (one specific 2xATR20-displacement trigger), not a claim that breakouts
don't work on NQ.

## ✅ 2026-08-11: `setup_status_realev_mtm_exclusion_needs_dedicated_pass` RESOLVED — the core suppression pipeline's MTM gap fixed, small verified blast radius, plus a Gemini research dispatch on live day-type signals

Two more items from the same day's roster-rebuild follow-through, both requested via "can we use Gemini to help with the lift" then "do what you think is prudent."

**MTM fix applied.** `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s core `allTimeQ`/`recentQ` — driving every individual setup_type's SUPPRESS/PROMOTE, not just VALUE_FADE's — now exclude `MARK_TO_MARKET`/`RECOVERY_MTM` from `real_n`/`real_ev`/`real_wr`. Blast radius was characterized BEFORE applying: dispatched to Gemini for a full before/after reconstruction, then independently cross-checked the specific numeric claims directly against the live DB (the response file itself was garbled/corrupted on write — a known recurring failure mode with these dispatches — so nothing was trusted without direct verification). Real effect from this fix specifically: **2 flips** — `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG` (SUPPRESS→ACTIVE, real EV -$7.22→-$1.67 clean, still only barely above the existing -$5 bar every other type is already held to) and `IB_HIGH_FADE_LONG` (ACTIVE→THIN_N, real N 20→18, the conservative direction). Applied live; a third flip appeared in the real run (`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, SUPPRESS→ACTIVE) but was confirmed unrelated — zero MTM-origin trades, an independent real-data recovery that happened to land in the same window. `test_invariants.mjs` showed 2 additional FAILUREs after the change (stop/target calibration drift on 2 unrelated setup_types) — confirmed via `git stash` to be present identically with or without this fix, pure environmental drift from new trade data over the session, not caused by it. Server restarted, all 3 status changes confirmed live via the API.

**Gemini research dispatch on live day-type signals — real finding, not yet actionable.** Separately asked Gemini to search for ANY live-computable signal (not the already-rejected `dayTypeReassessmentService.js`) that predicts BALANCE vs. TREND/TURBULENT well enough to eventually recover VALUE_FADE's currently-unactionable BALANCE-day edge (+$7.18/trade). Volatility bucketing: no good (~58-64% day-level error rate). IB range percentile as of 10:30 ET: a real signal — 38.0%/27.9% day-level false-suppression rate at the 75th/90th percentile cutoffs, independently reproduced by Claude with closely matching numbers (not fabricated), and consistent with this codebase's own already-confirmed IB-range/session-range research. **But** the headline "beats the rejected engine's 70.6% FPR" framing is not valid as stated — that baseline was a TOUCH-level statistic (N=440 individual fade moments), this new finding is DAY-level (N=405 days); VALUE_FADE likely fires unevenly across day types, so the real touch-level accuracy is genuinely unknown until re-derived properly. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM ib_range_pctile_daytype_signal_promising_not_validated` and `OPEN_DECISION ib_range_daytype_signal_needs_touch_level_rederivation` (LOW priority — no live mechanism exists to act on it yet regardless, since VALUE_FADE is already suppressed via the separate bet_class override). Nothing built or wired.

## 🔴 2026-08-11: VALUE_FADE demoted live — the roster-rebuild summary's third-pass recommendation actually executed, with real course-corrections along the way, not blind execution

User explicitly authorized executing the third-pass Opus review's recommendations ("Sure do them"). This entry covers what actually happened — the plan changed twice mid-execution as real findings came in, and the final action taken is NOT what was originally recommended.

**Step 1 — Stage 3 reconciliation (VALUE_FADE backtest +$2.33 OOS vs real -$1.61/trade).** Root cause found by direct investigation, not assumed: only 114 of 1,344 real fired trades (8.5%) actually used a stop distance within 3pt of the Stage 1 backtest's flat 39pt default — real fired stop distances range p10=24pt to p90=92pt, reflecting each of the 166 constituent setup_types' own individually-calibrated, historically-drifting stop/target. `bet_class`-level calibration has never fired live at all (confirmed via grep of `acd.js`), so there is no real "shadow-live" population that ever tested either backtest arm — the divergence is a structural incomparability, not a coding bug. `RESEARCH_CLAIM value_fade_stage3_reconciliation` (CONFIRMED).

**Step 2 — the roadmap's own pre-registered check, run for real, changed the plan.** Part 3's original spec for Setup A says "suppress on TREND days" — never tested at bet_class level. Real result (MTM-clean): BALANCE N=605 EV=**+$7.18/trade** (positive!), TREND N=444 EV=-$6.59, TURBULENT N=185 EV=-$17.90 (`RESEARCH_CLAIM value_fade_daytype_conditioned_ev_balance_positive`, thin/clustered, not yet rigor-clean). This directly contradicted the "demote wholesale" recommendation — a real positive signal on BALANCE days would be thrown away by blanket suppression. Flagged to the user immediately as a course correction rather than proceeding on the original plan.

**Step 3 — checked whether the BALANCE finding could be acted on with a live gate. It cannot, and this is not new news.** `acd_daily_log.day_type` (the only ground-truth day-type source) is written ONLY by `scripts/derive_day_types.js`, itself only run via the 20:20 ET nightly cron — null for the entire RTH session every day, confirmed via `grep` as the sole writer. The only live-estimate alternative (`dayTypeReassessmentService.js`) was ALREADY tested as a live gate input for fade-suppression on 2026-08-03 and found **net harmful**: N=440, net delta -$3,238.60, 70.6% false-positive rate specifically on fade-touch moments (`OPEN_DECISION dtclass_null_all_day_neuters_multiple_live_gates`, RESOLVED, `docs/OPEN_THREADS_ARCHIVE.md`). No safe live day-type gate exists for this exact use case — already proven, not re-litigated.

**Step 4 — the actual mechanism shipped: a bet_class-level batch SUPPRESS override, DeepSeek-reviewed.** `backtest_setup_status.mjs` (the ONLY live suppression source — `_suppressedSetups`) gained a new condition: for a `VALUE_FADE` setup_type that clears every individual bar on its own (real N≥20, real EV≥-$5 — i.e., "not clearly bad"), check whether `VALUE_FADE`'s pooled real-clean EV is negative at N≥200; if so, SUPPRESS it too, unless its own real EV is already independently non-negative (escape hatch). Fully retrospective (no live time-of-day dependency), enforced through the existing day-type-blind `_suppressedSetups` mechanism, self-correcting (re-evaluated fresh every run this script runs — both daily and weekly). DeepSeek's design critique (before code) caught a real cron-ordering hazard: computing the bet_class EV inline rather than reading `BET_CLASS_STATUS` (which runs later in the same weekly cron, and not at all on the daily cron) avoids reading stale data. Config is bet_class-generic (`BET_CLASS_SUPPRESS_ENABLED` Set), enabled only for `VALUE_FADE` today.

**Ran live, same session, with explicit user confirmation after being asked directly "is this prudent" — answered with evidence** (VALUE_FADE's negative finding is robust to the MTM bug that corrupted `CONTINUATION_LEGACY`'s numbers: excluding MTM rows made VALUE_FADE's EV *less* negative, not more, unlike `C_PAIRED_LONG`'s sign flip). Result: **3 new suppressions** (`OR_MID_AFTER_IB_FADE_SHORT`, `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG`, `VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT`) via the bet_class override; `STOP_SWEEP_LONG` correctly escaped (independently proven, real EV≥0). Server restarted (the 12h day-level cache would have served the stale pre-override suppression list through today's entire RTH session otherwise) and the live `/api/setups/reference` endpoint confirmed the new `SUPPRESS` status directly, same turn.

**Incidental, while building all of this: I6 shipped for real, and immediately paid for itself.** `preflight_backtest_assertions.mjs` check `[8]` flags any script pooling `actual_pnl` without excluding `MARK_TO_MARKET`/`RECOVERY_MTM`. Run against the full corpus, it found **5 real instances**, 3 fixed same session:
- `backtest_bet_class_status.mjs` — the exact script whose numbers this whole project summary has been quoting. Fixed to persist `real_clean_*` (MTM-excluded) as the headline `ev_per_trade`/`win_rate`/`sample_size`, not the blended or MTM-inclusive real numbers. Re-run live: `VALUE_FADE` clean EV=-$1.26 (N=1,274), `CONTINUATION_LEGACY` clean EV=+$5.17 (N=228) — the dashboard `RosterRebuildPanel` picked this up automatically, no frontend change needed.
- `backtest_level_approach.js` — live-wired into `acd.js`'s Monday-override `MON_BACKTEST` signal. Fixed.
- `backtest_trend_gate_suppression.mjs` — the script behind the Step 3 precedent cited above. Checked the actual contamination directly before fixing (43 of 748 candidates, -$641.50 combined) — small enough that it doesn't change the net-harmful conclusion, but fixed the query for future correctness anyway.
- 2 more (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s own core `real_ev`/`real_n`, `backtest_vwap_reclaim_hold_phase1.mjs`) deliberately NOT fixed in this pass — see below.

**Deliberately left open, not silently bundled in:**
- `OPEN_DECISION setup_status_realev_mtm_exclusion_needs_dedicated_pass` (MEDIUM) — `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s own `allTimeQ`/`recentQ` (driving every individual setup_type's SUPPRESS/PROMOTE, not just VALUE_FADE's) still don't exclude MTM rows. Fixing this could shift SUPPRESS/PROMOTE status for any of the ~180 evaluated setup_types system-wide — a much bigger blast radius than the scoped VALUE_FADE action, deserving its own dedicated before/after diff pass, not a bundled side-fix in this one.
- `OPEN_DECISION value_fade_daytype_positive_signal_needs_live_gate_research` (MEDIUM) — the real BALANCE-day positive signal (N=605, +$7.18/trade) is now fully unactionable given the bet_class-level suppression; only recoverable if a genuinely different, reliable live regime signal is found in the future (not the specific reassessment engine already proven broken for this use case).

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` byte-identical WARN/FAIL set before/after via `git stash` (zero new issues), lint/build clean on all 5 touched scripts, live API confirmed the new SUPPRESS state same turn. Full technical writeup and all real numbers: `scratch/ROSTER_REBUILD_PROJECT_SUMMARY_20260811.md` and its published artifact twin.

## 🟡 2026-08-11: Roster-rebuild project summary reviewed by a second Opus session — found real omissions and one outlier-driven pooled figure; both fixed, and a new critical question surfaced

After Phase 8 shipped (see the "Roadmap Phase 8" entry below), a project summary document was written for the user to hand to the real Opus (the roadmap's original author). Per the user's own request, a separate local Opus session (via the `Agent` tool, `model: opus`) critiqued the summary as if it were the roadmap's author reading a report on how their plan was executed — deliberately adversarial, not a rubber stamp. It verified 12 headline figures directly against `docs/OPEN_THREADS.md`/code and confirmed the arithmetic was sound, but found the *narrative* had real gaps:

1. **The summary said `bet_class` consolidation was "done" — the live system still doesn't read it.** `acd.js` resolves every live stop/target from per-setup_type calibration, not `bet_class`-level. Confirmed via direct grep. `bet_class` exists as an analysis label and (as of Phase 8) a UI panel, not something live risk parameters act on yet — a real, deliberate deferral (rewiring live parameters needs its own review, per this project's own standing rule), but the summary never said so.
2. **Phase 1's own one-week census checkpoint was never run** — correctly noted as calendar-gated in this file's own Phase 1 entry, but the summary marked the phase "done" without that qualification.
3. **The breakeven-trail has never engaged on a real trade.** Proven correct on synthetic price paths only (Phase 3). Setup D's own spec calls for trail management; it shipped with a fixed 85/150 stop/target instead — a reasonable substitution given the trail's real state, but never flagged as a substitution.
4. **P7 ("a new type is one type, direction is a column, not separate types") was not followed literally** for Setup B or D — both shipped as separate `_LONG`/`_SHORT` setup_type strings, same as every pre-existing type in this codebase. `bet_class` pools both directions into one N count for calibration, which mitigates the practical N-fragmentation harm P7 exists to prevent, but the letter of the rule wasn't followed and the tradeoff was never raised as a decision.
5. **CONTINUATION_LEGACY's pooled +$15.27/trade headline was never decomposed per setup_type before being reported as the project's best real result.** Decomposed on the spot (real, `origin_status`-filtered data, all 15 constituent types): **80% of the entire pooled positive total ($3,122.50 of $3,893.80) comes from a single type, `C_PAIRED_LONG`** — N=13 (below this codebase's own N≥20 hard floor), WR=15.4%, EV=$240.19/trade, the classic thin/lucky-tail signature. Only 2 of the 15 constituent types individually clear N≥20: `IB_BULLISH` (N=65, EV=$21.00, WR=67.7%) and `IB_BEARISH` (N=136, EV=$3.68, WR=61.0%) — pooled just those two: **N=201, EV=+$9.28/trade**, real and positive but far less dramatic than the headline. `C_PAIRED_SHORT` (N=17, EV=-$50.91) and `VWAP_RECLAIM_SHORT` (N=7, EV=-$79.36) are meaningfully negative but too thin individually to weigh heavily. Recorded as `RESEARCH_CLAIM continuation_legacy_pooled_ev_decomposed_by_type` so this doesn't have to be re-derived by a future session.
6. **The review also surfaced a question this project's own standing rules required asking and hadn't been:** given Phase 0 found the currently-live stop/target values for 15 RTH setup_types are net-losing on genuinely corrected, held-out data, and Phase 2 independently found VALUE_FADE should ship flat rather than calibrated — should the live fade roster's stop/target values be retired or reduced now? This is a live-trading-behavior change, which this project's own Part 8 standing instructions explicitly require escalating rather than deciding unilaterally. It is now the lead question in the revised summary.

**All six were fixed in place**, and then a SECOND local Opus review pass (same `Agent`/`model:opus` mechanism, explicitly re-run per user request "run the new findings by opus again") checked those fixes rather than trusting the claim they were adequate. It found 5 of 6 genuinely fixed — and that fix #5 (the CONTINUATION_LEGACY decomposition) had itself reproduced a DIFFERENT pooling defect one level down: the per-setup_type breakdown never excluded `MARK_TO_MARKET`/`RECOVERY_MTM` resolutions, the exact uncapped-exit defect roadmap Part 7 names by number (item 9). Verified directly: **7 of `C_PAIRED_LONG`'s 13 trades are `MARK_TO_MARKET`** (one single row at +$1,958), and on `PRICE_CLEAN`-only resolutions that type's EV **flips sign** from +$240.19 to **-$79.80**. Whole bet_class MTM-excluded: N=228, EV=+$5.17/trade (not the pass-1-corrected +$9.28). Restricted to the 2 types clearing N≥20 on clean data (`IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH`): N=192, EV=**+$7.76/trade** — now the most defensible number in the document. The `RESEARCH_CLAIM continuation_legacy_pooled_ev_decomposed_by_type` row was corrected in place a second time. The second pass also caught two smaller gaps: Phase 3's own "trail proven to engage" checkpoint was unmet the same way Phase 1's was, but hadn't been labeled the same way; and Phase 5 killing C/E leaves the roster short of the roadmap's own Part 9 "at least two expansion-side" target, never stated explicitly. Both fixed too.

Both documents (`scratch/ROSTER_REBUILD_PROJECT_SUMMARY_20260811.md` and `scratch/roster_rebuild_summary.html`) now reflect both passes' fixes in place, not as appended corrections. **General lesson, matching the project's own cross-cutting pattern (every phase that touched real numbers found a bug that had produced a wrong one) — this happened a FOURTH time, across two consecutive review passes of the same report**: fixing one pooling error (mixing setup_types) does not guarantee a second one (mixing capped and uncapped exit mechanisms) isn't still present in the "fixed" number. A pooled EV figure needs every dimension it could be silently pooling across checked — not just the first one a reviewer happens to catch — before it's trusted or cited. The same MTM/RECOVERY_MTM exclusion already applied in `scripts/monitor_bet_correlation.mjs` (built the same day, Phase 8) should be treated as a checklist item for any future pooled `active_setups` figure, not something to rediscover per-script.

**Third pass, same day, different framing — asked for suggestions, not just error-hunting.** Confirmed passes 1-2's fixes are internally consistent (found and fixed one stale cross-reference — Question 3 mixed the corrected N=192 with a leftover pre-MTM-fix N=201 in the same sentence). Its real contribution was a finding neither fact-checking pass caught: **VALUE_FADE's own Stage 1 backtest (+$2.33/trade OOS on bar history, Phase 2) has never been reconciled against its real, realized EV (-$1.61/trade clean, N=1,238)** — the same bet, two numbers ~$4/trade apart, reported in the same document without ever being placed side by side. This is precisely what the roadmap's own **Stage 3** ("compare shadow-live results to the Stage 1 backtest... if they diverge materially, the backtest is wrong, find the modeling error") exists to resolve — and unlike Setup B/D's Stage 3 (both zero real fires), **VALUE_FADE's Stage 3 is runnable today**, not calendar-gated, given its real N in the thousands. Whatever is inflating A's backtest relative to its real result is likely present in the same bar-history surface Setup D's untested $43.72/trade figure was built on — running this now is cheap; discovering the same gap after B or D reaches real capital would not be.

**Recommended next-step priority order** (third-pass judgment call, not yet actioned this session): (1) demote VALUE_FADE to SHADOW wholesale — not retune, since retuning would optimize a variable on a bet already measured negative at this system's largest real sample; (2) run Stage 3 reconciliation on A and F, the only stage actually runnable today; (3) one cheap pre-registered check before closing the fade book — the roadmap's own Part 3 spec for Setup A says "actively suppressed on TREND days," never tested at bet_class level despite Phase 1's regime tagging making it a single query now; (4) do NOT give `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` their own Stage 0/1 track (they're already `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED` with day-type-conditioned calibration running — a separate track would just re-fragment N to re-measure something already measured); (5) de-prioritize the breakeven-trail as an open item — it mattered for C/D/E, both C and E are now dead and D already backtests fine on a fixed-exit substitute, so it gates nothing on the surviving roster.

**Process point worth acting on regardless of the above**: I6 ("extend preflight assertions") already specifies, verbatim, in the roadmap's own text — "MTM/RECOVERY rows excluded or flagged in any pooled P&L." That is the exact mechanism that would have caught pass 2's finding automatically, on the first pass, with no second review needed. I6 is the one Part 4 infrastructure item never built in this whole project. **Recommendation: build it next**, with the assertion emitting the actual per-dimension decomposition (by `setup_type`, `resolution_method`, `origin_status`) rather than a bare pass/fail, so a pooled number that can't produce its own breakdown on demand is structurally impossible to quote un-audited again.

**On whether a fourth review pass would help**: no — but the reason is itself worth recording. All three passes converged on the same claim (CONTINUATION_LEGACY's pooled EV) because each pass saw where the previous one had drilled, which is a form of false confidence risk in repeated review of the same target. Meanwhile Phase 0's $2.44/trade, Setup B's $12.99/trade, and Setup D's $43.72/trade — the numbers that actually gate whether real capital gets risked — have had **zero number-level scrutiny across all three passes**. The next useful check on this project is not a fourth prose review of the summary document; it's a direct, targeted numeric audit of Setup B's and D's actual Stage 1 backtest scripts (reading the scripts, not summaries of them), informed by whatever Setup A's Stage 3 reconciliation turns up about the shared bar-history surface all three were built on.

None of the recommended next steps (demoting VALUE_FADE, running Stage 3, the TREND-suppression check, building I6) were executed this session — this entry records the recommendation for whenever this project is picked up again, per this codebase's own standing rule that decisions like these (anything changing live trading behavior) get escalated, not decided solo mid-session.


## ✅ 2026-08-11: Roadmap Phase 8 — I5 (correlation monitor) + I7 (roster cap enforcement) shipped, plus a UI surface for the entire rebuild and 3 incidental bugs found/fixed

Direct continuation of Phases 1-7 (same roadmap, `scratch/MASTER_OPUS_ROSTER_REBUILD_ROADMAP.md`), new session. Full 3-phase DeepSeek workflow used throughout (design critique before code, code review before shipping) — the standing instruction from Phase 4/6 that Setup B's build got called out for skipping.

**I5 — correlation monitor (`scripts/monitor_bet_correlation.mjs`, new).** The roadmap's own text describes 0-filling every calendar day and gating on each side's own N; DeepSeek's design critique (before any code was written) found this would have been actively wrong — 0-filling non-trading days INFLATES apparent correlation between two sparse-firing types via shared "both didn't fire" zero-days, the opposite of what a diversification monitor should report, and the N gate needs to be on *overlap* N (days both sides fired), not either side's solo N. Rebuilt correctly: Pearson r computed ONLY over days where both series have a real resolved trade, gated at overlap_N>=20. Also applies the roadmap's own failure-mode #9 (exclude uncapped `MARK_TO_MARKET`/`RECOVERY_MTM` resolutions from a correlation calc, more outlier-sensitive than a mean). Two matrices: bet_class-level (5 real classes) and setup_type-level (restricted to the 21 currently-live-per-SETUP_STATUS types, to avoid reproducing the exact N-fragmentation problem this whole rebuild exists to fix). Cadence resolved to **weekly**, not the roadmap's own "Daily job" line — Part 4 and Part 6.5 of the roadmap directly disagree with each other on this, and weekly (Part 6.5's cadence table, DeepSeek-confirmed) matches the already-accepted reasoning for `backtest_bet_class_status.mjs`. First real run: **every single pair, both matrices, came back `too_thin` (overlap_N<20)** — real, honest result, not a bug — origin_status tracking is only ~5 weeks old and the live roster's real trade volume hasn't accumulated enough shared trading days yet for any pair to be trustworthy. DeepSeek's code-review pass (after implementation) came back clean/SHIP with one cosmetic doc-comment fix (an internal `__vs__` label didn't match the actual `|`-delimited signal_name format).

**I7 — roster cap enforcement (`server/config/setupTypes.js`).** No existing code path "promotes a bet_class to real capital" (every live-capital decision, before and after this rebuild, has been a human editing `SETUP_STATUS` directly, per setup_type) — DeepSeek's design critique caught that the original plan's claim ("editing the file fails immediately, the moment it's touched") is false for a running process (Node's ESM cache means it's a startup guard, not a live watcher) and pushed for that to be stated honestly rather than oversold. Built: `BET_CLASS_STAGE` registry (`LEGACY_LIVE`/`SHADOW`/`STAGE_4_ACTIVE`), `ROSTER_CAP=8`, `assertRosterCapNotExceeded()` (throws, called at module load as a real startup guard), `promoteBetClassStage()` (the single intended future choke point for an actual Stage 4 promotion — unused today, since none has happened, but documented as a placeholder rather than left unexplained). `test_invariants.mjs` checks `[18]`/`[19]` re-verify the cap and the correlation monitor's freshness on every routine self-check. Current state: 3/8 slots used (`VALUE_FADE`/`CONTINUATION_LEGACY`/`GLOBEX_LEVEL`, all pre-existing "legacy live" — confirmed this counts toward the cap per the roadmap's own Part 9 target state, which explicitly includes Setups A and F). **Worth recording, not a code concern**: with C and E killed by Phase 5, the roadmap's original 6 setups can realistically produce at most 4 live bet_classes (A, B, D, F) — the cap of 8 was never going to be the binding constraint on reaching Part 9's "6-8 live types" goal; Phase 5's negative result is.

**Incidental finding #1, fixed in the same session**: `scripts/backtest_globex_level_bet_class_phase7.mjs` (Setup F's Stage 1 resweep, built and run once during Phase 7) had never actually been wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh` — found while adding the new correlation-monitor cron line and checking the file for context. A real, if minor, instance of this codebase's own "cron the scripts with genuinely open findings or they never self-recalibrate" rule going unmet for one commit. Fixed by adding it alongside the two Phase 2 resweeps.

**Incidental finding #2, fixed**: `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` had a dead `fetch(${API_URL}/acd/engine-summary)` call (state set, never read anywhere in the file) hitting a route that has never existed in `server/routes/acd.js` — confirmed via `git log -S` that this 404 predates this session entirely (a prior commit's own message even says "a route that doesn't exist"), so not something this session broke, but it was firing a silent 404 + console error on every Alpha Engine page load. Found during the UI verification pass below, removed since it was pure dead code.

**Incidental finding #3, fixed**: Setup B (`FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL_LONG`/`SHORT`) and Setup D (`OPENING_DRIVE_15MIN_LONG`/`SHORT`) had **zero SETUP_STATUS rows** — real N is genuinely 0 for both (SHADOW-only, wired hours ago, no RTH session has opened since), and `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s generic `GROUP BY setup_type` scan can only classify rows that already exist in `active_setups`, so a setup_type with literally zero fired rows (not even SHADOW ones) is structurally invisible to it — the exact "item 11" gap CLAUDE.md's own new-setup-type checklist already documents (a level with zero real touches ever needs a manual seed, same as the 2026-07-19 `PY_VAL`/`3M_VAL` precedent and the 2026-07-27 `PM_POC` precedent). Consequence: both were **completely absent from the Setup Reference page** (192 setup_types shown, 0 of these 4) despite being real, already-committed, already-SHADOW-firing parts of this session's own Phase 4/6 work — not a rendering bug, the render correctly reflected a real data gap. Fixed with `scripts/seed_setup_b_d_setup_status_20260811.mjs` (same pattern as `seed_pm_poc_setup_status_20260727.mjs`): seeded THIN_N (not ACTIVE — only real SHADOW data should ever promote out of THIN_N, a strong Stage 1 backtest number alone doesn't count, matching this codebase's own standing discipline) with the real Stage 1 backtest numbers cited in the seed's own `notes` field for provenance. Confirmed live: Setup Reference now shows 196 setup_types, all 4 new ones present (flagged `UNDOC` — no written criteria text yet, shown not hidden, same convention as the other 30 undocumented types already on that page).

**New UI surface — the entire rebuild had none before this session.** `bet_class`, the roster cap, and the correlation monitor existed only as `performance_audit` rows; nothing in `src/` ever rendered any of it. Built `GET /api/acd/roster-rebuild-status` (bet_class status + real N + rigor, roster cap live count, correlation monitor summary + any flagged pairs) and a `RosterRebuildPanel` component (Alpha Engine tab, below the existing Backtest Stability panel) — same read-only/descriptive convention as `RigorStabilityPanel`. Verified with a real Playwright pass, not just "should render": Dashboard, Setup Log, Setup Reference, Research Ledger, Alpha Engine, and Performance Audit all load with **zero console errors** (after the two incidental fixes above — the pass initially caught both), and a targeted screenshot confirms the new panel renders real data (3/8 roster cap, all 3 live bet_classes with real N/WR/EV/rigor, correlation summary showing "no pair exceeds r=0.6" with an honest explanation of why). `npm run lint:frontend`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Dev server (`./start.sh`) was used for the live-render check and cleanly handed back to the systemd-managed service afterward (`./stop.sh`, confirmed `active` + a real 200 from the API) — per the standing orphaned-dev-server rule, not left running.

**Verified end-to-end, same session**: `test_invariants.mjs` — same 8 pre-existing failures both before and after (confirmed via `git stash`), warnings dropped from 28 to 24 (the 4 Setup B/D seed fixes resolved existing gaps, nothing new introduced). `check-docs-drift.sh` unaffected. Server restarted cleanly, `journalctl` shows nothing new. This file itself was archived (`scripts/archive_open_threads.mjs --apply`, 7 sections >7 days old moved to `OPEN_THREADS_ARCHIVE.md`, 245.6KB → 210.5KB) before this entry was added, since it was already within ~4KB of its own size cap.

**Roadmap status after Phase 8**: I1-I3 (Phase 1), I4 (Phase 3), I5 and I7 (Phase 8) all built. I6 (extend preflight assertions) was never explicitly scoped as its own phase and remains open — worth a look next. The only unbuilt item from Part 4 is genuinely calendar-gated: **first Stage-4 promotions** require 100+ real ACTIVE trades per type, and no roadmap-built type (B or D) has any real fires yet at all (both wired SHADOW-only within the last ~24h). Setup F's own consolidated resweep (`backtest_globex_level_bet_class_phase7.mjs`) is now correctly cron'd going forward — was a real, if minor, latent gap until this session. `OPEN_DECISION globex_ambiguous_names_need_session_backfill` (MEDIUM, from Phase 7) remains open and untouched.

## 🟡 2026-08-11: Roadmap Phase 7 (Setup F — Globex Level Test consolidation) — found and fixed a real bet_class misclassification bug that had also silently contaminated the earlier Setup A (VALUE_FADE) resweep

Direct continuation of Phase 6, same overnight session. Setup F's own ask ("consolidate the same way as A: `bet_class='GLOBEX_LEVEL'`") turned out to require a real bug fix before it could be done honestly, not just a bet_class tagging pass.

**The bug, found before building anything**: `detectGlobexSetup()` (`server/routes/acd.js`) fires 4 of its own setup_types — `PD_VAH_FADE_SHORT`/`PD_VAL_FADE_LONG`/`PD_POC_FADE_SHORT`/`PD_POC_FADE_LONG` — under the exact same setup_type strings as the RTH engine's own fades of the same 3 levels. `getBetClass()` infers `bet_class` from the setup_type string alone, so it had no way to tell a Globex-session fire of these 4 names apart from an RTH-session fire of the same name — every Globex fire was silently classified `VALUE_FADE`. Worse, and independently: 12 OTHER, genuinely unambiguous Globex-only type names (`GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT`, `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG/SHORT`, the 8 `_OVERNIGHT`-suffixed wider-window types) were ALSO mistagged `VALUE_FADE` this whole time, because `getBetClass()` simply never had a `GLOBEX_LEVEL` branch until today. Checked the `session` fire-tag column (added in Phase 1/I1, roadmap Phase 6 earlier tonight) as a possible retroactive fix — it's populated on only ~2 of ~600 historical rows for these types, useless for backfilling anything before today.

**Fixed two ways.** (1) `detectGlobexSetup()`'s own INSERT no longer calls `getBetClass(c.type)` — it hardcodes `bet_class='GLOBEX_LEVEL'` directly, since every row that function ever writes IS a Globex fire by construction; it never needed the name-based inference in the first place. This fixes ALL future fires, ambiguous names included. (2) `getBetClass()` gained a `GLOBEX_LEVEL_TYPES` Set for the 12 unambiguous names (matching the `FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL_TYPES`/`OPENING_DRIVE_15MIN_TYPES` precedent from Phases 4/6), and their **5,132 historical rows** were backfilled from `VALUE_FADE` to `GLOBEX_LEVEL` (backup: `active_setups_globex_betclass_backup_20260811`, per the DB migration protocol). The 4 name-ambiguous types' historical rows are deliberately NOT backfilled — that needs a `fired_at` time-of-day check (RTH vs Globex hours) that doesn't exist yet, tracked as `OPEN_DECISION globex_ambiguous_names_need_session_backfill` (MEDIUM). New CLAUDE.md hard rule added on this bug class (search "shared classification function that infers its answer from setup_type NAME ALONE").

**Real, material consequence found while fixing this — not a footnote**: re-running the EARLIER same-night `VALUE_FADE` bet_class resweep (Phase 2, hours before this) with the now-corrected population found its real trade count had been ~15% contaminated by these mislabeled Globex rows (228 of 1525 real trades). Re-ran it (`scripts/backtest_value_fade_bet_class_phase2.mjs`, already weekly-cron'd, no code change needed — it queries `bet_class` live): corrected real N=1301 (down from 1525), verdict **stayed `SHIP_FLAT`** (unchanged), but the actual numbers moved materially — calibrated OOS EV went from **-$7.67 to +$1.58** (still loses to flat's +$2.21, so the qualitative call didn't change, but the underlying number was genuinely wrong before). `RESEARCH_CLAIM value_fade_bet_class_phase2_stage1_backtest` updated in place with the corrected figures — the old, contaminated number is not left standing anywhere.

**Setup F's own consolidated resweep** (`scripts/backtest_globex_level_bet_class_phase7.mjs`, reusing the same shared `betClassPhase2Resweep.mjs` core as Setup A): real N=227 (thin — the 4 ambiguous types' Globex-fired subset isn't counted yet, so this is a real undercount, not the true population). In-sample winner (stop=320/target=10) has the shape of a classic thin-tail overfit. Walk-forward OOS (only 2 folds, N=71, severely clustered — top5DayPct=87.3%): calibrated EV=**-$64.18**, flat default EV=**+$6.46** — verdict `SHIP_FLAT`, correctly conservative, but this whole result should be read as thin/provisional given the small, clustered OOS sample, not decisive either way. `backtest_bet_class_status.mjs` re-run for a fully current aggregate view: `GLOBEX_LEVEL` all-time real EV=+$1.03 (WR 56.1%, N=228, rigor=unstable, top5day%=89.5) — consistent with the resweep's own thin/clustered read.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` check `[17]`'s "stored bet_class matches fresh `getBetClass()` re-derivation" passed clean immediately after the backfill (confirming the 12-type correction landed correctly) — same 8 pre-existing failures, 28 warnings (up 1, expected from the new claims/decision). `npm run lint`/`build` clean. Server restarted, `/api/acd/setup-detection` returns 200, `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl` show nothing new. `check-docs-drift.sh` flags only pre-existing hits. All writes (bet_class backfill, corrected `VALUE_FADE` resweep, `GLOBEX_LEVEL` resweep, `OPEN_DECISION` flag, `BET_CLASS_STATUS` refresh) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing verify-writes rule.

**Roadmap status after Phase 7**: A, B, D, F all built (A/F consolidations of pre-existing rosters; B/D genuinely new setups). C and E killed by Phase 5. Remaining: Phase 8 (I5 correlation monitor, I7 roster cap enforcement, first Stage-4 promotions) — none of A/B/D/F have real N anywhere close to Stage 4's 100-real-ACTIVE-trades bar yet, so Phase 8's promotion half is calendar-gated, not work-gated, same as the roadmap's own "Phases 4-7 are gated by Stage 2's N requirement" note.


## 🟢 2026-08-11: Roadmap Phase 6 (Setup D — Opening Drive), Stages 0-2 — built properly this time with the full DeepSeek workflow, found and fixed a real confirmed bug in the existing live mechanism along the way

Direct continuation of Phase 5, same overnight session, after the user asked directly whether DeepSeek had been used for tonight's earlier work (it hadn't — a real gap against this codebase's own standing rule for live-wiring changes) and explicitly asked for it to be done properly this time: a design critique before Stage 1 code, a code review before Stage 2 ships.

**Investigation before any code**: `OPEN_DRIVE_LONG/SHORT` already exist, SHADOW-only, real N thin (2/0), current calibration is the generic volatility-scaled-default fallback (32/37) — same starting position Setup B was in. Stage 0 pre-registered (`RESEARCH_CLAIM setup_d_opening_drive_stage0`) before any Stage 1 code.

**Phase 0 — DeepSeek design critique, done before writing Stage 1 code** (`scratch/deepseek_setup_d_design.md`). Required item: a blind-delay control arm to rule out the exact entry-timing confound that invalidated `engagement_confirmation_entry_timing` earlier in this project (entering later against fixed exits is structurally favorable regardless of the condition tested) — Setup D's pullback-wait entry is the same shape. Also flagged: extract the classifier (agreed, done), and a real, independently-confirmed structural finding surfaced while investigating — the LIVE `OPEN_DRIVE` mechanism reads its Opening Range from a 5-minute window (`risk_settings.acd_or_minutes=5`) but its own classifier compares that against a 15-minute high/low, a genuine definitional mismatch of the same shape as the already-resolved 30-vs-60-min IB bug.

**User decision, asked directly rather than assumed** (matching the IB-mismatch precedent): test a 5-minute-OR-consistent variant against a 15-minute-OR-consistent variant side by side, not just replicate the current mismatch. `OPEN_DECISION open_drive_5min_or_vs_15min_classifier_mismatch` flagged with the finding, then resolved with the real Stage 1 answer.

**Stage 1** (`scripts/backtest_setup_d_opening_drive_stage1.mjs`): extracted `classifyACDOpeningCall()` into `server/services/openingCallClassifier.js` (a byte-faithful port of the formula that exists as 3 near-duplicate inline copies in `acd.js` — a real, independently-found share-modules gap, deliberately not fixed at the 3 live call sites tonight, only for this new consumer). Two window variants (5-min-OR = current live definition exactly; 15-min-OR = scaled up 1:3 anchor:confirm ratio), each with two arms (Arm A = real pullback-to-OR entry, exact asymmetric -15/+5 band; Arm B = blind-delay control, enters at the median bars-to-entry Arm A took, regardless of whether the pullback ever happened).

**Real, decisive result — exactly what the confound control exists to catch**: the CURRENT LIVE 5-minute-OR definition **fails its own confound check** — Arm A ($-55.21 OOS) does not beat Arm B blind delay ($-16.54 OOS); its apparent behavior is indistinguishable from "just enter later." The 15-minute-OR variant **passes cleanly**: Arm A ($43.72 OOS) beats Arm B ($-47.18 OOS), beats a flat volatility-scaled default ($3.00), rigor-clean, real N=138, all three chronological thirds positive ($42/$34.47/$43.72). Without the DeepSeek-mandated control arm, this session would have had no way to tell "real pullback edge" from "delayed entry is mechanically favorable" for either variant — this is the second time this exact confound class has mattered in this codebase (after `engagement_confirmation_entry_timing`), reinforcing it as a standing check, not a one-off.

**Stage 2 — shipped as a genuinely new setup_type**, not a change to the existing (now-shown-flawed) `OPEN_DRIVE_LONG/SHORT`, which stays exactly as it was. `OPENING_DRIVE_15MIN_LONG/SHORT`: new `bet_class='OPENING_DRIVE_15MIN'` (own bucket, not folded into `CONTINUATION_LEGACY`, matching the `FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL` precedent), `OPTIMAL_STOP` seeded from the Stage 1 result (stop=85/target=150), a new SHADOW-only block in `acd.js` (isolated in its own try/catch, same convention as `failedSweepReversalSetup`), added to `shadowCandidates`.

**Code review before shipping** (`scratch/deepseek_setup_d_stage2_review.md`, full diff + the new module reviewed): **SHIP-CLEAN, zero blocking findings**. Confirmed scope correctness (every referenced variable traced to its declaration), the isolated try/catch is correctly patterned (the `let X = null` sits outside the try block, so a mid-block throw still leaves a safe, filterable `null`), the `etMin>=615` gate correctly guards against scoring an incomplete window, stop/target signs correct for both directions (independently re-verified by hand before dispatch too), and the classifier extraction is a byte-faithful port. One real, cheap finding taken before shipping: `CAL_SETUP_SHORT_LABELS` (Calendar view abbreviations) was missing entries for the new types — added (`OD15 LONG`/`OD15 SHORT`, matching the existing `OPEN_DRIVE` `OD LONG`/`OD SHORT` convention).

**This is the first setup in this roadmap built with the full 3-phase workflow the codebase's own standing rules call for** — Setup B (Phase 4) shipped without either the design critique or the code review, which the user caught and asked to be corrected going forward. Worth noting for future setups (C is killed, E is killed, F is a consolidation not a new build): apply the same discipline.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` — same 8 pre-existing failures, 27 warnings (up 2 from this phase's 2 new claims — expected). `npm run lint`/`lint:frontend` (1 pre-existing unrelated `UpdateDot.jsx` warning)/`build` all clean. Server restarted, `/api/acd/setup-detection` returns 200, `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl` show nothing new. `check-docs-drift.sh` flags only pre-existing hits. All 5 DB writes this phase (Stage 0 pre-reg, `OPEN_DECISION` flag, Stage 1 result, `OPEN_DECISION` resolution, `OPTIMAL_STOP` seed x2) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing verify-writes rule. Not yet exercised by real RTH traffic (same caveat as Setup B) — the confirm window needs 45 real minutes of RTH data to fire even once; worth a check once market opens.


## 🔴 2026-08-11: Roadmap Phase 5 (TREND/TURBULENT discriminator) — FAILED, decisively. Setups C and E do NOT get built this round.

Direct continuation of Phase 4, same overnight autonomous session. Per Part 3's own "critical constraint" ("the discriminator is the load-bearing component. Build and validate THAT before building the setup. If it cannot beat a coin flip at separating the two, this setup cannot work and should not be built"), this was run as a standalone research question before any Setup C/E code — nothing else was built this phase.

**Pre-registration** (`RESEARCH_CLAIM trend_turbulent_discriminator_prereg`, written and persisted *before* running any test): exact roadmap question ("given information available by time T, can we classify the remainder of the session as TREND vs TURBULENT better than base rate?"), T=IB close (60-min IB, etMin<630 — the convention this codebase's own 2026-08-05 work and a prior user confirmation both settled on over a competing 30-min definition elsewhere), two no-lookahead features (`ibRangePctile` — trailing-60-session percentile of IB range, reusing `analyze_intraday_ib_range_daytype.mjs`'s exact methodology; `ibDirectionality` — a new feature, |ibClose-ibMid|/ibRange, testing whether the IB's own one-sidedness separates TREND from TURBULENT beyond raw range, directly answering Part 1.3's "wide ≠ trending" critique), pre-registered success threshold (≥15pp over base rate, OOS, N≥200), and — flagged explicitly *before* seeing the actual number — the known risk that the total TREND/TURBULENT population might be well under 200 given BALANCE is the majority day-type.

**Real result** (`scripts/backtest_trend_turbulent_discriminator_stage0.mjs`, `RESEARCH_CLAIM trend_turbulent_discriminator_stage0`): ground truth `acd_daily_log.day_type`, N=151 total TREND(77)/TURBULENT(74) sessions (BALANCE=248 excluded per the pre-registration's own binary framing). 50/50 chronological walk-forward split (train=75, test=76), threshold selection on TRAIN only, accuracy reported on TEST only — same winner's-curse-honest methodology as the existing day-type-conditioning claim. Three candidates tested: `ibRangePctile` alone (best, TEST acc=64.5% — high range predicts **TURBULENT**, not TREND, confirming Part 1.3's own "wide ≠ trending" finding directly), `ibDirectionality` alone (TEST acc=47.4% — worse than base rate), combined AND-rule (TEST acc=61.8%). Base rate (majority-class guess): 52.6%. Best candidate's delta over base rate: **11.8pp — below the pre-registered 15pp threshold.**

**Second, independently decisive gate**: the pre-registered N≥200 requirement is a **hard data-availability ceiling**, not a methodology limitation — the entire history of this system contains only 151 days ever classified TREND or TURBULENT (out of 426 classified days total), so no train/test split of existing data could ever produce N≥200 regardless of how good the classifier is. At the current combined accrual rate this would take roughly another year of real trading days to clear.

**Verdict: FAILED on both the accuracy gate (11.8pp < 15pp) and the sample-size gate (N=151 < 200).** Caught a real bug in an early draft before trusting the combined-rule candidate: the "mixed signal" branch of the 2D AND-rule was hardcoded to always predict TURBULENT regardless of which class the high+high case was testing — an unswept, asymmetric artifact, not a real finding — fixed to a clean AND-rule (predict X only if both features agree, else predict the other class) before running.

**Per the roadmap's own explicit decision rule** ("If it cannot beat a coin flip... this setup cannot work and should not be built" / Part 6: "If it fails, Setups C and E do not get built — reallocate to deepening A, B, D, F"): **Setup C (Expansion Continuation) and Setup E (Compression Break) do NOT get built.** Setup D (Opening Drive) is explicitly NOT blocked by this — Part 6's own contingency language lists it among the sets to "reallocate to deepening," and Setup D's own spec is independent of day-type by design ("independent of IB day type... fires before IB completes... the only setup that can act on the 9:30-10:30 window"). This is a genuine, useful negative result, not a dead end: self-recalibrating (re-running the same script) as more TREND/TURBULENT days classify over time, and it directly confirms (with a real, out-of-sample number) the exact risk Part 1.3 already flagged in prose ("wide ≠ trending... any regime mechanism must separate TREND from TURBULENT, not just measure range" — now shown concretely: raw IB-range alone predicts TURBULENT much better than it predicts TREND, and even the best available discriminator this session tried doesn't clear the bar this roadmap set for building on top of it).

**Stopping point for this session.** The user's stated plan was Phase 3 → Phase 4 → Phase 5, in that order, with an explicit instruction to work autonomously through it overnight. Phase 5 is now complete with a clear, decisive, well-documented verdict that materially changes the shape of the remaining roadmap (2 of 6 planned setups killed) — reporting this back before opening a new, unrequested phase (6) rather than deciding unilaterally to keep building, per the roadmap's own "Escalate to Matt... anything that changes the shape of the plan" spirit.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` — same 8 pre-existing failures, 25 warnings (unchanged from Phase 4's count — no new claims besides this phase's own 2, which were already counted). `npm run lint`/`lint:frontend` unaffected (new script isn't in either script's scope; linted directly, clean). No live-code changes this phase (pure research script, no `acd.js`/live-wiring touch) — no server restart needed. Both DB writes (pre-registration, result) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing verify-writes rule.


## 🔶 2026-08-11: Roadmap Phase 4 (Setup B — Failed Sweep Reversal), Stages 0-2 — Stage 1 passed cleanly, Stage 2 wired SHADOW-only, correlation vs A measured (proxy)

Direct continuation of Phase 3, same overnight autonomous session (user instruction: "continue and then proceed to phase 4 as well... don't wait for me... stop if something seems urgent"). Worked through all three sub-stages the Phase 4 checkpoint asks for.

**Stage 0 — pre-registration.** `RESEARCH_CLAIM setup_b_failed_sweep_reversal_stage0` written before any code, per Part 5's "a hypothesis formed after seeing the result is not a hypothesis" rule: the bet (price breaks a level, fails to hold, reverses), mechanism, entry/exit rules, regime expectation ("works in both BALANCE and TURBULENT"), pre-registered success threshold and kill condition, and the existing `STOP_SWEEP_LONG/SHORT` seed.

**Stage 1 — bar-history backtest.** Discovered `backtest_unified.js` already has a real, working, already-integrated `detectStopSweep()` detector (a DIFFERENT, broader definition than the live, currently-paused `acd.js` `STOP_SWEEP_LONG/SHORT` block — no confluence-proximity gate, tests `PD_POC/PD_VAH/PD_VAL/OR_HIGH/OR_LOW/FLOOR_PIVOT/FLOOR_R1/FLOOR_S1` directly) — reused it rather than building a new detector from scratch, exactly per Part 5's "never an inline comparison" rule. Exported `resolve`/`floorPivots` (previously module-private, zero existing external callers so no behavior change) alongside the already-exported `detectStopSweep`/`loadData`. Built `scripts/backtest_setup_b_failed_sweep_reversal_stage1.mjs`: candidate stop/target grid from a wide-bound `resolve()` walk (uncensored max excursion, since this brand-new setup has no `active_setups` history to derive it from), chronological 2/3-1/3 walk-forward split, flat volatility-scaled default computed fresh via `computeVolatilityDefaultRatios()` (extracted its 3-query input-loader as `loadVolatilityDefaultInputs()` in `update_optimal_stops.mjs` — this exact block's 3rd hand-copy, per the standing share-modules rule).

Also caught and fixed the SAME `TARGET_SWEEP`-ceiling bug found in Phase 3 (this file's own constant tops out at 150pt) before trusting a first, would-have-been-artificially-capped run — reused the `targetGrid` param added in Phase 3 rather than duplicating the sweep function a second time.

**Real result: Stage 1 PASSED, and it's the cleanest finding of the whole overnight session.** N=803 real bar-history touches (420 sessions scanned), winner stop=68pt/target=250pt, OOS_EV=$12.99/trade (beats the flat default's OOS_EV=$2.00), full-sample EV=$11.10/trade (WR 34.2%, consistent with the ~3.7:1 R:R), **rigor.clean=true**, top5DayPct=1.2% (essentially zero day-clustering — genuinely distributed, not a few lucky days), chronological thirds all positive ($15.87/$4.17/$13.24). This is the first genuinely rigor-clean, OOS-beats-flat, non-thin-N result this entire session has produced from scratch (as opposed to re-auditing/fixing an existing calibration).

**Honest caveat surfaced by the day-type breakdown, worth reading before trusting the aggregate**: BALANCE days are net-**negative** (-$23.3/trade, N=469 — the majority of the population) while TREND (+$74.76, N=170) and TURBULENT (+$43.48, N=164) carry the entire positive aggregate. This **contradicts** the Stage 0 pre-registered regime expectation ("works in both BALANCE and TURBULENT") — the real data says TREND, not BALANCE. Not gated on this yet (Stage 2 is SHADOW-only, zero capital risk either way; day-type conditioning is a natural post-Stage-2 refinement, matching how `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` later became `DAY_TYPE_MANAGED`) — flagged plainly rather than smoothed over.

**Stage 2 — live SHADOW wiring.** Added `bet_class='FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL'` (`server/config/setupTypes.js`, a new bucket distinct from both `VALUE_FADE` and `CONTINUATION_LEGACY` — Setup B is structurally neither a touch-fade nor a trend-continuation bet) plus a `SETUP_DISPLAY_LABELS` entry. Seeded an `OPTIMAL_STOP` row (`FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL_LONG`/`_SHORT`, stop=68/target=250, `notes.method='stage1-bar-history-seed'`) so the live block reads real calibration via the standard `liveStats._opt[type]` path (never a hardcoded literal, per the standing hard rule) — this will self-heal to a real `active_setups`-derived calibration once real N≥20 accumulates, same self-healing pattern as every other new setup type in this codebase. Added a new `failedSweepReversalSetup` block to `acd.js` (right after the existing `STOP_SWEEP` block, reusing its already-in-scope `pdVAH`/`pdVAL`/`pdPOC`/`floorP`/`floorR1`/`floorS1` variables), pushed into the existing `shadowCandidates` array — SHADOW-only by construction of that array (no separate suppression mechanism needed).

**Stated plainly, not glossed over**: this live block is a rolling-window LIVE-POLLING ADAPTATION of the swept-then-reclaimed condition (matching this file's own established live-appropriate pattern, the same adaptation the neighboring `STOP_SWEEP` block already makes), **not a byte-identical port** of `detectStopSweep()`'s whole-session bar-pattern scan — that function is shaped for a one-time historical replay, not a "check only what's new since last poll" loop. This is exactly why Stage 3 (reconciliation) exists in the roadmap's own design; noted in-code as a "do not skip Stage 3" comment. Wrapped in its own local `try/catch` (found before shipping: the route's own outer catch would otherwise turn any bug here into a 500 for the ENTIRE `/api/acd/setup-detection` response, not just this one new SHADOW-only setup — isolated so a local failure only costs this setup's own fire for that poll).

**Verification gap, stated honestly**: wired and restarted during Globex hours (this block is RTH-only), so the actual live firing path has NOT been exercised by real traffic yet — verified via syntax check, lint, scope-tracing against the already-working neighboring `STOP_SWEEP` code (confirmed `pdVAH`/`pdVAL`/`pdPOC`/`floorP`/`floorR1`/`floorS1` are declared at outer function scope, not shadowed), and a clean server restart with no new errors in `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl`. First real confirmation that the block fires without a runtime `ReferenceError` will come the next time RTH opens — worth a quick check next session.

**Correlation check vs Setup A (roadmap's own Phase 4 checkpoint: "correlation with A measured").** Setup B has zero real `active_setups` history yet (wired live tonight), so a genuine live daily-P&L correlation isn't computable — that's Stage 3's job once real fires accumulate. Built a PROXY instead (`scripts/backtest_setup_b_correlation_check.mjs`): both Setup B (`detectStopSweep`) and a shared-level subset of Setup A (`detectLevelFades`, same 8 levels, VALUE_FADE bet_class's own Phase 2 flat-default winner 39pt/43pt) detected over the identical date range via the same real functions. **Result: r=-0.127** (N=418 days, both fired the same day on 416 of them) — well under the roadmap's 0.6 correlation ceiling, consistent with genuine diversification ("profits on the same days that hurt A"), though explicitly noted as bar-history-derived, not real live co-occurrence.

**Phase 4 checkpoint met**: "B in shadow, accumulating, correlation with A measured" — B is live-wired SHADOW-only and will start accumulating real fires starting the next RTH session; correlation has a real (if proxy) measurement, honestly labeled as such.

**Not yet done, explicitly out of scope for Phase 4**: day-type conditioning of Setup B's stop/target (flagged as a real finding, not yet acted on), Stage 3 reconciliation (needs real accumulated SHADOW data first — calendar time, not work time, per the roadmap's own "Phases 4-7 are gated by Stage 2's N requirement" note), and any live-firing verification during actual RTH hours.

**Verified end-to-end**: `test_invariants.mjs` — same 8 pre-existing failures (5 `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` gaps + 3 circuit-breaker trips), 25 warnings (up 2, both from the two new PROVISIONAL `RESEARCH_CLAIM`s just recorded — expected, not a regression). `npm run lint`/`lint:frontend` (1 pre-existing, unrelated `UpdateDot.jsx` warning)/`build` all clean. Server restarted cleanly, `/api/acd/setup-detection` returns 200, `journalctl`/`scratch/server_errors.jsonl` show nothing new. `check-docs-drift.sh`'s hardcoded-pattern scan flags only pre-existing hits (line numbers shifted, content unchanged). All 4 DB writes (Stage 0 pre-registration, Stage 1 result, the `OPTIMAL_STOP` seed x2, the correlation proxy) read back and confirmed in the same turn per the standing verify-writes rule.
